# Environment flags — the audited catalog

> **A new `MEMRA_*` read needs a row here IN THE SAME COMMIT.** `tools/hooks/pre-push` runs
> `tools/check-flags.sh` on every push (+0.55 s) and refuses one that adds an uncovered name. That
> arm landed 2026-08-23 after main went red three times in one day on this exact rule — the census
> was correct, but its only enforcement was a CI job, so every violation was discovered *after*
> landing and the lanes queued behind it each paid a repair commit. The refusal message names the
> missing rows; the emergency escape and its logging are in CLAUDE.md beside the other hook arms.
>
> **There is no grandfather list to add a name to** — do not go looking for one. A 75-name baseline
> existed until 2026-08-23 and was deleted once measurement showed all 75 were documented here
> anyway, i.e. every exemption was dead while still able to absorb a live one: with it in place,
> *deleting* the row for `MEMRA_SPEC` or `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE` kept the gate green. Its absence is now
> asserted, so restoring it is a deliberate act needing an owner ruling and an expiry check, not a
> file. Document the flag instead.
>
> Note for whoever writes the next such banner: a family row is written as the backticked name plus
> a trailing `*`, and this paragraph deliberately does not spell one out. An illustrative prefix in
> prose is a LIVE prefix row to the census — the draft of this note used the tcol family as its
> example and would have covered every future `MEMRA_TCOL_` flag while all three existing ones are
> catalogued individually below. Same trap the v0.98 review caught on the dspark family.

Doctrine (CLAUDE.md): **winners are defaults** — naked commands run the tuned path. A flag exists only when it is (a) runtime parameter, (b) machine-specific config, (c) documented rollback seam, (d) diagnostic, or (e) explicitly-blocked experimental door. When an experiment concludes negative or flat, the flag and dispatch arm are deleted — the `research/tune-data/*.jsonl` row is the record, not dead code. The 2026-07-08 flag audit enforced this repo-wide; removed-flag ledger at bottom.

Provenance dates refer to `research/tune-data/rig5090.jsonl` / `cloud-rtx6000.jsonl` rows from
that date. Some older rows also cite lane handover notes; those were archived out of this repo on
2026-08-17 as work coordination, and the dated JSONL rows are the surviving primary source.

Rig names below are receipt provenance, not the current gate roster. The 5090 carries the
development-iteration battery and single-card regression boards; pre-merge/pre-tag validation
and final tuning were measured on 2x RTX PRO 6000 pairs (hardware shape, not a deployment
statement — deployment facts live outside this repo). Existing measurements keep their
original rig labels.

**Coverage, stated honestly** (re-audited 2026-08-19 on v0.92.0, 8b61604cc0): the code has
546 distinct literal `MEMRA_*` names at read sites — `env::var`/`env::var_os`/`env!`/
`option_env!` plus the local env helpers in the Rust tree (including `build.rs`), plus
`getenv` in the CUDA/C sources and the `tools/memra_cpu_experts.cpp` companion. After this
audit every one of them is catalogued in §1-§7 or named in the removed ledger (a few families
use their row's shorthand: `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST`,
`MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT`/`_FREQ`/`_PRESENT`/`_LAST_N`). The previous "residue lives at its read
sites" policy is retired because the v0.92.0 release found it had drifted — 124 read-site
names had no entry here. The internal ones are still internal:
per-kernel A/B forcers, dump/trace probes, and gate/bench-bin inputs are marked as such in
§3-§5 and are not operator knobs — setting one in a deployment is a measurement act, not a
configuration. Zero live-catalogued flags were found without read sites (the stale direction
of the drift is clean; past removals are all in the ledger below). Do not read a terse entry
as an invitation — the doctrine at the top still holds: winners are defaults, and a naked run
needs none of the internal seams.

Scope note: the merged dsv4 knobs are catalogued individually in §5 below — the
numeric arms (`MEMRA_DSV4_DECODE_PATH`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_ARM`) and the serve-door
seams (`MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES`, `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH`,
`MEMRA_DSV4_VT`, `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU`/`_FLOOR`,
lane/dsv4-flash-revival-20260822). The dsv4 door itself is **experimental engine
support, not a serving-grade path** (docs/MODELS.md has the posture and the numbers;
tuning is issue #32). The remaining `MEMRA_DSV4_*` names are that door's bench/gate
instrument seams (bench drivers, NVTX/profile hooks, gate dumps, sweep inputs),
covered here as a prefix — none of them is a serving knob.
Unmerged lane flags stay deliberately absent — they arrive with
their lanes and get catalogued at merge. **The dspark knobs left that list in v0.98.0**: the
q38 dspark lanes merged, so every `MEMRA_DSPARK_` name is now catalogued individually below
(and the census gate treats a backticked `NAME*` as a documented prefix, so keeping a merged
family in this note would blind the gate to its future undocumented flags — the v0.98 review
caught exactly that on `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH`/`_DEFER_READBACK`). **The spec-cache knobs
left that list in v0.95.0**, the same promise kept earlier:
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`, `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY`, `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`,
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH`, `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` and `MEMRA_SPEC_BOUNDARY_TRACE`
arrived with their three lanes and are catalogued in §7 below. The automated census reads 507
literal runtime names at v0.95.0 (501 at v0.94.0 + these six).

**Correction, v0.94.0**: treat 546 as a FLOOR, not a total. That audit was measured with the
same instrument that missed `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER` — the flag deciding whether a
model loads at all — which `tools/check-flags.sh` could not see for two independent reasons:
its crate (`crates/memra-tokenizer/src`) was not in the script's hand-written list of three
runtime dirs, and the read is const-indirected
(`std::env::var(ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER_ENV)` carries no `MEMRA_` name on its line). Both
are fixed: the script now discovers every `crates/*/src` and resolves
`const NAME: &str = "MEMRA_…"` bindings that are actually passed to an env call, with
`tools/test_check_flags.sh` gating one regression case per cause in CI. The automated census
went 494 → 501 literal names on that change; the seven it newly sees
(`MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER`, `MEMRA_INVALID_PINNED_FRAC_TEST_CHILD`, `MEMRA_FATBIN`,
`MEMRA_FP8_FOLD`, `MEMRA_MOE_MMAP_ADVICE`, `MEMRA_NV_W4`, `MEMRA_ST_TEST_DIR`) were all
already catalogued here, so the gate stayed green — but it stayed green for the wrong reason
before, and the 546 hand count has not been re-run against the widened instrument.

---

## 1. Runtime parameters

### Generation (run-gen / run-spec / run-eagle)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PROMPT` | numeric ids `101..612` | prompt TEXT (tokenized with the model's tokenizer) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` | — | prompt text from a file (wins over `MEMRA_PROMPT`) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_TOKENS` | — | Diagnostic-only explicit token-id prompt for `verify_shape_bisect`; accepts ids separated by non-digit characters so a failing serving tape can be replayed without tokenizer/template ambiguity. |
| `MEMRA_NGEN` | bin-specific (64–256) | number of tokens to generate |
| `MEMRA_CHAT` | off | `1` wraps the prompt in the model's chat template (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PRINT_TEXT` | off | `1` decodes gold tokens between markers (agent-loop harness, run-spec, 2026-07-08) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_ONLY` | off | run-spec: skip prime-inclusive timing, report gen-only |
| `MEMRA_SEED` | 0 | sampler seed (run-gen sampling path) |
| `MEMRA_TEMP` | greedy | temperature; enables the sampling chain |
| `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` | off | sampling filters (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT` / `_FREQ` / `_PRESENT` / `_LAST_N` | off | repetition penalties (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_STOP` | EOS only | comma-separated stop strings (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` | `~/.cache/memra/models` | where `hf:owner/repo[:file]` specs download to and are looked up in (`hf/<owner>--<repo>/`; `frspec-owngen` also puts datasets under `hf-datasets/`). The one knob for putting weights on a different filesystem — point it at the fast local NVMe rather than symlinking a cache |

### Speculative decode (run-spec / serve)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K` | serve: request policy; `run-spec`: K=1..8 battery | Explicit non-negative values pin draft depth for every eligible request; `0` pins plain decode. With the flag unset, serving chooses per request: `K=0` for measured losing placement/concurrency cells, `K=2` for a long prompt with at least 1024 actually-resumed tokens (`K=5` when the loaded MTP head is FR-Spec rank-TRIMMED — the ~29 us trimmed draft lm_head re-prices the kpolicy-20260808 verdict; ornith15 cached-long 350.8 vs ~273 tok/s, research/orndecode-20260822), and `K=3` otherwise. The cached-long direction is measured, not assumed: K=2 beats K=3 by 1.49% on q9 and 7.11% on q27, N=3. The full table and raw receipts are in `research/kpolicy-20260808/`. `run_hy3_local_5090.sh` additionally accepts `all` to omit its override and run the mandatory K=1..8 battery in one model load |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HOST_EMBD` | off | `1` keeps the token-embedding table in host memory for the Hy3 spill profile, reducing HBM pressure at the cost of a host-row transfer |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN` | 0.0 | p-min confidence gate — stop the draft chain when head confidence drops below it (0.15–0.3 typical). K-DEPENDENT (2026-08-05, both rigs): at K=5 on 188 SM it's worth +6.1% e2e (0.3 optimum, 0.2/0.4 both worse); at the 5090's K=3 serve optimum it's FLAT-to-negative despite the same +4–15pp acceptance jump — a K=3 chain is too short for gating to pay. Exactness clean both rigs (byte-identical greedy, run-spec 9 PASS lever-on). Receipts: `research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/`, `research/q27-deepdive-p2-20260805/`. **glm5 scope (lane/glm5-loop-port, 2026-08-30)**: the glm5 T-parallel loop honors the same pair — native chain gates on p-of-pick (`prob_of_token_device`, one 4-byte read per armed draft, breaks BEFORE the next full-MoE-layer chain forward is paid); the DFlash2 arm truncates the proposal at the first selector-q slot below p_min (tau-slot form; greedy walk now records its T=1 q via `dflash2_walk_greedy_q`). Truncation moves draft counts only, never the tape (`gpu_confidence_gate_truncates_drafts_never_the_tape`, both arms, decisive p_min=1.1 zero-draft + slot-0-survivor arms). Engagement receipt `[glm5-spec] draft confidence gate armed: PMIN=..`. VALUE IS UNMEASURED ON GLM5 — stays unset until the box-B tau ladder prices it (per-model law: q27 lost 1.9% at 0.3; step37 ships 0.5) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0` | off | `1` lets p-min gate slot 0 too (zero-draft rounds). Pays below ~75% base acceptance, hurts above ~90% (2026-07-08, 35B +13–23 tok/s). glm5 (lane/glm5-loop-port): honored — a zero-draft glm5 round verifies the anchor row alone (m=1 = a plain decode step, always legal; sampled rounds take the shared full-accept bonus draw) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HPOST` | off | `1` feeds the MTP head POST-output_norm hidden — acceptance lever on the 27B (>100 tok/s crossing, 2026-07-06). Per-model choice |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | **MULTI-HEAD CHAINS FIXED (2026-08-27):** the trim used to `mtp_extra.clear()` — under `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=3` it silently collapsed the chain to ONE trimmed head recursed at offsets it was never trained for, measured as the K=3 deep-slot collapse (0.734/0.330/0.053 vs 0.731/0.538/0.282 untrimmed; bf16-head and no-W8 arms reproduced it bit-for-bit — the banked single-head-recursion signature, NOT the wrong-rows mechanism this row briefly claimed). NOW: per-head trim — the d2t ranking is token-frequency and head-independent (all remaps keep reading head 0's map; the chain validator requires d2t equality across heads), and each extra head's ROWS gather from its OWN block's `nextn.shared_head_head.weight` (this family ships a different lm_head per block; a block without its own head ends the chain, never substitutes). Receipt line: `[frspec-trim] per-head trim: N extra chain head(s)...`. Even the collapsed 1-head form WON both shapes under the PMIN fence (greedy 93.18 vs 86.62 untrimmed; sampled 81.79 vs 77.44) — the fence truncates what recursion breaks while the 3.6x-smaller head keeps its speed; the per-head form is upside on deep slots, gate pending |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | **HEAD SOURCE FIXED 2026-08-25 (step-3.7-flash):** the trim gathered its rows from the TRUNK's `output.weight`, which is exact only for tied-head families. step-3.7-flash ships a DIFFERENT lm_head per nextn block, and reading the trunk head there is this repo's measured 0/248-acceptance defect across K=1..8 *with* self-consistency PASS — no exactness gate catches it. The gather now prefers `blk.<n_trunk>.nextn.shared_head_head.weight` when the artifact carries it (pinned by `frspec_trim_prefers_the_nextn_blocks_own_head_name`) and falls back to the trunk head only for families that genuinely share it; `d2t_from_target_head` is set false in the own-head case so DFlash2 never borrows an MTP-head-derived trim. **STEP37 LADDER, MEASURED 2026-08-25** (K=1 = step37's banked policy, agentic turn-8 ctx, x2 reps, prime-exclusive receipt): plain 75.97 · 3-head untrimmed 58.15/57.97 · 1-head untrimmed 59.74/59.63 · **1-head trimmed to 32768 60.63/60.67**. Acceptance is BYTE-IDENTICAL at every rung (drafted 229 / accepted 170), so a 3.9x narrower draft vocabulary is acceptance-neutral; it buys +1.5% throughput and, more importantly, residency — the trimmed rungs run at the natural 262144 context where the 3-head chain OOMs (each untrimmed nextn head is BF16 [128896,4096] = 1.06 GB against 0.27 GB trimmed). Spec remains 0.80x plain because verify-wait is 68.5% of the round (GPU-bound) and routed experts do not amortize across verify columns. **FLOAT HEADS SUPPORTED (same lane):** step-3.7-flash keeps `lm_head.weight` and every `nextn.*.shared_head.output.weight` in BF16 [128896, 4096] while its experts are NVFP4, and `from_quant_bytes` panics on BF16 — the gather now lands BF16 rows in `GpuTensor::FloatBf16` and F32 rows in `Float`, quantized rows unchanged. A row gather is dtype-agnostic (rows are independent, nothing is requantized). |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | `<ranks.txt>`, `<frspec.gguf>`, or `hf:owner/repo:file-substring`: self-trimmed draft lm_head — gathers top-frequency rows from the model's own `output.weight` via the d2t ranking. An `hf:` spec resolves into `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` like a model path, so the q38 DFlash2 default can fetch its rank artifact declaratively. A **ranks `.txt`** does the trim at load time, which is the safetensors path and needs no separate draft file; a `.gguf` carries a pre-trimmed head. Generic rankings transfer across same-vocab heads, specialised ones do not (2026-07-07). Published ranks: Qwen3.8-27B agentic/prose/mixed in [Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF); Gemma family under `research/gemma4-bringup/*-ranks-*.txt` Since lane/dflash2-head-trim (2026-08-25) the DSPARK/DFlash2 selector round consumes the same self-trim BY DEFAULT when it is loaded: the round's draft lm_head matmul + top-k run over the gathered rows and candidates remap through d2t before the selector walk — verify stays full-vocab (exactness unchanged; boot receipt `[dspark] <model>: DFlash2 draft head TRIMMED to N rows`). Measured PRO 6000 x3: gate trim-q4 160.4 vs full-q4 157.9 spec tok/s; served steady chat 142.9 vs 136.3 / agentic 157.2 vs 154.5 (accept 0.64/0.73 — the wall wins the acceptance trade). Markov/chain drafter families keep the full head by construction. glm5_next (2026-08-30): the T-parallel spec loop (`MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC`) consumes the same trim — draft argmax over the gathered rows, d2t remap before verify, verify full-vocab untouched; the trunk-head gather arm is exact BY CONTRACT for this family (the NextN block ships no private lm_head and projects through the trunk head); glm5 ranks mint pending (owner lane), self-trim d2t arm live. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4` | off | `1` loads each NextN block's own lm_head as **NVFP4** instead of the BF16 the step-3.7-flash checkpoint ships (`f32_to_nvfp4` emits the internal QK=64 / 36-B block layout the decode dp4a path already consumes, so no kernel changes). RESIDENCY is the point: an untrimmed head is BF16 [128896, 4096] = 1.06 GB, so a 3-head chain spends 3.18 GB and OOMs beside a 262144-token cache — NVFP4 takes the three to 0.89 GB. This is the repo's own draft-regime standard (`tools/make-trimmed-draft.sh`: "block Q4_K_M + head NVFP4 … NVFP4 head measured zero acceptance cost"), done in-engine because that builder is a GGUF pipeline and this family is safetensors. Draft-head precision cannot change served output — verify arbitrates every drafted token — so acceptance is the only quantity at risk, and it is what the trim ladder measures. **MEASURED 2026-08-25, K=1 agentic ctx, prime-exclusive:** acceptance is UNCHANGED by the 4-bit head — 170 accepted in every arm (trimmed or full, 1-head or 3-head). ctx 8192: bf16-trim 60.62 (head 256 MiB) vs nvfp4-trim 60.05 (72 MiB, 3.6x); bf16-3head 58.13 (3x1007 MiB) vs nvfp4-3head 58.57 (3x283 MiB). NVFP4 is the RESIDENCY choice, not the speed one: -0.9% on the trimmed head because the dp4a/LUT unpack is ALU-bound near 0.8 TB/s where q8 mmvq measures 1.71, +0.8% on the chain where smaller heads relieve cache pressure. At the natural 262144 context only the SINGLE trimmed head fits (60.18 tok/s): the 3-head chain still OOMs after the 2.2 GB saving, and so does W8 alongside it. |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM_NVFP4` | off | `1` re-quantizes the FR-Spec trimmed rows BF16 -> NVFP4 (same encoder, same reasoning). A trimmed head goes 0.27 GB -> 0.076 GB. Independent of `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4`, which covers the untrimmed heads. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS` | all declared | `N` caps the embedded NextN chain at N heads. A measurement knob, added 2026-08-25 with the step-3.7-flash trim work: `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` forces the chain to ONE head, so a trimmed-vs-untrimmed A/B otherwise confounds the trim with the loss of the chain. With this the ladder is 3-head untrimmed -> 1-head untrimmed -> 1-head trimmed and each step is attributable. Prints `[mtp-chain] MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=N: capping the embedded chain from M heads`. Note `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=0` is silently ignored (the parser filters non-positive values); "load no MTP block at all" is `MEMRA_MTP_SKIP`, below. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_SKIP` | off | `1` skips loading the embedded MTP/NextN block(s) at model load: the block's attention mixer, full FFN (MoE included), and nextn glue (enorm/hnorm/eh_proj, per-block head) are never uploaded, reclaiming their VRAM on deployments whose drafting program is dspark/DFlash2 and whose MTP spec arm is disabled anyway (the q38 serving shape; owner call 2026-08-30, goal: extra session headroom on the serving card). Receipt: `[mtp-skip] MEMRA_MTP_SKIP=1: skipping N embedded MTP/NextN block(s) blk.X..=blk.Y (~M MiB of weights not loaded)`. The DFlash2 trimmed draft head SURVIVES the skip: with `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` set, the loader gathers the trimmed rows from the trunk `output.weight` as usual and parks them in a dedicated stub (`DflashTrimHead`; receipt `[mtp-skip] FR-Spec stub draft head built: N rows of main output.weight ...`), which the dspark round consumes identically, so boot still prints `[dspark] <model>: DFlash2 draft head TRIMMED to N rows` with the same N. `model.mtp` stays `None`, so `mtp_spec_capable` and every MTP forward path are off by construction, never reachable through the stub. REFUSES LOUD instead of no-opping (each arm executed in the lane receipts): skip together with `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` (contradictory); skip + trim on an artifact that ships its own per-block lm_head (the trim rows would live in the skipped block: the 0/248-acceptance wrong-head class); skip + trim with an empty d2t list; any value other than `0`/`1`; and, with no dspark drafter armed, ANY `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC` state other than the literal `0` (refuse-loud contract 2026-08-31, hermes finding baf261e2bfdae118): an explicit non-zero value is a spec request the server cannot honor (`[mtp-skip] <model>: ... cannot be honored`), and unset/blank leaves serving spec ON by default, so booting would silently serve plain decode at reduced speed with no receipt (the DFlash2 2026-08-25 incident class; this supersedes the original announce-and-boot default). Only `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0` boots, announcing `[mtp-skip] <model>: serves PLAIN decode by explicit choice ...` per affected model. A model with no declared NextN block is untouched (a global env must not kill a co-loaded plain model), and the flag only exists on the hybrid loader (`load_from_source_impl`); the dsv4 research loader keeps its own MTP path. OFF arm (default, unset or `0`): byte-identical boot receipts and outputs, gated. Rollback seam: unset the env and restart; no persisted state. Default OFF by design: the skip trades the embedded MTP spec arm away, a choice only a dspark-armed (or deliberately plain) deployment should make. VRAM delta, boot-receipt parity, and exactness receipts: `research/mtp-skip-q38-20260830/`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_MTP` | off | `1` loads the glm5_next NextN/MTP block as the embedded draft head (MLA + own k-pool indexer + MoE, serial residual — the checkpoint ships no private lm_head, so the draft projects through the trunk `lm_head`). DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (2026-08-30): the head is a full MoE layer (288 routed experts on GLM-5.3-Flash), so an unconditional load adds a trunk-layer of VRAM and load time with NOTHING consuming it yet — with `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC` unset the spec entry points refuse hc trunks (`refuse_hyper`) and both spec manifests report the plan unblessed for serving, so the worker never routes to it; the consumers are the draft-side forward `mtp_head_forward_mla_cached` (and its gate) and, since lane/glm5-spec-routing (2026-08-30), the glm5 T-parallel serve route when `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC=1` also arms. OFF arm = today's serving, byte-identical (the head was ALREADY never loaded: the four `nextn.*` glue names had no glm5_next ggml->HF mapping row until this lane, so `blk.45.nextn.eh_proj.weight` read as absent and the embedded-MTP loop broke at offset 0). ON arm = head loads (log line `[mtp-glm5] MEMRA_GLM5_MTP=1: loading the glm5_next NextN block`), draft forward gated engine-vs-reference at fixture scale: green worst-row 1.144e-3 vs eh_proj-transpose 4.165e0 and h_seed-off-by-one 6.723e-1 red arms (`glm5_mtp_head_gpu`, 5090, TF32 off, 2026-08-30). Rollback seam: unset the flag (load-time only, no persistent state). Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mtp-draft-20260830/` |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC` | off | `1` is the ONE master flag for glm5_next speculative decoding — it flips BOTH the engine route (`generate_spec` on an hc trunk with a loaded MTP head -> the T-parallel loop) AND, since lane/glm5-spec-routing (2026-08-30), the SERVING route: `glm5_spec_capable` in the worker requires it, so with it unset no customer request can reach the loop. The program: draft K via `mtp_head_forward_mla_cached` -> verify in ONE t=K+1 walk in the batched-decode kernel classes (`glm5_verify_rows`; **K+1 <= `hyper_batch_cap()` = 15 hard bound**, the shexp decode-exact knee — the worker clamps operator pins there, loudly) -> greedy longest-matching-prefix OR rejection-sampled accept (`SpecSampling`: filtered p/q via `filter_stats`/`softmax_gather_filtered`, residual resample on rejection, host accept uniforms `host_u01` tag 0xFFFF_FFFE, Philox counters session-owned — randomness never repeats across bursts) -> rollback (MLA latent truncate + `truncate_index_pool_keys` clamp; KDA per-step state columns, ~0.95 GiB transient at K=7 on GLM-5.3-Flash). SERVING SHAPE (all decisions deliberate, each with its receipt): capability = hc trunk + loaded MTP head (`MEMRA_GLM5_MTP=1`) + this flag + a gated placement (single-engine, OR a ppN split at **stages 2 or 3** with a qualified pipeline rewrite — lane/glm5-ppn-verify landed the verify-walk ppN twin, per-stage rollback and last-stage MTP chain, red-proven by `glm5-spec-ppn-gate`; more stages or a step-TP/EP composition refuses by name via `glm5_sharded_placement_admits`) + the `GLM5_SPEC` manifest class (exactly glm5_next; qwen/dsv4/gemma stay fail-closed — `glm5_spec_class_matrix` pins the matrix, and `MTP_SPEC` still reports glm5 unsupported so the two spec programs can never both claim one plan) + a `glm5-spec.v1` rewrite receipt when a sealed bundle is loaded (fail-closed for production bundles until the real-artifact qualification lane banks one). K rides the SHARED spec-K policy surface (`MEMRA_SPEC_K` pin / `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE*` concurrency shed / prompt-cache table: cold 3, cached-long 2, trim 5 — the kpolicy-20260808 + orndecode-20260822 precedents; NOTE, measured 2026-08-30: glm5 prefix entries cannot snapshot, so the `cached>=1024` rows are unreachable and the policy default is K=3 everywhere on this family, and the c>2 concurrency shed is protective — pinning K=3 through c=4 costs 24% aggregate throughput while the automatic policy sheds to plain and matches it exactly, `3way-decision-20260830/` cell 5). EXCLUSIONS, each loud: penalized requests (greedy AND sampled — no penalty arm yet) serve plain; constrained, vision, warm/reused sessions serve plain (cold-only — except the prefix-restored carrier under `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX`, its own row, lane/glm5-prefix-latent2; parking/demotion-to-park are named follow-ups); admission memory reserve still prices the session as plain — the K-column KDA transient is unpriced until the real-artifact lane. The `_rf` tail: (a) one shared region per token column (`red[t*blockDim]`, dynamic shared = t*block*4 B) so the t trees share ONE barrier sequence — a store barrier plus one per block-wide level down to s=32; (b) levels s<=16 are intra-warp (`__shfl_down_sync` chain, offsets 16..1, one column per warp) — **9t -> 3 barriers per block** on the x1 form at 128 threads. Gate `crates/memra-engine/tests/glm5_matvec_doors_gpu.rs::gpu_red_fused_tcols_matches_standing_tree_bitwise`: both grid forms t=2..=8, tcols16 t=9..=16, the t=1 degenerate bounds vs the per-row t=1 program, all bitwise with `BF16_TCOLS_RED_FUSED_DISPATCHES` anchored (>0 ON, flat on the pinned-`=0` arm); the SHIFTED-PAIRING red (`..._rf_redshift`, ascending shuffle offsets = a different association of the same 32 partials) must bite. ENGAGEMENT GUARD: requires a power-of-two `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` (the fused tail's block-wide loop must pass exactly through s=32; the default 128 qualifies) — any other block falls through to the standing tree. DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (new-flags law): the rig is exactness-only, no box timing receipt exists; predicted **-1.0 to -2.0 ms/round on the 37.328 ms winner wall, capped by the 87% bound at -1.94** (moe-loc §2.2: kda trunk alone -1.75 at the bound); the box window prices and owns the flip. This seam remains a correctness program, NEVER a serving or performance claim (`performance_claim=false` on every `[glm5-tp-*]` marker, and those markers now print the LIVE `transport=`); the join-diet doors are the orthogonal count-cutting axis and the serving wiring is still the named box arc. A rank count other than 2 still refuses by name at spawn, before any model load starts: TP-4 is engine-qualified here but its serving lifecycle has not been box-gated (a separate increment). See docs/SERVING.md "TP-2 serving (serving half)". | `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` | **host-canonical** (OFF by written decision) | GENERAL TP transport seam (generalized from `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT`, lane/glm5-extract2 — the alias row below stays honored). ENGINE-GENERIC BY CONTENT: `tp_transport.rs` is `Rank`, a publication event and named point-to-point hop shapes over dense blocks — not one function in it knows a layer type, a mixer, an expert or a model family (`pp.rs`'s `BoundarySlot` is where the shape came from). Rank-widened to TP-N by lane/glm5-composition and merged here: ranks are plain indices (`0` = root, owner-first unchanged), the ladder runs over every ordered rank pair, and at two ranks each arm reproduces the v1 walk HOP FOR HOP so the banked TP-2 numbers stay comparable. The glm5 TP-N shard walk is the ONLY consumer today and keeps its rank geometry, owner-first law and shard maps; an hy3/step TP walk will arm the same transport once it exists — the seam is general, the second consumer is not written. Same silence as the EP doors and worth the same warning: `tp_transport::transport_env()` has exactly ONE caller (the glm5 TP loader), so setting this on a non-glm5 deployment today does nothing and says nothing. RECEIPT TAGS ARE THE CALLER'S (the phase-1 `[glm5-phase]` rule): glm5 passes `glm5-tp-transport`, so every banked receipt line from lane/glm5-tp-transport keeps its exact bytes. TWO-NAME LAW: this is a VALUED flag resolved ONCE at arm time, so both names set to DISAGREEING values REFUSES THE LOAD naming both (`tp_transport::resolve_transport`) — unlike the per-call boolean doors, which fall closed, because arming happens before any session exists and a load that silently picked a transport the operator did not choose is worse than a refused load. Every refusal and every peer-access grant names the flag the OPERATOR set. Gate arms: `glm5-tp-gate` X0/X1/X2/X3/XT/XF drive the alias (the banked path), **XG** arms peer-pull through the general name with the alias unset (peer_pulls must be >0), **XD** sets a disagreeing pair and asserts the load refuses naming both. Original lane text: glm5 TP-2 TRANSPORT ARM (lane/glm5-tp-transport, 2026-09-01) — WHICH BYTES-MOVER the armed `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` seam uses for its cross-rank hops. Values (`tp_transport::parse_transport`, any other spelling REFUSES by name at load): `host-canonical` / `0` / unset = the v1 arm, every hop a `dtoh` -> host `Vec` -> `htod` pair; `peer-pull` / `1` = every hop a consumer-issued device peer copy (`cuMemcpyDtoDAsync` on the READING rank's stream) published by one CUDA event per rank, with ZERO host boundaries. WHY THE ARM EXISTS: the TP program's cross-rank hops are pure movement (the one arithmetic site, the MoE slot-ordered `fmaf` combine, stays on root), so the transport cannot change a bit — and the movement census derived from code puts the v1 arm at **446 draining `Engine::dtoh` calls and 14.63 MiB per decode token**, which at the measured **13-18 ms/token v1 join tax** (`tp2-battery-20260831/RESULTS.md` cell 3) is **29-40 us per host round trip** and leaves bytes contributing 2% of the tax. It is a COUNT tax, and this flag deletes the count. BOTH ARMS, so a reviewer can price the rollback: OFF keeps v1's hop pattern hop-for-hop (not just byte-for-byte — the sequential EP walk deliberately keeps its one-z-`dtoh`-then-per-token-row-`htod` shape so `=0` reproduces the banked 22.65 tok/s engine-twin walk and not a faster cousin); ON does 4 peer reads + 3 local copies + 4 event primitives per KDA layer-call, 5 + 3 + 5 per MLA, 2 per dieted MoE. PULL not push, by design: `RESEARCH.md` §2.2b measures SM-issued peer reads at ~52 GB/s against writes at 2.6 GB/s (110 us push vs 26 us pull) and b12x banks push collectives as a receipted dead end — that scoping is SM-issued and our copies are copy-engine (~54-56 GB/s both ways), so the load-bearing reason is ORDERING: a consumer-issued copy is ordered against the consuming kernel for free, where a producer-issued push needs a second event. ATOMICS-FREE and SENTINEL-FREE, forced: every SM120 PCIe pair reports `NativeAtomicSupported=0` (§2.1) and CAS on peer memory loses barrier tokens LOAD-DEPENDENTLY, and a payload-value sentinel prototype caused a launch-day production stall (§6.13) — so `nvidia-smi topo -p2p a` returning `NS` is EXPECTED here, not a fault. THE DELTA VS THE STEP SEAM, which is why this is a new arm and not a copy of `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P`: `tp.rs`'s pull sites fence the producer with a host `engine.stream().synchronize()` (the "PRODUCER FENCE (2026-08-20 flake fix)" comments at `tp.rs:2636`/`:3543`) — correct, and a full stream drain, i.e. it keeps the cost class this arm exists to delete. This arm takes the `pp.rs` `BoundarySlot` ordering contract instead (`ev.record(tx)` then `rx.wait(&ev)`), which the hy3 PP-4 qualification closed at 50/50 fresh processes and 200/200 runtime probes with zero byte mismatch on this exact RTX PRO 6000 card class. FAIL-CLOSED ARMING: on a real pair, `tp::grant_peer_access` in BOTH directions (both halves — `cuCtxEnablePeerAccess` for legacy allocations AND `cuMemPoolSetAccess` on the owner's default pool, because the former does not map stream-ordered pool allocations and every memra buffer is one), then a byte-integrity PULL ladder through the exact primitive the walk uses, both directions, at 16 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB / 64 MiB, against a poisoned destination, compared on `f32::to_bits`; one differing word refuses the load with zero shards built. Announce `[glm5-tp-transport] armed transport=... performance_claim=false` plus the ladder PASS line, and all four pre-existing seam announces (`[glm5-tp-preflight]`, `-kda`, `-mla`, `-ep`) now print the LIVE transport instead of the hardcoded `transport=host-canonical` they printed before (a hardcoded value would have made this very A/B unreadable from the boot log the battery greps). WHAT THE LADDER DOES NOT PROVE, stated because §2.4 is emphatic: it is a TRANSFER-tier check. It does not prove a KERNEL peer dereference works, and on direct-attach (`NODE`) hosts — our box class — the driver stages SM-issued peer access through SysMem by default (§2.3b) while `topo -p2p r` reads OK and `cudaMemcpy` looks healthy. This arm never dereferences a peer pointer from a kernel, so that default cannot bite it and NO `ForceP2P` modprobe change is required to ship it; the kernel path is measured separately by the lane's `peer-read-probe.cu` (a `simpleP2P`-class byte-validating kernel peer read) which `tools/box-health.sh` runs at every box window, and which gates the FUTURE fused-collective arm. WHY THE DEFAULT IS OFF: on the day this landed the ON arm had ZERO receipts on real peer hardware — the rig is one card (`LAW:rig-exactness-only`), so its gate arms run the code over two contexts on ONE device and prove bit-preservation only, never fabric engagement. Unmeasured behaviour does not default ON. The default flips in the same commit as the box window's interleaved-x5 re-price receipt, never before. ROLLBACK SEAM: `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT=0` (or unset) restores the v1 walk exactly; the flag is read once at preflight and frozen on the runtime, so no request can straddle arms. GATES: `glm5-tp-gate` ALL ARMS on BOTH transports under the same-device emulation, bars UNCHANGED (decode BYTE identity on the non-MoE classes, the calibrated 2e-4 prime band, the pre-registered EP band, reds orders above), PLUS a transport-vs-transport byte-identity bar decode AND prime — because a transport that moved prime bits inside the calibrated band would hide inside it — PLUS census non-vacuity (OFF: `host_syncs>0 && peer_pulls==0`; ON: `peer_pulls>0 && host_syncs==0 && host_legs==0`). COMPOSITION: orthogonal to `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_DIET` and `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_GROUPED_PRIME` by construction (those cut hop COUNT, this cuts hop COST) and both compose; every `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` refusal (PP>1, step doors, spec sessions, the four decode-diet doors) applies unchanged. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp-transport-20260901/LANE.md` (the receipt-correction note, the movement census, the payload histogram, the TP-2/TP-4 predicted arithmetic), `gates/` — plus the two probes that lane wrote, now promoted to `tools/box-health.sh` and `tools/peer-read-probe.cu` (lane/glm5-extract2; docs row in docs/TESTING.md). |

Same values, same fail-closed parse, resolved by `tp_transport::resolve_transport`; set alone it is honored and refusals then name THIS flag; set alongside a DISAGREEING `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` the load refuses naming both. | `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_GATE_RED` | unset | gate-harness knob, never a serving flag: `swap-wo` hands each glm5-TP rank the OTHER rank's column-`wo` out rows (broken shard map, load-time); `swap-ep-gateup` swaps the root EP slab's gate/up projections (wrong expert weights, load-time); `skip-peer-combine` drops every peer-owned expert slot at the EP combine (RUNTIME — the knob must stay set through the walk; it doubles as the peer-engagement non-vacuity proof); `corrupt-ep-map` reverses the placement's rank-0 local-slot table after slab packing (owner table and slab bytes disagree — the corrupted-map-row red, load-time). Each red's run MUST diverge loudly (`glm5-tp-gate` banks the exits); unknown values refuse at load. Read only by the gate harness, never by `crate::glm5_tp`'s serving-load path, so it cannot reach a TP-2 serving worker even now that `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` is admitted there (lane/glm5-tp-serve-wiring-20260902) |

WHY: on the serving shape the HOST table build is the selection's ONLY consumer, and paying for it costs a full `cuStreamSynchronize` + 2 DtoH + 2 host `Vec` allocations + 2 pageable HtoD PER MoE LAYER-CALL = **42 device-wide drains, 84 DtoH and 84 HtoD per ship round** (the decode-gap attribution's "43 cuStreamSynchronize/token ... the per-layer router-admission sync structure", and 53.8% of the 156 HtoD calls/round this lane attributed against the census's UNATTRIBUTED 71.6/token). ONE launch path, two table provenances — nothing downstream can tell the arms apart. | `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEDUP_STAT` | off | glm5-moe-loc MEASUREMENT INSTRUMENT, never a serving door: on the vrows HOST table-build arm, count the pair union's expert VISITS (`t*n_used`) and DISTINCT experts per layer-call into `MOE_VROWS_PAIR_VISITS` / `MOE_VROWS_PAIR_DISTINCT`. WHY IT EXISTS: the pair runs at **90.2% / 89.9% of this card class's theoretical 1.79 TB/s DRAM peak** and therefore ABOVE the banked 87% achievable bound (moe-loc LANE.md §1: `down`/`gate_up` avg_us ratio 0.5016 vs byte ratio 0.5000 — time IS bytes to 0.3%), so cross-row expert-slab DEDUP is the only remaining byte lever on it, and its size is exactly `1 - distinct/visits`. That number is an unmeasured routing property spanning **+0.9% (the 3.2% independent-routing bound) to +23% (the 70.1% all-rows-identical ceiling)** of ship tok/s — far too wide to start a kernel campaign on. This counter prices it for the cost of a host bitset. NOT the same measurement as the `moesd` capture, which unions experts across a whole capture WINDOW (a resident-working-set question, not a per-layer-call sharing question). REQUIRES `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEV_TABLES=0` — door D removes the host selection this counts. Adds a `HashSet` per layer-call, so it is OFF in every timed arm; report it from a dedicated instrument boot. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/moe-loc-20260831/LANE.md` §1.6. |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEDUP_ORDER` | off | glm5-dedup door E: the verify-rows MoE gate/up launch takes the `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_rows_ord` twin — the grid is TRANSPOSED so the PAIR index is the fastest dimension (`(n_pairs, n_ff)` instead of `(n_ff, n_pairs)`) and the pair each block walks comes from an EXPERT-MAJOR order plane appended to the pointer table as a fourth plane (`ptrs[3*n_pairs ..)`, gate\|up\|down\|order). WHY: the struct-battery instrument booted `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEDUP_STAT` on the real artifact and the ship recipe and measured a **21.96% cumulative repeat fraction** over 99,751 layer-calls / 2.55M expert visits (mode-stable: 22.27% greedy / 21.53% vendor-default sampled) — **6.9x the 3.21% independent-routing bound**. The pair is at 90.2% / 89.9% of this card class's theoretical DRAM peak, so the ONLY lever left is not re-reading a slab a sibling verify row already read, and that requires the repeat visit to be SCHEDULED inside the reuse window. This transposition collapses the reuse distance from "a 9.44 MB slab pass must survive in L2" (2048 blocks under the shipped o-fastest grid) to ~1 block over ~2 x 2.3 KB of expert row bytes — an L1-class hit rather than an L2 gamble; the charter's slab-residency argument (4.72 MB slab vs ~128 MB L2) is the fallback, not the operative mechanism. EXACTNESS: every output is a pure function of its `(o, pr)` coordinate (`ptrs[pr]`, `scl[pr]`, `tok = pr/n_used`, `act[pr*n_ff+o]`) and no block communicates — neither kernel has a `__syncthreads` or shared memory — so re-indexing WHICH BLOCK COMPUTES WHICH OUTPUT moves no bits; the order plane is a permutation, so every pair is still computed exactly once. Gate `glm5_dedup_sched_gpu`: device order plane == host stable sort (permutation + stable-expert-major + run-count==distinct asserted), gate/up bitwise vs the shipped schedule in 28 arms (t=2..=8 x {live macros, none} x {host tables, device tables}), 3 VALID SHUFFLES bit-INERT, and two NON-PERMUTATION reds (one dropped pair / all-zero plane) that BITE. BOTH TABLE ARMS: with door D off the host appends the plane to the vector it already uploads (zero extra transfers); with door D on `moe_vrows_order_from_sel` builds it on device (+42 launches/round ~ 0.093 ms, the named fusion follow-up recovers it). REFUSED BY NAME, falling closed to the shipped schedule: door M (`MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_PACK`) takes precedence in the launcher, the door engages only when the order plane is actually present (`ptrs.len() >= 4*n_pairs`, so every standing gate's 3-plane call keeps the shipped program), and `n_ff > 65535` falls closed on the grid.y bound. DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN: the identity is structural but the WIN is a block-dispatch-order property, and the rig is exactness-only (rig law) — it ships with a COUNT receipt (`MOE_VROWS_DEDUP_ORDER_DISPATCHES`, `MOE_VROWS_SLAB_READS_AVOIDED`) and a predicted **-1.44 ms/round** for this half; the box prices the wall. Rollback: read PER CALL at the dispatch and the launcher; `=0`/unset restores the shipped grid AND stops the plane being built. Announce once per boot: `[moe-vrows-dedup-order] engaged`. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/dedup-20260831/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DOWN_TMAJ` | off | glm5-dedup door E-down: the verify-rows MoE down launch takes the `moe_down8_fma_q8_rows_tmaj` twin — grid transposed to `(t, out_f)`, TOKEN fastest, so the t verify rows at one output row are adjacent blocks and a repeated expert's down row (1152 B at the serving geometry) is read once for every token that shares it. Split from `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEDUP_ORDER` as its own flag so the box can attribute the two halves of the dedup lever separately: gate/up is 2/3 of the pair's 15.896 GB/round, down 1/3. EXACTNESS: the slot-ordered `__fmaf_rn` chain that IS the accumulation order lives INSIDE the block and keeps its ORIGINAL slot order (`j = 0..n_used-1`, `pr = tok*n_used + j`) — the vrest gate-4 bit bar is untouched; only the grid moves, and no block communicates. The down chain therefore takes NO order plane (a permutation of it would move the accumulation, which is refused). Gate `glm5_dedup_sched_gpu`: bitwise vs the shipped schedule in 42 arms (t=2..=8 x {live macros, none} x {down-only, composed host tables, composed device tables}), with routing weights spanning five decades so a reordered accumulation could not pass as identical. Refusals: door M wins; `out_f > 65535` falls closed. DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN, same reason as door E — predicted **-0.72 ms/round**, count receipt `MOE_VROWS_DOWN_TMAJ_DISPATCHES`, box prices the wall. Rollback: read PER CALL at the launcher. Announce once per boot: `[moe-vrows-down-tmaj] engaged`. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/dedup-20260831/LANE.md`. |

The standard artifact is the regime draft (docs/DRAFT-REGIME.md): own-gen ranks + NVFP4 head + Q4_K_M block, one file, no other flags. Per-model equivalent on the server: `MEMRA_MODELS="name=/trunk.gguf+/draft.gguf"`. This is ALSO how step35 attaches its published external MTP head (Step-3.7-Flash ships the NextN blocks in a separate GGUF, so the trunk reads `nextn=0`); a step35 trunk served WITHOUT one WARNS at load, and a `+draft` path that is missing or unloadable REFUSES to start with the cause quoted rather than degrading to plain decode. Receipts `research/step-draft-20260807/` |

It does **NOT** attach an MTP head; on a qwen/step35 model it attaches nothing at all. What it *does* do everywhere, by mere presence, is re-key three defaults onto "spec serving": `memra_kv::wkv_on()` → **OFF** (gemma windowed KV leaves e4m3 for q8_0/q5_1 — `memra-kv/src/lib.rs`), `fa_f16pv_on()` → **OFF** (f16 P/V prefill class — `memra-engine/src/lib.rs`), and `MMQ_SK_FORCE` → tiling for big dense `n_embd ≥ 3500` at model load (`hybrid.rs`). Each is separately measured and separately correct (see the `MEMRA_GEMMA_WKV` / `MEMRA_FA_F16PV` / `MEMRA_MMQ_SK` rows), and each is *silent*. Explicit `MEMRA_GEMMA_WKV` / `MEMRA_FA_F16PV` / `MEMRA_MMQ_SK` always win over the flip. |

Flipped the 31B chat cell from −17% to +2.5% and made trim ≥ untrimmed on EVERY measured cell (2026-07-19) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP` | greedy (0.0) | speculative sampling temperature — the spec path's own knob, NOT `MEMRA_TEMP`. Above 0 switches the round from greedy verify to Leviathan/Chen modified rejection sampling (`norm(max(0,p-q))` on reject, bonus drawn from `p` on full accept), and takes `MEMRA_SEED` plus the `MEMRA_PENALTY_*` set through the same `SpecSampling` struct. Its gate is seeded REPRODUCIBILITY, not token-identity to plain sampling: spec and plain consume randomness differently, so Leviathan/Chen guarantees equality of DISTRIBUTION only — `run-spec` asserts the same (seed, prompt, K) reproduces an identical stream on a second run |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_CAPMAX` | 7 | gemma adaptive-K cap ceiling. >7 opens the b16 verify tier (t=9..16) — correct since 2026-07-12 (three host bugs fixed) but measured perf-negative (depth K8-10 283-293 vs K6 301-306); the door is a measurement, not a crash |

#### SEAM TRAP: `MEMRA_DRAFT` is the GEMMA drafter. `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` is the QWEN one.

Two flags, one word apart, attaching different mechanisms to different families. Getting it
wrong does not error — **it passes.**

| you want | flag | equivalent | log line that PROVES it |
|---|---|---|---|
| qwen / step35 MTP head from a separate GGUF (e.g. the FR-Spec drafter) | `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT=<draft.gguf>` | `MEMRA_MODELS="name=<trunk>+<draft>"` | `[mtp-draft] loading external MTP draft: …` / `[worker] <name>: regime draft attached (…)` |
| gemma-4 assistant drafter over trunk KV | `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K` **+** `MEMRA_DRAFT=<assistant.gguf>` | — | the gemma draft-attach line at spawn; boot PANICS if `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC` is set without `MEMRA_DRAFT` |

**How this went wrong, so the shape is recognisable (96GB window, 2026-08-19).** A qwen
27B cell was launched with the gemma flag. The server logged `models config = [(…, None)]`
and **no `[mtp-draft]` line** — the drafter was never loaded. Spec still engaged, off the
trunk's own embedded MTP head, so **every assertion in the cell went green** while the thing
the cell existed to test ("the frspec drafter loads and drafts") was never exercised. Two
compounding reasons it was invisible: an unused `MEMRA_DRAFT` on a non-gemma model is not an
error, and a qwen trunk that already carries `nextn>0` has a working drafter *without* the
file — so the negative control was silently unavailable.

Rules that follow, and are now enforced rather than remembered:

- **A gate tool handed a drafter path MUST assert the attach line in the server log.** A run
  with no `[mtp-draft]` / `regime draft attached` line is a FAILED run, not a passing one —
  see `tools/assert-drafter-attached.sh`, wired into `spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh`,
  `spec-cache-gate.sh`, `spec-cache-mixed-gate.sh`, `sampled-restore-ab.sh` and
  `btemp-power.sh`. The qwen seam in every one of them is `MEMRA_GATE_MTP_DRAFT=<f>`
  (forwarded as `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT`); `spec-cache-mixed-gate.sh`'s positional 4th argument
  is the GEMMA drafter and the tool now refuses if both are given.
- **Never infer the attach from spec engaging.** `usage.spec.accepted > 0` on a qwen trunk
  proves the *embedded* head ran; it says nothing about an external drafter.
- **`MEMRA_DRAFT` on a non-gemma run is a config error, not a no-op** — it still flips
  `wkv_on()`, `fa_f16pv_on()` and the MMQ-SK form, so such a run is not even measuring the
  production kernel/format defaults.

### Benchmark harness (run-gen / session-bench)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` | off | prefill-only timing mode (times `prime_cache` — the pass serving primes with, and the ONLY prefill class that carries the m≥16 GEMM/MMQ hooks; see `MEMRA_PP_NLL`) |
| `MEMRA_PP_REPS` | 1 | prefill repetitions (N-median protocol) |
| `MEMRA_PP_WARMUP` | 1 | prefill warmup runs |
| `MEMRA_PP_LOGITS` | off | `<file>`: under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY`, dump `prime_cache`'s last-row logits as raw LE f32 — the cross-arm drift vector (max_abs / rms_rel / top-k order) for a **prefill-GEMM** arm |
| `MEMRA_PP_NLL` | off | `1`: under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY`, teacher-forced prefill exactness on the prime path — argmax disagreement + mean NLL where **the prompt itself is the tape** (position `i`'s logits score prompt token `i+1`). No reference-tape asymmetry by construction: both arms are scored on the same externally-given sequence, so neither can win by reproducing its own output (contrast the decode battery, whose tape is one arm's greedy output and therefore needs a reverse-tape control). WHY IT EXISTS: `run-gen`'s verify-prefill gate calls `decode_step_t` → `matmul_decode_exact`, which has NO GEMM/MMQ arm by design (decode-parity law), so `MEMRA_PREFILL_LOGITS` is **blind** to a prefill-GEMM arm — it reports identical vectors and `hook entries=0` no matter what flag is set. Added 2026-08-05 after that exact trap invalidated a first pass at the block-128 MMQ cells |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_LOGITS` | off | `<file>`: dump the VERIFY-class (`decode_step_t`) batched-prefill logit row as raw LE f32. Cross-arm instrument for a kernel that changes the verify class — **not** for a prefill-GEMM arm (use `MEMRA_PP_NLL` / `MEMRA_PP_LOGITS`) |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE` | 32 | decode tokens measured after counter reset |
| `MEMRA_FORCE_TOKENS_FILE` | off | run-gen diagnostic: teacher-force numeric token ids from a file so placement A/Bs traverse an identical decode route. Also the decode-side branch-(b) exactness instrument (disagreement count + tape NLL); pair it with a REVERSE-TAPE control run, since a tape that is one arm's own greedy output structurally favours that arm |
| `MEMRA_FORCE_LOGITS_AT` / `MEMRA_FORCE_LOGITS_FILE` | off | run-gen diagnostic used with `MEMRA_FORCE_TOKENS_FILE`: dump the raw LE f32 logit row that predicts the zero-based forced-tape position named by `_AT`. Both variables and the forcing tape are required, so an incomplete probe cannot silently measure a different trajectory |
| `MEMRA_TURN_TOKENS` | 512 | session-bench: suffix length per turn |
| `MEMRA_HIST_FILE` | required | session-bench: conversation history text file |

### Server (memra-server)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_MODELS` | — | server model list `alias=path[+draft.gguf],...` — `+draft` attaches that model's regime draft per model (docs/DRAFT-REGIME.md); paths accept `hf:` specs. A path may also be an HF safetensors checkpoint DIR (`config.json` + `model.safetensors[.index.json]`, chat template from `tokenizer_config.json`/`chat_template.jinja`) or a memra repack dir (`manifest.json`) — validated at startup, template-less dir ckpts 400 on chat (serve-st lane, `research/serve-st-20260803/`) |
| `MEMRA_REWRITE_BUNDLE` | none | Path to a sealed `memra model inspect`/`verify` bundle. At model load the worker validates the artifact lock, compiled-plan hash, rewrite manifest, indexed receipt hashes, and each receipt's artifact/plan binding before admitting an optimized rewrite. An absent bundle keeps the legacy native policy; an invalid configured bundle fails model load. Optional unqualified batch/graph/prime surfaces may use the bundle's qualified native-eager rewrite, while required speculative and pipeline selections fail closed. |
| `MEMRA_MODEL_METADATA` | none | TOML metadata for strict marketplace feeds: `GET /models?schema=openrouter` (OpenRouter Provider Monitor 2.4) and `GET /models?schema=openmodels` (OpenModels provider shape). `[models."<MEMRA_MODELS alias>"]` declares description/quantization/per-request limits/operational fields, with nested `.pricing` per-unit USD strings and `.capacity` limits. Declared `max_output_length` bounds explicit `max_tokens`; optional `default_output_length` separately bounds an omitted value so a large provider ceiling does not force a large KV reservation. Declared `max_prompt_length` is checked against the worker-rendered/tokenized prompt before cache lookup or GPU admission, and every request remains capped by trained model context. Optional `default_reasoning_effort` (`none\|minimal\|low\|medium\|high`, boot-validated) resolves requests that expressed no reasoning choice as if the client had sent that value; explicit client reasoning fields always win. Optional per-model VENDOR SAMPLING defaults — `default_temperature` (`(0,2]`), `default_top_p` (`(0,1]`), `default_top_k` (`>=0`), `default_min_p` (`[0,1)`), `default_presence_penalty` / `default_frequency_penalty` (`[-2,2]`), `default_repetition_penalty` (`>0`), all boot-validated — substitute the model vendor's own published recommendation into sampling fields the client OMITTED, identically on all four surfaces (`/v1/completions`, `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages`, `/v1/responses`); an explicit client value always wins, and an explicit `temperature: 0` still produces true greedy. `default_temperature = 0` is REFUSED at boot: a zero default would reinstate greedy-by-default deployment-wide for every omitting client. Optional `surface` (`chat`\|`embedding`\|`rerank`, default `chat`, boot-validated) declares which API surface the model serves, and is the ONLY input to the model `type`, `endpoints`, `output_modalities` and the chat-shaped capability flags on all three catalog feeds (`/v1/models`, `?schema=openrouter`, `?schema=openmodels`) — an embedder must not advertise `tools`/`streaming`/`reasoning`, and a surface that emits no completion tokens advertises no `max_output_tokens`. It is DECLARED rather than inferred because embedding/rerank capability is decided at runtime (does the prime path yield hidden state) and is unavailable when the catalog row is built; omitting it reproduces the pre-2026-08-28 chat row byte-for-byte. Undeclared parameters keep the API-standard default rather than an invented one. An optional nested `.non_thinking_sampling` table (same seven keys unprefixed, same ranges/boot validation, empty table refused) declares the vendor's SECOND arm: a request whose RESOLVED thinking mode is off (any off spelling, an operator `default_reasoning_effort = "none"`, or a `response_format`-forced switch) takes that arm for its unset sampling fields; models without the table are byte-identical to before it existed, and the arms never blend field-by-field. Both these key sets are `deny_unknown_fields`, so an OLDER binary FAILS BOOT on a config declaring them — deploy the binary before the config. `[planned_models]` entries are validated but never emitted or served. OpenRouter omits undeclared operator fields; OpenModels rejects missing required fields with HTTP 400. Values are never invented or zero-filled; bad files or active aliases fail before GPU load. See docs/SERVING.md "Gateway listing surface" |
| `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER` | — | REMOVED from the stock binary (engine-billing-extraction-20260829; only the engine is open). The request/cost ledger is a deployment-binary surface: wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the vars it consumes. The stock server FAILS STARTUP if this is set — set-but-unread configuration never fails open. |
| `MEMRA_CAPTURE_DIR` | — | REMOVED from the stock binary (engine-billing-extraction-20260829; only the engine is open). The per-tenant capture is a deployment-binary surface: wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the vars it consumes. The stock server FAILS STARTUP if this is set — set-but-unread configuration never fails open. |
| `MEMRA_TENANT_BUDGETS` | — | REMOVED from the stock binary (engine-billing-extraction-20260829; only the engine is open). The prepaid budgets is a deployment-binary surface: wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the vars it consumes. The stock server FAILS STARTUP if this is set — set-but-unread configuration never fails open. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_ADDR` | — | REMOVED from the stock binary (engine-billing-extraction-20260829; only the engine is open). The provisioning listener is a deployment-binary surface: wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the vars it consumes. The stock server FAILS STARTUP if this is set — set-but-unread configuration never fails open. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE` | — | REMOVED from the stock binary (engine-billing-extraction-20260829; only the engine is open). The provisioning bearer is a deployment-binary surface: wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the vars it consumes. The stock server FAILS STARTUP if this is set — set-but-unread configuration never fails open. |
| `MEMRA_ADDR` | `127.0.0.1:8080` | listen address. A non-loopback address is a startup FATAL unless `MEMRA_API_KEY` or `MEMRA_API_KEYS` is configured; validation happens before model/GPU initialization |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_OPEN_BIND` | off | `1` explicitly permits a no-key non-loopback development bind. Completion routes are open in that shape, but `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics` still require `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`; never use this as a production auth substitute |
| `MEMRA_API_KEY` | none | single static bearer key (the daily driver); setting it also defaults `MEMRA_COMPAT=openai`. Authenticates as tenant `default` and, without `MEMRA_API_KEYS` or `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`, authorizes completion and both legacy metrics views. Composes with `MEMRA_API_KEYS` — both may be live at once, with the keyring deployment's tenant-only metrics policy applying to every completion credential |
| `MEMRA_API_KEYS` | none | multi-key tenant keyring (lane/api-keys, 2026-08-05): a `keys.toml` path (entries store SHA-256 hex + tenant + lane class + optional `rate_limit`; hot-reloads on mtime, ≤2s poll; atomic revoke rewrites prevent readers from observing a truncated ring) or an inline `tenant:sha256hex[:lane]` list. A file-backed ring is boot-fatal unless it is one private regular non-symlink file (0600/0640 class), owned by the service uid, with one hard link and size ≤8 MiB; every hot reload and CLI mutation revalidates the same class. Key → tenant identity: PC-ISO cache namespace becomes `t:<tenant>␟<cache_salt>` (a tenant's keys share cache; tenants never cross), batch-class keys default to harvest and are 403'd off `x-lane: interactive`, per-key `rate_limit` = min(override, lane cap) in the X-RateLimit headers, and `[meter]` admit lines carry the tenant id. Without `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`, a key authorizes `/metrics` but receives only its own tenant rows; process-wide counters and `/yield/metrics` are withheld. Configure the dedicated metrics token for the full operator surface; completion keys then receive 403 on both routes. Manage with `memra-server --gen-key <tenant> [--lane batch] [--rate-limit N]` / `--revoke-key <prefix>` (docs/SERVING.md "API keys") |
| `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN` | none | exclusive `Authorization: Bearer <token>` credential for the process-wide `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics` operator surfaces; when configured, completion API keys receive 403 on both routes and the scrape token sees all tenant rows. A multi-key `MEMRA_API_KEYS` deployment defaults to tenant-only `/metrics` and withholds `/yield/metrics` until this token is configured. It does not authorize completion routes. Metrics require a bearer whenever any API-key source or this token is configured, or whenever `MEMRA_ADDR` is non-loopback. Only a no-key loopback development server exposes metrics without auth |
| `MEMRA_COMPAT` | native (`openai` when API_KEY set) | response shape: `openai` = OpenAI completions SSE |
| `MEMRA_CTX` | 8192 | fallback session-cache context when a request omits both `max_ctx` and a finite `max_tokens`. An explicit `max_ctx` is authoritative; without it, a finite request uses `prompt_tokens + max_tokens + 8`. KV @8192 ≈ 119MB/session on the 9B. Machine envelope: on the 24GB 5090 under the q8rp-mirror config, c=32 ctx-8192 sessions OOM (captured `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`; ~27 admit) — set the fallback to the unbounded-request workload, 2048 clears the same cell at 502 tok/s (`research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/`) |
| `MEMRA_DRAIN_S` | 30 | graceful-drain deadline (serve-tail lane, 2026-08-04): on SIGTERM the server 503s new completion requests (`code: draining`, `Retry-After` = this value clamped to 60 s, with the matching `retry-after-ms`), in-flight requests (streams included) finish up to this many seconds, then the process exits 0. During the drain `/health` stays **200** with `status:"draining"` (a drain is a healthy, deliberate shutdown — 503 there would invite a supervisor to SIGKILL a process mid-stream) while `/readyz` answers **503**, which is what takes the instance out of rotation. Keep this below `TimeoutStopSec` in the systemd unit; the server also sends `EXTEND_TIMEOUT_USEC` under `Type=notify` |
| `MEMRA_RL_RESET_S` | 2 | static `X-RateLimit-Reset` fallback (seconds) when all slots are taken and the live meter has no signal yet; with signal, Reset = mean tokens/request x p50 step latency (honestly coarse) |
| `MEMRA_MAX_QUEUE_DEPTH` | 4 × the selected lane cap | Absolute per-lane queue bound — the backpressure BACKSTOP (lane/deadline-billing, 2026-08-23; lane-scoped hard reservations in security batch 2). The interactive lane admits up to `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` and queues beyond it; judge/harvest normally shed at their worker policy, while this bound also keeps their handler/DSV4 ingress queues finite. Past a lane's bound, a new request is SHED with `429` + `Retry-After` (`code: shed_queue`, ledger outcome `shed_queue`, **never billed**) instead of joining a queue it cannot be served from. Reservations are counted separately per lane, so subordinate traffic cannot consume interactive queue capacity. The estimated-wait-vs-`timeout_ms` test remains interactive-only and usually sheds first there. An already-admitted request is never shed. Raise it for bursty clients that would rather wait than retry; lower it to fail fast. |
| `MEMRA_QUEUE_WAIT_CEILING_S` | **0 = OFF by design** | QUEUE-WAIT CEILING for the interactive lane (q5-queue-wait-shed, darklanes issue #5; darklanes `research/stress-campaign-20260901/RESULTS.md` finding 3). Set to `N > 0`, an interactive request whose estimated queue wait exceeds `N` seconds is refused at submission with the shed contract's `429` (`code: shed_queue_wait`, ledger outcome `shed_queue_wait`, **never billed**, `Retry-After` = the estimate clamped 1..=60 s with the matching `retry-after-ms`, X-RateLimit trio attached). WHY: the interactive lane queues beyond `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`, and the existing deadline test (`shed_deadline`) judges only the caller's OWN `timeout_ms`, so a patient caller in a burst discovered the wait by enduring it: prod measured 133-137 s of pre-header silence with not one 429 (the stress campaign's finding 3). This arm judges the same wait estimate (mean tokens/request x p50 step latency, scaled by queued waves: the X-RateLimit-Reset machinery) against an operator ceiling instead of the caller's deadline. Placement and scope: evaluated only for a request that actually waits (a free slot on an empty lane admits immediately, estimate not applied), interactive lane only (judge/harvest shed at their worker policy), AFTER the existing `shed_queue` and `shed_deadline` arms so those answer first and unchanged, and never against an already-admitted request. DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-for-byte today's behavior (silent FIFO wait up to the caller's deadline; the ceiling arm never runs); ON converts projected waits past the ceiling into an honest, retryable `429`, but is unmeasured on serving hardware, so the default flip waits on a bench replay of the stress-campaign burst shape plus the owner's ruling on the ceiling value. Rollback seam: unset (or `0`). Receipts: darklanes issue #5, darklanes `research/stress-campaign-20260901/RESULTS.md` finding 3; unit cells `a_saturated_queue_with_free_http_slots_queues_silently_without_a_ceiling` (the flag-off contract), `the_queue_wait_ceiling_sheds_with_429_retry_after_and_the_ratelimit_trio`, `the_queue_wait_ceiling_admits_under_it_and_never_touches_dark_lanes`, `the_queue_wait_ceiling_leaves_the_existing_shed_arms_first_and_unchanged`, and the wiring cell `the_queue_wait_ceiling_is_wired_through_the_production_wrapper`. |
| `MEMRA_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` | unset (= the 90,000 ms platform ceiling) | MEASUREMENT-CELL override of the `timeout_ms` ceiling AND default (`parse_timeout_ms`, all four surfaces; read once per process). **The 90 s ceiling is a platform fact of the fronted product route** (Cloudflare 524 at ~100 s time-to-headers), so a raised value is only honest on a direct-to-server connection and MUST NOT be set on a customer-facing deploy: it would promise a deadline the proxy upstream cannot keep, and it also stretches the estimated-wait shed test that protects the interactive queue. It exists for exactly one shape: an offline capacity/correctness cell whose single prime legitimately runs past 90 s end to end (born lane/glm53-1m-demo 2026-08-29, where a monolithic ~1M-token GLM-5.3-Flash prime runs for hours and the 90 s first-token deadline would cancel every arm of the demonstration; receipts in `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/1m-demo-20260829/`). Default OFF by construction: unset, unparseable, or below `TIMEOUT_MS_MIN` keeps the shipped constants byte-for-byte. When set, the request default follows the new maximum (the documented "default equals the maximum" contract), and an explicit `timeout_ms` is validated against the raised range. ROLLBACK SEAM: unset it. |
| `MEMRA_KV_REUSE` | on | `0` disables the KV prefix-reuse pool (session-gate validated; 42.6x turn-start at 40k) |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL` | 2 | per-`(model, cache namespace)` LRU cap, applied independently to the continuation, speculative (MTP), and DFlash2/dspark whole-session pools; see [PC-ISO](SERVING.md#cache-salt-isolation). The dspark pool clamps EOS and `max_tokens` rounds to the public committed prefix before park; a max-token terminal resumes only when the next request contributes a non-empty suffix. A request whose ctx cap outgrew the parked allocation still serves cold. Before the process ceiling, one populated tier could retain roughly `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL × distinct live cache_salt namespaces × parked-entry bytes`: ~119 MB/entry at ctx 8192 on the 9B, but multi-GB spec entries at long context. At the default, sequential workloads of >2 sessions in one namespace hit the park-evicts-next-entry cascade (0/N resumes; pinned 2026-07-31); raise only with a matching global VRAM budget. `0` = park nothing (pooling off). |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL_GLOBAL_CAP` | 16 | process-wide maximum parked entries across every model, cache namespace, and all three whole-session pools: continuation, MTP-spec, and DFlash2/dspark. Before each park that would exceed it, the worker drops the globally oldest entry across all three pools; failure to produce a slot refuses the insertion, and the per-namespace cap above still applies. Size as `floor(parked VRAM budget / worst-case parked-entry bytes)` and lower it for long-context/multi-GB entries. `0` = park nothing process-wide. Default 16 preserves the measured [27bab two-namespace Q27 shape](../research/27bab-20260810/RESULTS.md#vram-ledger): 16 spec entries, zero OOM parks, 27.34 GB driver-free; the salt-per-turn control reached 76 entries and a captured CUDA OOM before this server-side ceiling existed. |
| `MEMRA_KV_PARK_COMPACT` | **0 = OFF by design** | CONTINUATION-POOL PARK COMPACTION (lane/kv-tenancy-compaction-20260831, tiering spec Arc C1). `1` = at PLAIN-pool park time the retiring session's `Cache` is compacted from its ladder cap (`cache.max_ctx`, up to the full server context: a 6k-token session can park ~1 GB on a 262k-ctx deployment because cap = max_ctx) to exactly its committed length: allocate a fed-length cache, D2D-copy the live rows through the same snapshot + checkpoint-restore machinery the plain-affinity grow path uses, free the big allocation on drop. Resume is the mirror image: the exact-extension probe admits a compacted entry (`plain_resume_cap_admits`) and the admit path re-allocates at the REQUEST's own charged ctx cap and restores the parked rows before any suffix primes; any failure drops the entry and serves cold (the plain-affinity failure contract). NAMED refusals park the original cache unchanged, compaction is an optimization and never costs a park: SWA-ring caches (ring-aware compaction is a named seam, not v1; the step37 shape), distributed TP KV mirrors, `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` (the snapshot would retain peer copies), `pos != fed` (nothing provably safe to copy), already-tight caches. SPEC/DSPARK pools are OUT OF SCOPE by design: they park live engine sessions (draft scratch, captured graphs, sampler state), not a lone `Cache` (spec Arc C scoping). Default OFF because the mechanism is unmeasured on serving hardware: the resume byte-identity gate (compacted-park vs plain-park, both resume shapes), the step-OOM adjacency replay (the multi-active OOM-park incident is adjacent code), and the park-time copy-cost receipt are pod-battery cells, PENDING (`research/kv-tenancy-20260831/REPORT.md`). OFF is byte-identical to today by construction: neither the park nor the probe path changes engage (the capacity predicate degenerates to the legacy `cap >= ctx_cap` comparison). Receipts when armed: `[kv-reuse] park-compact:` / `park-compact grow:` / `park-compact declined` log lines with row counts and copy wall-time. |
| `MEMRA_AFFINITY` | on | `0` disables session-affinity resume (docs/SERVING.md "Session affinity"): a conversation whose history the client REWROTE (think-strip) no longer resumes its parked session and re-primes in full — the pre-2026-08-05 behavior. Rollback seam / exactness A/B arm, not a tuning knob; prefer it over `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL=0` for that comparison, since killing the pool also kills the token/text-prefix resumes. Receipts `research/session-affinity-20260805/` |
> **Qwen35-MoE exactness repair (2026-08-14):** verification now uses the live serving numeric
> class, and every accepted round rebuilds committed cache state through sequential serving steps.
> The production IQ4_XS long-prompt gate is bit-identical at verifier widths T=1..4 for 128 rounds,
> K=1..8 sessions match plain for all 128 generated tokens, and the OpenAI serving envelope matches
> with spec forced ON/OFF. This correctness reference path is intentionally conservative; benchmark
> it before making any workload-specific performance claim.
| `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC` | on (GGUF and dir ckpts) | `0` disables the spec-decode serve path (greedy + MTP-head requests). **PRODUCTION POSTURE IS `0` ON SHARED-PREFIX SERVING SHAPES, measured 2026-08-13** — spec is numerically EXACT (content+reasoning SHA-256 identical to spec-off) but it FORFEITS the cross-request prefix cache, and on a cache-carried shape the cache is worth far more than the decode gain. Same box, same fresh cache, sold shape (4,860-token prompt + 60 out, 90% shared prefix): spec-off c=16 = 8.50 req/s at p50 1.87 s with 99.5% cache hit, spec-on c=16 = 2.14 req/s at p50 7.40 s with 18.4% hit — a **4x loss** (`research/canonflip-20260813/`, commit 753978e96). The CLI picture is the opposite and is what misleads: `run-spec` shows 1.51-1.70x vs plain at K=4..8 with 51.8-80.0% acceptance, because it has no cross-request cache to give up. So keep spec for cache-POOR shapes (cold/unique prompts) and keep it OFF where a shared prefix carries the prefill; do not re-enable it from the CLI numbers alone. Q35-MoE's exact serving path currently replays accepted bursts sequentially; constrained Q35 requests therefore fail closed to the exact plain constrained path because replay cannot commit a grammar-masked bonus. A cache-safe spec design is open work (see the prefix-cache rows and the spec_pool metrics). The 2026-08-03 dir-checkpoint quarantine is LIFTED (#68 closed 2026-08-04): the divergence was the per-session persistent draft graph replaying with dangling pool addresses (capture transients not retained + `fa_part_pool` freeing grown-past buffers the capture baked) — never ST-specific (reproduced on GGUF session bursts at n>=600). Fixed via capture-retain keepers on `DraftGraphCtx` + retire-on-grow for the fa partials pool; gate: `tools/serve-st-gate.sh` item 4 pins the DEFAULT (spec-on) server against the tokenwise **serve** oracle — `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0 MEMRA_SERVE_BATCH=0` on the same worker, 400-token window, prefix-tolerant for burst overshoot. The comparator is deliberately the serve oracle and not the `run-gen` CLI: both the batched-plain path and the CLI carry their own accepted near-tie FP classes at long windows (item 3 is the CLI comparison, at a 64-token window). Do not restate item 4 as "token-identical to the CLI oracle". Receipts `research/fp8ship-20260804/RESULTS.md`. **The PP-2 quarantine is LIFTED (#87 closed 2026-08-08)**: the sticky `CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS` was the ppN reverse-publication hole, fixed by `PpNRt::fence_stages_behind` at all three ppN bodies + stage-cache admission, with sentinel traps as containment. Crash gate: c=2 8/8, c=4 100/100, c=8 104/104; run-spec K=1..8 PASS; ppspec bit-identity 0 failing arms (`research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`). The later v0.72 head-affinity fix made both PP-2 placement orders the same 111-112 tok/s spec class; correctness is live, but the placement-aware spec gate now defaults PP-2 admission OFF because plain batched serving wins every measured c=1/2/4 cell. `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` still forces spec for rollback and the #87 crash gate. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` | on, placement-aware | `0` is the rollback seam: always-spec at every concurrency and placement. With the gate on, single-card keeps the measured scheduler from task #89: admit spec while `active+1 <= LOW=2`, demote live greedy spec sessions at `active >= HIGH=4`, and hand `(cache, next_pred)` to batched decode after flushing carried pending. Current-train q9 re-sweep: spec ON/OFF = 374.8/224.5 at c=1, 374.5/347.5 at c=2, 377.3/617.1 at c=4 (N=3; c=2 ranges do not overlap), so the crossover still lies between 2 and 4. Sharded cross-device PP-2 defaults to LOW=0/HIGH=1 (never admit spec): inherited q9 = 112 vs 223/340/593 at c=1/2/4; current step35 = 35.9/36.2/36.7 vs 85.7/101.6/121.7 (N=3). Greedy demotion exactness remains the task #89 proof; sampled and constrained sessions are excluded from demotion. Receipts `research/spec-gate-20260806/`, `research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`, `research/specplace-20260808/`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_LOW` / `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_HIGH` | single/non-PP2: 2 / 4; sharded PP-2: 0 / 1 | Admit-spec ceiling and demote floor. Explicit values override the placement defaults. The single-card 2/4 pair is the last measured WIN rung and first measured LOSS rung; `act == 3` is the hysteresis band, and one-way demotion bounds switches to O(load crossings). PP-2's 0/1 pair expresses the measured all-OFF verdict; LOW=0 admits no spec session and avoids charging the spec transient admission reserve. Set LOW=1/HIGH=2 only to test a c=1-only PP-2 policy; it is not the default because c=1 lost on both q9 and step35. `HIGH <= LOW` is clamped to `LOW+1` with a loud warning. |
| `MEMRA_OPTI_CONTROLLER_Q` | absent (**NO-GO**) | Fresh-process-only diagnostic door for the PP-2 depth-1 optimistic controller; setting a finite threshold in inclusive `[0,1]` arms it globally for that worker, while invalid values fail startup. Keep absent in production: every corrected q arm lost to serial, the merged seam, and plain because even rejected opportunities pay an online shadow-draft step. Revival requires all four gates in [opti2 RESULTS](../research/opti2-20260811/RESULTS.md#what-would-change-the-verdict): a trained/calibrated prefix-survival selector frozen from non-public retained traces; a low-confidence path byte- and performance-equivalent to plain with no shadow step; a retained segment whose conservative lower bound clears the re-solved economic threshold; then interleaved N=5 admitted-segment win plus zero low-q regression and the full 2x PRO 6000 pre-release battery. |
| `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` | 64 | max concurrently-active INTERACTIVE sessions under batched scheduling; admits beyond it queue FIFO. NOT a capacity guarantee: the VRAM-aware admission gate is the binding cap on real cards, and it can 429-reject a request that has no attainable headroom even with zero peers (pre-header, `[admit-oom] VRAM reject`). "Queue FIFO, never rejected" was the small-model c=64 story; the step37 capacity receipts falsified it as a general claim (the under-charged gate admitted past the card and requests died instead of queueing) — the boot-calibrated transient floor + per-session draft-state charge (lane/step37-vram-admission-20260830, `research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md`) are what make defer-and-queue actually hold. Legacy round-robin mode (`MEMRA_SERVE_BATCH=0`) uses the fixed MAX_ACTIVE=4 bound instead. Judge/harvest lanes cap separately (`MEMRA_LANE_MAX_*`) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_OOM_RETRIES` | 3 | step-time CUDA-OOM PARK budget per request (lane/admit-oom, 2026-08-06). A spec-burst step that OOMs despite admission rebuilds its request and re-queues at the FRONT instead of erroring the stream; the retire sweep that follows is what frees the VRAM. Parks only a session that has emitted NOTHING (restarting a streamed session would replay its prefix to the client) and only on a QUOTED CUDA OOM; anything else reports honestly and logs why it was not parked. Parking costs a re-prime; the SINGLE-active park is pure latency, but the claim that parking was categorically harmless was falsified by the step37 capacity receipts: MULTI-active parks segfaulted the GPU worker in libcuda (silent, dmesg-only, fleet-fatal) until lane/step37-vram-admission-20260830 fenced the teardown (device-synchronized drops, no pending-carry flush on a full card, no pool-park of OOM'd KV, post-sweep pool trim) — receipts `research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md`. A park REPLAY additionally serves capture-disabled (fail-safe: the capture appetite is part of what drove the OOM). `0` = rollback seam (pre-fix kill-on-OOM). Receipted load-bearing on its own: at a deliberately-broken reserve it salvaged 11/64 where the pre-fix path lost 64/64 (`research/admit-oom-20260806/`) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_RESERVE_MB` | unset (1536) | **teeth/diagnostics door, not a tuning knob** — overrides the spec-session admission transient reserve. The admission gate charges `SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE` only when the current placement policy can actually admit spec; PP-2's default LOW=0 is plain-only and keeps the legacy `cost` headroom term.
