That 1.5 GiB floor closed the single-card c=64 red (0/64 well-formed → 64/64). `tools/serve-stress-gate.sh --teeth` can force it tiny (16MB) and prove the gate still catches a broken cost model — a gate only ever observed passing proves nothing. Warns loudly when set. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREFILL_WORKSPACE` | **on** (`0` = pre-lane estimate) | Workspace-aware admission for HYPER-TRUNK (mHC / glm5_next) models (lane/glm5-gpf-workspace, 2026-08-30). `AdmissionCostModel` adds the engine-published prefill-workspace formula (`HybridModel::hyper_prime_workspace_shape`) to each request's charge, keyed on the request's PROMPT rows (never `ctx_cap`: a `max_tokens`-omitted request carries the whole server window there, and the workspace is a function of what the prime walks — the vendor-default 541-token prompt on a 262k window stays admissible by test): the chunk-bounded per-call transients (`chunk_token_bytes x hyper_prime_call_rows(prompt)` — stream state + ppN boundary pair, pre/norm transients, grouped-MoE staging `U*(10H+14F)+4H`, MLA q/attn planes, k-pool idx plane, prime tail), the prompt-coupled k-pool score plane (`call_rows x prompt/P x 4` — the allocation the 3-card 1M prime died on at 97.2 GiB), and the prompt-long returned `hiddens` stack (`H x 4`/token, the MTP-spec `prompt_h` / embed-capture input); the context planes keep charging `ctx_cap` in `context_bytes`, which IS the session's eager latent allocation. In the same lane, `cache_bytes_per_token_for_plan` (memra-kv) charges `StatePlan::LatentKvCache` layers (`width x 4` latent row + `index_head_dim x 4 / pool` resident key; the flat indexer plane only under `MEMRA_DSA_INDEX_RING=0`), mirroring `Cache::new_inner` — that half is an accounting FIX, not this flag's scope, and has no seam. WHY DEFAULT ON, with receipts: the 262k 2-card cell (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/262k-2card-20260830/LANE.md`) banked glm5 admission at literally `0 B/token x ctx + 155MB fixed` — every depth admitted, the box walling at ~8k prompt tokens (~0.8 MiB/token/card measured slope, vramwatch.csv) with a mid-stream `class=Overloaded` engine OOM as the only failure surface, and `observe()` structurally cannot learn the gap (it measures the admit-time delta; the workspace grows at PRIME time). BOTH ARMS: ON = an over-budget request takes the existing refusal shape (`[admit-oom] VRAM reject ... HTTP 429`) BEFORE prime, in-budget requests unchanged (the 7,108-token stable rung stays admissible by the same arithmetic — `hyper_prefill_workspace_makes_admission_see_the_262k_wall`, red arm included); OFF = the pre-lane estimate everywhere, byte-identical for every family. Non-hyper models publish no shape and are unaffected in BOTH arms. The formula is validated against the measured slope, conservative side up (~1.02 MiB/chunk-token formula vs ~0.8 MiB/token/card measured); gates: `glm5_admission_cost` (CPU, formula + chunk-bound identities on the mini glm5 plan), worker test above, and the ppN chunk-walk exactness arms in this lane's receipts (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/gpf-workspace-20260830/`). |
| `MEMRA_CI_STRESS` | on | `0` skips the c=64 serve-stress gate in `tools/local-ci.sh` (`tools/serve-stress-gate.sh`: 64 staggered streaming clients, asserts all complete + well-formed + worker alive + clean log). In-battery since the admission fix; it was deliberately left unwired while red |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` | 32 | tokens per spec burst — round-robin/streaming latency vs per-burst fixed cost (pending-flush + init-feed amortization). The 2026-07-06 "throughput-neutral" null was pre-persistent-draft-graph; re-measured 2026-08-05 on BOTH tiers: `128` is the throughput optimum — 5090/82 SM +7% c=1 (512-tok), +6.9% c=8, +9.2% on the 9B, curve turns DOWN at 256; PRO 6000/188 SM +7.5% (B64 flat there, not here). Byte-identical greedy both rigs. The old flip-blocker — ONE SSE event per burst (at 128: 2 chunks/1.15s first text vs 8 chunks/0.41s at 32) — is FIXED (sse-cadence, 2026-08-05): the worker flushes text per spec-round commit, so first text is ~0.12s and cadence ~27ms at ANY burst size for a solo stream. The SECOND flip-blocker — burst size set round-robin admission latency (contended first-text 0.57s at B32 vs 1.67s at B128) — is also FIXED (admission yield, 2026-08-06): a pending admit ends the in-flight burst at the round boundary and cold sessions burst first, so contended first-text is 0.123s(B32)/0.152s(B128), the solo class at any burst. Kept at 32 by the strict flip criterion: B128 contended is one round-cadence quantum (29ms) behind B32 while buying +8.4%(c=1)/+8.5%(c=8) — a live owner call, no longer a cliff. Set `128` on throughput-tier serve configs (c≥2 batch, judge/harvest, non-streaming API); leave default for the daily pill. **Not** the lever for the mixed-tick penalty: bounding the burst to recover the batched rows' latency was measured and REFUTED (lane/spec-gate, 2026-08-07). In an un-demoted mixed tick at c=6, `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST=4` leaves stream p95 at 11.075 s against the default-32 arm's 11.243 s — inside per-rep spread — and costs 1.4% aggregate throughput. The tick shape says why: mixed ticks go 17 → 109, i.e. the same total serial work sliced thinner, because phase (a) still runs every spec session's rounds before phase (c) is reached. Demotion (`MEMRA_SPEC_GATE`) is the fix that works there (p95 → 7.249 s). Receipts: `research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/`, `research/sse-cadence-20260805/`, `research/admission-20260806/`, `research/spec-gate-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SSE_PER_BURST` | off | `1` restores pre-2026-08-05 per-burst SSE emission (one `Event::Token` per spec burst) — rollback seam for the round-cadence streaming fix. Content is byte-identical either way (same detokenize-tail + utf8_delta cursor; gate receipts `research/sse-cadence-20260805/logs/ident-*`); only chunk boundaries change. Throughput parity measured c=1 and c=8 (fix-on 100.1/98.6 vs fix-off 100.0/98.4 agg tok/s, B128 q27) |
| `MEMRA_SSE_COALESCE` | off / unwired | No runtime reader exists on current main: SSE keeps one encoded event per body write. The 8-frame / 16 KiB / 32 ms coalescing experiment remained in scratch work and is not a serving default. Do not wire or default it on without a real SSE-client gate reporting ITL p50/p95/p99 (including `[DONE]`) in addition to TTFB and aggregate throughput. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_YIELD` | on | `0` restores pre-2026-08-06 admission behavior — rollback seam for the admission-latency fix (both pieces: full-burst holds AND index-order spec stepping). Default-on: a request arriving mid-burst ends the in-flight spec burst at the next round boundary (the `PENDING_ADMITS` gauge, polled by the sse-cadence round hook) and not-yet-emitted sessions burst before mid-generation peers, so contended first-text no longer scales with `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` (B128: 1.60s → 0.152s; B32: 0.54s → 0.123s — the solo class). Content byte-identical on/off, solo AND contended, background response included. Costs at c=8 saturation: −3.4% agg tok/s for 3.8x better p50 (newcomer-first vs lockstep-fair; p95 tail pays). c=1 parity. Set `0` on pure-batch tiers that want lockstep fairness back. Receipts `research/admission-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SLO_P99_MS` | 50 | x-lane QoS gate (lane/qos-p95, 2026-08-02 — the dl-metering QoS extraction): the interactive decode-step p99 SLO. Requests tag a class via the `x-lane` header (`interactive`\|`judge`\|`harvest`; absent = interactive — naked traffic is unaffected). Interactive always admits; judge/harvest SHED at admission (HTTP 429 + Retry-After) when measured interactive step p99 crosses `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` (1.00) / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` (0.90) of the SLO, and when a starvation sentinel fires. Interactive decode rows sort first; dark prefill runs after decode inside SLO headroom only. This is also the multi-tenant REQUEST-latency dial: fleet QoS@8 measured p50/p95 = 2.39/3.69s at 50ms, 2.06/3.56s at 35ms, 1.64/2.16s at 25ms (bulk pays: 2214 → 1592 → 818 tok/s — `research/qos-p95-20260802/`) |
| `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` | 1.00 / 0.90 | per-lane shed thresholds as fractions of the SLO |
| `MEMRA_SHED_MIN_SAMPLES` | **32** | Minimum estimator-window samples before the p99 shed gate may fire; below it the window counts as cold (admit), exactly like the empty-window rule. Default ON by design with a receipt (2026-08-28, box10 orn): with n samples the window's "p99" selects index round(0.99·(n−1)) — the MAXIMUM for every n ≤ 50 — and on an idle box whose only interactive traffic was the watchdog's one-token probe (~5 samples per 30s window), the probe's own prime-carrying tick (~46–50ms vs the 45ms harvest threshold) shed 2 of the day's 4 real capture requests on an empty machine (`rate_limit_exceeded`, dur=0, ledger-receipted; retries succeeded once the 30s window aged out). 32 separates probe cardinality (~5–10 samples/window) from real load (hundreds/s: interactive records per-token). The `starved` sentinel needs no window and still sheds under genuine starvation regardless of this floor; real congestion past the floor still sheds (unit-tested both ways in memra-lanes). `0` disables the floor — the pre-2026-08-28 behavior |
| `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST` | 32 / 4 / 8 | per-lane resident session caps (interactive's applies to the sidecar shape; the serve path uses `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`) |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_HARVEST` | 1024 / 256 / 256 | per-tick prefill token budgets: concurrent interactive sessions use 1024 each; a naked sole fresh interactive request widens once to at most 8192 tokens so PP-2 keeps its measured eight-microbatch geometry in one outer prime call. Setting `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` explicitly is authoritative and disables that solo widening. Judge/harvest remain per-tick stall bounds (a 2048-tok judge chunk ≈ 230ms decode starvation; 256 ≈ 30ms) further capped by measured SLO headroom **FIX 2026-08-28 (cold-TTFT lane):** the widening had been unreachable for any cold request that armed a plain-affinity checkpoint (`ckpt_at`), because the freshness predicate vetoed on it. `ckpt_at` is a capture BOUNDARY that `prefill_tick_take` already stops the take on, and it is COLD-only, so the veto excluded exactly the sessions this widening exists for: a 1480-token cold request primed 1024 and walked 456 PROMPT tokens through `decode_step`. `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK=8192` had been "working around" it only by taking the operator early-return. |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_WIDEN_PROBE` | **off** | `1`: one stderr `[widen-probe]` line per interactive prefill tick naming every input of the solo-widening decision (`configured`, `explicit`, `sole_unfinished`, `fresh`, and the four session fields behind `fresh`, plus `queued -> budget`). DIAGNOSTIC ONLY: it reads state and prints, it never changes a budget, a take, or a byte. DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (new-flag law): it prints per tick per session, so on a busy server it is log noise, and nothing in serving needs it. Rollback seam: unset the var (no restart-only state, read per tick). WHY IT EXISTS: the widening guard is a conjunction and a false term is invisible in every serving metric -- this probe is what named `ckpt_at` as the term costing 76% of a 3.9 s cold TTFT. Polarity trap: the `ckpt=`/`snapshot=` fields print `is_none()`, so `ckpt=false` means a checkpoint IS armed, which since the fix is a passing shape. Receipts: `lane/step37-mtp-masked-vocab-20260825` cold-TTFT commit The line also carries `rule=ckpt-bounds-take-v2`, a BUILD FINGERPRINT: `strings memra-server | grep -c ckpt-bounds-take-v2` answers 1 only for a binary carrying the 2026-08-28 freshness fix, so a measurement can prove which predicate it measured instead of trusting a build log. |
| `MEMRA_LANE_WINDOW_S` | 30 | interactive step-p99 estimator window |
| `MEMRA_HEALTH_STALL_S` | 120 | how long a BUSY worker may go without a heartbeat before `/health` (== `/livez`) reports 503 (lane/serve-hardening, 2026-08-06). NOT arbitrary: a naked sole fresh interactive request may prime up to 8192 tokens in one call, while concurrent `prefill_tick` work remains capped at `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` (1024) tokens per active session and loops every active session inside ONE iteration. At `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`=64 that concurrent bound is 65,536 primed tokens in one pass ≈ 55 s at the measured 1.2k tok/s 4k-prefill rate; 120 s is that with ~2.2x margin, and the same number `tools/serve-fleet.sh` uses for `LOAD_GRACE`. An IDLE worker is healthy at any beat age (it blocks in `rx.recv()` and legitimately stamps nothing). `/health` publishes `worker.tick_max_ms` — the longest scheduler iteration this process actually observed — so raising this is a measured decision, not a guess. Keep it BELOW `WatchdogSec` in `deploy/systemd/memra-server.service` DEPLOYMENT NOTE (glm5_next 3-card PP3, darklanes glm53 stack, 2026-09-01): a hyper-connections prime is MONOLITHIC per session (no 1024-token prefill_tick cap applies), so under waves of 8-22 admitted sessions carrying 20-88k-token prompts the worker went >120 s without a beat while BUSY AND PROGRESSING, `/health` answered 503 `unhealthy` for three 10 s guard ticks, and serve-guard SIGTERMed a server with 22 requests in flight (darklanes `research/glm5-serving-launch-20260901/soak-20260901/RESULT.md`, RED finding 1). That deployment pins **480** (the 229,376-token admission ceiling at ~2k tok/s prefill, stacked for several concurrent ceilings with this row's ~2x margin) and its guard's LOAD_GRACE follows; the STATED COST is that a genuinely hung worker is detected in ~8 min instead of ~2.5. The real fix is memra#50: health must distinguish busy-progressing from hung (a beat per prime chunk) and admission must cap prefill backlog against the first-token deadline; until it lands, a deployment whose prime is monolithic sizes this from its own admission ceiling and prefill rate, measured, and publishes `worker.tick_max_ms` from its stress arm as the receipt. THE THREE LEGS MOVE TOGETHER (health.rs: app threshold, bash supervisor grace, systemd `StartLimitIntervalSec`/`WatchdogSec` are ONE number): the glm53 deployment is a vast container with NO systemd unit (serve-guard.sh is the supervisor; its LOAD_GRACE=600 > 480), so `deploy/systemd/memra-server.service`'s `WatchdogSec=180` does not apply there; a systemd-supervised deployment that adopts a 480 s threshold MUST raise `WatchdogSec` above it (and size `StartLimitIntervalSec` for the longer restart cadence) in the same change, or the watchdog kills the busy worker the threshold was raised to protect |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_RESPAWN` | 1 | in-process respawn attempts after the GPU worker thread PANICS, before the process exits 70 for the supervisor to restart it whole. One by default: CUDA errors are sticky per process (after an OOM or an Xid the context is poisoned), so a respawn is a long shot worth exactly one try — it saves a ~120 s weight reload when it works, and a respawn loop against a poisoned context is a box that looks alive and serves nothing. `0` = never retry in-process |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH` | on | `0` disables the GPU-fault watcher (G24): the Xid tail (`/dev/kmsg`, falling back to `journalctl -k -f`; fatal classes 48/64/79/94/95/119/120 latch unhealthy, others count as warnings on `/health`) plus the periodic `nvidia-smi` ECC/row-remap probe. Diagnostics door — leave on in any deployment |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH_S` | 60 | GPU probe interval (seconds) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S` | 10 | hard deadline for one `nvidia-smi` probe, and THE alarm for the worst Blackwell wedge class: Xid 119/120 (GSP RPC timeout) emits nothing to the process and hangs the query tools themselves, so a probe that outlives this deadline is treated as a fatal GPU fault rather than waited on. The probe runs as a killed-on-deadline child on its own thread and health reads only atomics, so a hung `nvidia-smi` can never block a health answer |
| `MEMRA_PANIC_AFTER` | unset | **fault injection, never set in a deployment**: panic the GPU worker thread after this many completed requests, once per process. The only way to exercise the G5 supervision ladder (catch_unwind → `/health` 503 with the quoted payload → respawn → exit 70) against a REAL CUDA worker rather than a test fake — which matters because the first version of that supervisor deadlocked startup and only a live gate caught it. Receipts: `research/serve-hardening-20260806/probe-worker-death.sh` + `logs/worker-death.txt`. Costs one relaxed atomic load per completed request when unset |
| `MEMRA_VALLEY_S` | 2.0 | dead-darklane valley threshold (lane/darklane-training, 2026-08-07): how long the worker must be COMPLETELY idle (no active sessions, no queued admissions, no pending HTTP handoffs) before the box counts as "in a valley" and a background job may launch/resume. The signal itself is worker truth already there — `PHASE_IDLE` + heartbeat age + the `PENDING_ADMITS` gauge, zero new hot-path cost — published as `/metrics serve_idle_seconds` (0.0 the instant there is any work). Asymmetric on purpose: yield fires on the busy EDGE with no debounce; only the resume side waits the threshold |
| `MEMRA_BG_JOB` | unset | dead-darklane background job command (`sh -c`, own process group, PDEATHSIG=KILL). Runs ONLY in valleys — a lane class BELOW every serving lane (harvest is still an admitted request class; this is not a request at all). Yield = SIGSTOP to the group on the first sign of traffic, resume = SIGCONT next valley; measured request-fired-to-job-stopped 19.4 ms median / 23.3 ms max (N=5 — one 25 ms poll interval, bound 500 ms). Job exit 0 = complete (never relaunched), exit 75 = checkpointed (relaunches next valley), anything else = failed (never relaunched, loud). Unset = the runner does not exist and `/metrics` has no `bg` block — pre-lane payload byte-identical. Serve impact receipt: `research/darktrain-20260807/` (p95 within noise at the measured shape; see RESULTS) |
| `MEMRA_BG_POLL_MS` | 25 | bg runner poll interval — the detection half of the yield bound (full bound = poll + signal delivery; measured ms-class). Lower = tighter yield, more wakeups; 25 ms is ~1 decode step |
| `MEMRA_BG_YIELD_MODE` | stop | `stop` = SIGSTOP/SIGCONT (the memory — VRAM included — stays resident while stopped; budget for the LIFE of the job). `checkpoint` = the training-class seam: SIGUSR1 means "checkpoint to disk and exit 75", relaunch resumes from the job's own checkpoint next valley (at-least-once: a step may repeat, never be lost). Toy proof: `tools/bg-ckpt-counter.py` |
| `MEMRA_BG_CKPT_GRACE_MS` | 5000 | checkpoint-mode grace: a job still alive this long after SIGUSR1 is SIGKILLed (dirty preempt — resumes from its last on-disk write). The yield bound must hold even against a wedged job |
| `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB` | 0 | background job VRAM budget. 0 = CPU-only job, no GPU probe. >0: launch is REFUSED (state `refused_vram`, retried next valley) unless `min free across visible GPUs >= budget + MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB` (the same serve headroom the resident planner keeps — the job budget comes out of headroom the SERVER isn't using, never out of the server's own reserve). Min across GPUs, not sum: on a PP-2 pair both cards carry serve shards and the budget must fit the tightest. Fail-closed: unreadable `nvidia-smi` = refusal. The budget is exported to the job's env as `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB`; staying inside it at runtime is the JOB's contract (v1 has no cgroup-style VRAM enforcement), and a job that lies gets caught by the same VRAM-aware admission gate that defends serving from everything else |
| `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` | none (text-only) | qwen-class vision tower directory (the checkpoint's `outside.safetensors` with `model.visual.*`). The worker loads the tower at spawn iff this is set, and the HTTP layer accepts `image_url` parts under exactly the same condition; configured-but-unloadable is a boot PANIC (silently serving text-only would be dishonest). ~1.8 GB f32-resident (`memra-server/src/worker.rs`, `main.rs vision_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_VISION` | on when `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` set | `0` skips the tower even with the dir configured — the owner knob for VRAM-tight boxes. Image requests then 400 at the HTTP layer. The same 400 fires when the PLACEMENT is inadmissible (see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`): the tower loaded but the overlay cannot reach embedding intake, decided once at boot for every family (memra #25) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION` + `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ` | off | gemma-4 image serving: BOTH are required — `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION=1` arms the seam and `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ=<gemma4v mmproj path>` names the tower; unloadable is a boot PANIC. A deployment serves ONE vision family (one model per GPU), so this process-wide switch also decides which placeholder + prep image parts take (`main.rs gemma_vision_enabled()`). Image parts additionally pass the boot-time placement decision (`VISION_PLACEMENT_SERVING`, see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`) at the media part: armed on an inadmissible placement refuses each image part at the waist with a named 400, never mid-prefill; the switch itself stays keyed on these two knobs, so text rendering never moves with the placement (memra #25) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION` (+ optional `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION_DIR`) | **on** (auto-detect) | glm5_next (GLM-5.3-Flash) image serving. DEFAULT ON by owner order ("image should be default on, queue it", 2026-08-30; lane/glm5-vision-default-on): a glm5_next DIRECTORY checkpoint whose own safetensors index carries `model.visual.*` (probe tensor `model.visual.patch_embed.proj.weight`) loads its tower FROM ITSELF at boot — the mint ships all 347 tensors (MINT-BYTES twin, research/glm5-vision-20260830/LANE.md); absent tensors = text-only artifact, vision off, no flag needed. ARMS: `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION=0` = the ROLLBACK SEAM (tower not loaded, `image_url` refuses by name); `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION_DIR=<safetensors dir/shard>` overrides the tower source (split artifacts); detected-or-configured-but-unloadable is a boot PANIC, never a silent text-only downgrade. The HTTP intake keys on the worker's actual tower decision (`GLM5_VISION_SERVING`), not a raw env read. VRAM: measured device delta printed on the boot line (`[glm5-vision] tower loaded ... GiB device delta at load` — 2.12 GiB measured on the 5090 gate run 2026-08-30, f32-resident; the ~2.3 GB figure in older docs was the parameter-count estimate). RECEIPTS: tower vs upstream `memra-reference/tests/glm5_vision_upstream.rs` + `memra-engine/tests/glm5_vision_gpu.rs` (det112 merger 5.1e-7); 1-card box probe can't-hallucinate PASS, exact codes on BOTH the greedy and the vendor-default sampled shape (research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/acceptance-probe-20260830/vision-cell/, lane/glm5-card3-acceptance-probe); flip blockers closed same lane — text-only literal `<|image|>` injection (probe arms 20/22) now refuses at the tokenize waist on token ids (`worker.rs vision_special_guard_tests`, surface fixtures byte-identical), and the ppN overlay splice is stage-0-intake, BIT-IDENTICAL vs the substituted-token truth across n2/n3/n4 stages with a biting span-shift red arm (`glm5-hyper-ppn-gate` arm 5, research/glm5-vision-20260830/ppn-overlay-gate/). SERVING-SHAPE HISTORY (lane/glm53-vision-ppn, 2026-09-01): the 3-card ppN can't-hallucinate cell was run at the launch window and vision was UNSERVABLE on that shape in v0.123.0 — every image request 500'd on the old OVERLAY DEVICE LAW (`ptr::eq(stage-0 engine, primary)`), because the worker's primary engine follows the LAST pp stage, so the tower ran on dev2 while stage-0 intake owned dev0; the launch pinned `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION=0` and shipped facts text-only (darklanes research/glm5-serving-launch-20260901/window-20260901/, finding 7). The overlay arithmetic was never implicated: under `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` the same cell returned the exact codes, at ~3x decode cost. FIXED by publishing the overlay into the intake engine's context (`MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH` below). REMAINING PRECONDITION for customer exposure is unchanged in kind: the serving-shape cell must be re-run GREEN on the fixed binary (exact codes, named refusals, no decode tax, text-only byte identity with vision armed) before any product surface moves. Same one-vision-family-per-deployment law as the gemma seam; `video_url` refuses (census only). (`main.rs glm5_vision_enabled()`, `worker.rs` spawn, `vision_glm5.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH` | **auto** (publish only when the intake context differs) | Where a vision overlay's rows must LIVE, and whether they are republished to get there (lane/glm53-vision-ppn, 2026-09-01). A device pointer is meaningful in ONE CUDA context; `EmbedOverlay` records the context its rows live in and `splice_into` refuses a mismatch, for every arm. Under a per-stage-stream ppN split the splice runs on pp STAGE 0, whose engine is a different device from the primary whenever the placement is real (the worker's primary follows the LAST stage — `worker_device`, and that is load-bearing: the stage-0 pin was the v0.72 tag-blocker-2 regressor, 112.5 -> 17.5 tok/s), so the tower's rows are published into stage 0's context at construction (`vision_intake_engine` + `EmbedOverlay::new_published`). DEFAULT IS `auto` BY DESIGN, not by implementation order: `auto` is byte-identical to the pre-lane program on every shape that already worked (single device, door shut, `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` — same context, zero copy) and is the ONLY way the ppN serving shape serves images at all; the alternative default would be shipping a 500 for every image request. ARMS: `force` = always take the publication path — the one-card rig gate arm (`glm5-hyper-ppn-gate` 5d/5e, bit-identity vs the substituted-token truth, non-vacuity asserted against `vision::overlay_publications()`); `0`/`off` = ROLLBACK SEAM, the pre-lane program (no publication; a cross-context intake then refuses by name, and the only vision-capable shape is `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` at ~3x decode). An unrecognized value is a BOOT DEATH, not a first-overlay refusal: the door is resolved ONCE at startup, unconditionally, for every vision family (it used to be read per session inside `new_published` while only the glm5 branch validated anything, so a typo'd value on a gemma/qwen/step37 deployment booted clean and 500'd mid-prefill on the first image request). A mistyped correctness door must never resolve to a default, and must not wait for traffic to say so. The resolved value then feeds ONE placement decision for every loaded tower family (memra #25: `worker.rs vision_placement_admissible`, qwen, gemma4, glm5, step37, one `[<family>] overlay intake: ... servable=` line per loaded family; the glm5 line keeps its `glm5_models=` shape); `servable=false` publishes `VISION_PLACEMENT_SERVING=false` and every family's `image_url`/`video_url` PART refuses with the same named 400 at the media part (`lib.rs vision_placement_admits`) instead of 500ing mid-prefill; the family switches, and the content walkers they select, stay keyed on the operator knobs alone, so text-only prompt bytes are identical whatever the placement says. Before #25 only glm5 was placement-guarded, and step37 served vision live without it. COST: publication is a host bounce of `[rows, n_embd]` f32 ONCE PER SESSION, prefill only (~5 MiB for a 256-row image) against a tower that already host-bounces q/k/v and the merger input in every block; decode never touches an overlay, so there is no per-token cost. A peer D2D twin is a named follow-up, not a correctness question. RECEIPTS: rig arms 5d/5e + the 3-card serving-shape can't-hallucinate/no-decode-tax/text-identity battery in `research/glm53-vision-ppn-20260901/` (`vision.rs`, `hybrid_forward.rs vision_intake_engine`, `worker.rs build_vision_overlay`) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5V_GATE_RED` | unset | gate-harness knob, never a serving flag: `span-shift` makes `glm5-hyper-ppn-gate`'s OVERLAY TWIN arm shift every vision splice span +1 — the deliberately broken splice placement whose run MUST exit non-zero (the banked red arm, research/glm5-vision-20260830/ppn-overlay-gate/90-91-RED-*). Read only by the gate binary; the server never consults it |
| `MEMRA_STEP_VISION_DIR` | none (text-only) | step37 (Step-3.7-Flash) image serving: points at the serving artifact's OWN directory — the perception_encoder tower + `vit_large_projector` live unquantized (BF16) inside the NVFP4 checkpoint, routed by `model.safetensors.index.json`. Set => the worker loads the tower at spawn (~8 GB f32-resident) and the HTTP layer accepts `image_url` parts with the step placeholder expansion + the step template's text-separator law; unset (the default, by design: the tower ships gated on its parity + serve receipts in `research/step37-vision-20260830/`) => image requests 400 and text serving is byte-identical to before this seam existed. Configured-but-unloadable is a boot PANIC. Image parts additionally pass the boot-time placement decision (`VISION_PLACEMENT_SERVING`, see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`) at the media part, while the switch, and the step text walker (separator law) it selects, stay keyed on these knobs alone so text bytes never move with the placement: before memra #25 that decision was glm5-only, so a step37 box with a bad `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH` value or `=0` on a cross-context placement booted clean and 500'd on the first image prefill (`memra-server/src/worker.rs vision_placement_admissible`, `lib.rs step_vision_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_VISION` | on when `MEMRA_STEP_VISION_DIR` set | `0` skips the step tower even with the dir configured — the same VRAM-tight owner knob as `MEMRA_VISION`. Image requests then 400 at the HTTP layer. The same 400 fires when the PLACEMENT is inadmissible (see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`): the tower loaded but the overlay cannot reach embedding intake, decided once at boot for every family (memra #25) |
| `MEMRA_VIDEO_MAX_PIXELS` | 2097152 | serving cap on total video patches (`groups*gh*gw` across ALL sampled frames — the HF smart_resize budget covers `t_bar*h*w`). Deliberately under the HF default because `sdpa_naive` keys the whole span in shared memory (`memra-engine/src/vision_pre.rs video_max_pixels()`). Frame floor/caps are compile-time (`VID_MIN_PIXELS` 4096, `VID_MAX_FRAMES` 32) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC` | unset = K=5 with `MEMRA_DRAFT`, else off | gemma4 SERVED spec depth (`memra-server/src/worker.rs gemma4_spec_k_env()`): unset + a drafter = armed at the shipping K=5; unset without a drafter = off; `0` = the eager/plain kill switch; explicit K>=1 = that depth and REQUIRES `MEMRA_DRAFT` (boot refuses loud). Any other value REFUSES LOUD — the mis-typed-seam law |

---

## 2. Machine-specific config

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PP_FP8` | off | `1` enables the cuBLASLt FP8-activation prefill GEMM on FP8-native (safetensors) checkpoints via a DUPLICATE resident e4m3 stash kept alongside the Q8_0 slab. +78–129% pp on rtx6000 (2026-07-08); local NV-27B pp1855 1341→1412 (+5.3%, 2026-07-09). Opt-in: costs resident-copy VRAM (`MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`). **Largely superseded for the per-tensor class** since `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` went default-on (2026-08-05): those tensors never surface as Q8_0, so the stash arm never fires and prefill rides the single resident copy with no budget. Still the route for the block-128 class stash |
| `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB` | 1536 | VRAM budget for the DUPLICATE resident FP8 weight copies the `MEMRA_PP_FP8` / `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` stash makes (rig-dependent; 2560 = +16.6% pp1845 on 24GB, 2026-07-08). Irrelevant for the per-tensor class under the default `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` (one copy, no stash — measured: native residency is 430 MiB *smaller* than the slab it replaces, not larger) |
| `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` | **on** | F8-E4M3-origin safetensors projections keep RAW e4m3 as the ONE resident copy (no Q8_0 re-encode): decode dequants e4m3 in-kernel (`qmatvec_e4m3_mmvq` + the batched/fused twins), prefill rides the FP8 GEMM on the same bytes. NEW NUMERIC CONFIG (checkpoint-native weight precision — the Q8_0 hop was the lossy one). `0` = rollback seam to the Q8_0 slab. **DEFAULT-FLIPPED 2026-08-05** (lane/fp8-decode-v1, receipts `research/fp8dec-20260805/`) on the 27B FP8-ST: decode **+2.58%** (38.75 → 39.75 tok/s, N=5 interleaved in one lock hold on one frozen binary, min(B) > max(A) so the distributions do not overlap) — split by the `MEMRA_E4M3_DUAL=0` arm into +1.47pp from the BYTES and +1.09pp from launch fusion; **430 MiB freed** at a measured byte ratio of exactly 1.06250 vs theory 34/32 = 1.06250 (matching to five decimals is itself the proof of single residency — contrast `MEMRA_PP_FP8`, whose stash keeps e4m3 *on top of* the slab and therefore needs a budget); teacher-forced exactness 2/128 near-tie flips with **lower** NLL on the slab arm's own greedy tape than the slab arm scores on it (0.220117 vs 0.222145); kernel-check ALL GREEN incl. 24 E4M3-FUSED cells; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS; serve-st-gate 0 failed (CLI-vs-server ids identical). SCOPE (**widened 2026-08-05**, lane/fp8-blk128-decode): this is now the shared rollback seam for BOTH e4m3 scale classes — the per-tensor scalar class AND the **block-128** class (`MEMRA_ST_E4M3_BLK` is the narrow per-class seam for single-variable A/B). Per-row F8 still takes the Q8_0 re-encode, and GGUF is untouched (`find_fp8_native` is None for GGUF sources) |
| `MEMRA_ST_E4M3_BLK` | **on** | Narrow per-class seam for the BLOCK-128 e4m3 residency arm (lane/fp8-blk128-decode 2026-08-05, receipts `research/fp8blk-20260805/`). `0` sends only the block-128 tensors back to the ARM B' Q8_0 slab while the per-tensor class keeps its shipped default — which is what makes a block-128 A/B single-variable. `MEMRA_ST_E4M3=0` reverts BOTH classes (the doctrine's one seam for all native e4m3 residency). WHY THE CLASS MATTERS: Qwen3.6-FP8 ships `weight_block_size [128,128]`, so before this arm the flagship FP8 class missed the whole native win — the 27B block-128 artifact has 208 such projections (6880 MiB). MEASURED, 27B, N=5 interleaved in ONE lock hold on ONE md5-pinned binary: decode **+1.69%**, **430 MiB** freed (native e4m3 1.0 B/w vs the slab's 1.0625), prefill **+0.83%** over the floor once the class routes through `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` (a dequant-per-call prefill cost −13.7% before the kernel was vectorized and −5.8% after; ~4.5pp of that was structural 3x-weight-traffic, removable only by the route change). Exactness: branch-(b) (in-kernel per-k128 dequant is not the Q8_0 re-encode's arithmetic, so bit-identity is the wrong bar) — teacher-forced 2/128 near-tie flips with a **reverse-tape control** proving the NLL ordering is a tape artifact (each arm scores best on its own tape: 2/128 @ 0.205029 vs 0/128 @ 0.198189, symmetric); kernel-check ALL GREEN incl. 2 new ragged `[5x128]`/`[6x160]` tail cells; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL` / `MEMRA_PARALLEL_DEVICES` | off / required by `auto` | `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` is the model-family-independent 2–4 card placement entrypoint. Before CUDA allocation, the loader compiles `ModelPlan`, binds the source tensor census to the semantic tensor contract, derives legal PP cuts and every routed-MoE layer, reads artifact-level expert activation precision, and compares the checkpoint residency of registered operation backends against each selected card's physical capacity. No layer list or architecture allowlist participates. Dense-only plans select memory-balanced contiguous PP. Routed W4A16 plans select whole-expert EP only when the root and every peer preserve `MEMRA_PARALLEL_RESERVE_MB`; otherwise they fall back to legal PP, and no-fit configurations refuse with both byte estimates. EP uses native P2P and the artifact's BF16 expert boundary. This W4A16 automatic arm also selects BF16-resident non-expert rows before tensor load (`MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1`) unless the operator explicitly sets `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=0`; the rollback is logged as `bf16_nonexpert=f32-expanded(explicit-rollback)` and does not carry the HY3 MTP receipt. Activation-quantized artifacts use PP until a qualified generic device backend exists. Auto-generated PP sets `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS` from exact checkpoint bytes; serial PP is the default arm and `MEMRA_PP_WAVE` stays OFF until that model x hardware carries receipts. `auto` conflicts with legacy `MEMRA_STEP_EP` / `MEMRA_STEP_TP` recipes and manual `MEMRA_PP_STAGES` / `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES` / `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS`. Rollback is `MEMRA_PARALLEL=off` plus an explicit legacy placement. Default OFF; this mode is experimental until each selected model x hardware program carries serving receipts. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION` | off | Strict `0`/`1` extension of `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` for a routed W4A16 plan whose automatic backend is whole-expert EP. `1` derives a TP-attention sidecar for every trunk attention layer from the compiled `ModelPlan`—query/KV head ranges, Q/K/V column shards, O row shards, rank-local KV geometry, and the existing native-P2P runtime—while the same TP group continues to own whole routed experts. It consumes no family name, architecture allowlist, or operator layer list; an unsupported attention operation, non-divisible head geometry, a pipeline-only placement, or use without `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` refuses before serving. Generic ungated decode currently requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2=1`; fused-Q and separate-head gate programs retain their existing qualified paths. Unset/`0` preserves root-local attention plus automatic expert-only EP exactly. Default OFF by design: the 2026-08-31 HY3 four-card plain-c1 trace proves the direction (root GPU 84.7% busy versus 22–23% peers; root-only BF16 projections about 10.0 ms/token and attention kernels about 2.7 ms/token), but the TP+EP arm still owes exact checkpoint/serve gates, sampled c1 pricing, and explicit sidecar-memory admission before any default or support-state change. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION_RANKS` | all automatic ranks | Optional strict `2`/`3`/`4` width for `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION=1`. When narrower than the automatic placement, only the owner-first prefix participates in TP attention while every routed layer retains whole-expert ownership over the full automatic rank set; for example, a four-card plan with `=2` compiles TP2 attention + EP4 experts. The attention ranks must be an owner-first subset of the expert ranks, and every `ModelPlan` head/dimension divisibility check still applies. Setting it without the parent flag, requesting more ranks than the placement, or any other spelling refuses before weight upload. Unset preserves the original same-width TP-attention/EP topology. Default is not `2`: the prior HY3 implementation was fluent but measured 31.01 tok/s versus the 35.84 tok/s EP4 control because fine-grained projection gathers, rank events, and root reductions erased the compute saving. The 2026-09-01 exact-head profile independently confirms the need for a coarser successor: root-only BF16 projections consume 8.96 ms/token while device 0 is 91.9% kernel-busy and peers are 16.6-17.7%. This flag exposes the rank-group substrate; it carries no performance/default claim until persistent replicated residuals and coarse collectives pass the same-artifact serving gates. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_RESERVE_MB` | 6144 | Per-card checkpoint-planning reserve used only by `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto`. The planner adds 6 GiB to every candidate rank before admitting EP or PP, covering CUDA context, loader transforms, boundary buffers, and a small initial serving workspace. It is deliberately separate from request-time KV/admission accounting: a placement passing this check is allowed to load, not promised an arbitrary context/concurrency target. An explicit unsigned value overrides the reserve for a measured deployment; `0` is a diagnostic posture, not a production recommendation. If neither EP nor PP preserves the configured reserve, startup refuses before model bytes move to CUDA. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_DEVICE_ROUTER` | off | Experimental generic W4A16 whole-expert EP route. `1` keeps sigmoid top-k ids/weights on the root GPU and lets rank-local kernels reject non-owned global slots. One fused peer-staging launch per rank replaces the input copy, route-id/weight copies, and BF16 conversion. Every exact-W4A16 batch width, including t=1, writes one canonical global slot row per routed pair before the root applies route weights in token/slot order. Real HY3 K=1 exposed a decode-vs-verify numeric-class mismatch at token 48; the former t=1 owner-grouped FMA was already explicitly a separate numeric class, so it was removed from the exact path before the next gate. The program removes D2H routing, CPU top-k, and host metadata uploads while preserving BF16 expert boundaries and one accumulation program across decode/verify. Requires `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto`, the W4A16 NVFP4 expert backend, and native P2P. `0` is the host-visible router rollback. Default OFF until bounded-logit, long-prompt, sampled c1/c4, and MTP gates pass on the exact model x hardware tuple. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_GRAPH` | off | Exact-W4A16-only multi-device graph experiment for the automatic EP route. `1` captures fixed-address per-rank expert programs after an eager warmup and replays them for t=1; it refuses `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT=1`, and `MEMRA_STEP_TP_TIMING=1` disables replay so diagnostics observe eager calls. Default OFF by design: the HY3 RTX PRO 6000 qualification failed speculative self-consistency at every K=1..8 despite plain t=1/t=4 output identity, and an attempted cross-stream entry-event repair was rejected by CUDA capture isolation. Unset/`0` is the exact eager rollback and the only admissible MTP arm until a new graph dependency design passes the full same-artifact gate. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_SCOPE` | `all` inside the opt-in Q8 arm | Diagnostic precision split for `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT=1`: `all` quantizes the expert input and post-SwiGLU row; `gate-up` uses Q8 only for the input/gate/up program and returns to the exact BF16 boundary for down; `down` keeps exact BF16 gate/up and quantizes only the post-SwiGLU/down program. Unset preserves the original `all` arm. Setting the scope while Q8 activation is OFF refuses instead of silently no-oping; any other spelling refuses. This is not an independent enable flag and carries no default-on claim. It exists to isolate the smallest internal numeric class that meets the same-artifact logit and sampled-quality gates after HY3's all-Q8 arm measured top-20/argmax/MAE/RMSE/max-abs green but missed the predeclared cosine floor. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_GU_PAIRED` | **on inside the opt-in Q8 gate/up arm** | Strict `0`/`1` schedule seam inside Q8 gate/up. ON restores the admitted paired-CTA program: one CTA computes the same output row for gate and up, shares the q8_1 activation loads, and keeps independent accumulators and reduction trees for the two weight banks. `0` is the post-integration separate-CTA rollback. The unset default is scoped: it resolves ON only while `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT=1` and gate/up actually use Q8; with the parent OFF or `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_SCOPE=down`, unset is inert. An explicit `=1` in either invalid posture refuses by name. DEFAULT ON BY DESIGN inside that parent arm, which remains globally OFF: on exact HY3 W4A16 and 4x RTX PRO 6000 Server Edition, same-binary `=0` changed the four-prompt fingerprint from `Below,Below,Expert,Below` to `Below,!,Expert,Below` and produced degenerate sampled text at 36.62 tok/s; paired-CTA restored 4/4 and fluent vendor-default sampled output. On the exact tested binary, three consecutive default-unset probes measured c1 34.88/34.88/34.93 tok/s and c4 aggregate 35.08/35.08/35.25 tok/s with the same fingerprint; a batch-enabled cold/hit pair proved 0/222 then 192/222 cached tokens. The full-vocabulary oracle passes equal argmax, top-20 19/20, cosine 0.999561, RMSE 0.05654, MAE 0.04479, and max-abs 0.30809. The paired-Q8 CUDA kernel, TP schedule, hybrid forward path, and server library blobs remain identical in the receipt-parent runtime; later main deltas are Qwen-only, research/build-only, or default-off and model-scoped, and the rebased full server battery passes 527/527. `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0` throughout. `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT` itself stays OFF and carries the broader admission policy. Engagement is explicit in `[parallel-ep-q8] gate_up_schedule=paired-cta\|separate-cta`. Receipts: `research/hy3-q8-gu-paired-20260901/RESULTS.md`. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_PAIR_DOWN` | off | Experimental t>1 W4A16 EP down-projection kernel that assigns one block to each global routed pair instead of preserving the older owner-filtered slot program. Synthetic layer gates were exact and faster, but the same-artifact HY3 K=1 gate was red with both this arm ON and OFF, so it has no independent end-to-end correctness or performance receipt. Unset/`0` keeps the simpler canonical slot-row oracle; `1` remains diagnostic-only until a green baseline exists and an isolated K=1..8 A/B passes. |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT` | off | Optional numeric-class acceleration inside automatic W4A16 EP decode. `1` affects only the t=1 routed-expert interior: the external layer boundary remains f32/BF16 and checkpoint weights remain NVFP4, but each rank quantizes the active expert input and SwiGLU output to q8_1 for the established NVFP4×Q8 dp4a gate/up/down kernels. Host-expf and owner/rank route accumulation order remain pinned. t=2..4 and prefill stay on the exact BF16-boundary program. Requires `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_DEVICE_ROUTER=1`; unset/`0` is the W4A16 arithmetic rollback. Default OFF because this is an internal W4A8 numeric class: it needs same-artifact logit/first-token bounds, sampled quality, c1 speed, MTP, and repeated-boot gates before any deployment decision. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP` | off | Legacy explicit-layer EP diagnostic, formatted `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]` (for example `24-25@1,2;31@0,2`). New family onboarding must use the structural `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` loader; this recipe remains for focused bisects and partial-layer gates. Every selected routed-expert layer drops its single-device expert slab, assigns each native expert to one owner rank, and keeps router/shared-expert work on the owning stage. Duplicate assignments, unsupported artifact layouts, non-PRO hardware, duplicate devices, and a device list that omits the layer owner refuse before CUDA. Host-bounce is the default oracle; BF16-activation NVFP4 artifacts may opt into native execution with `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1 MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1`. That W4A16 arm accepts either stacked or per-expert physical tensors only after `HostExps` normalizes and validates the canonical NVFP4 bank geometry, scales, macros, and complete expert count. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP` | off | Step-3.7 live TP correctness seam. Explicit placement uses `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]`; `all@0,1,2,3` selects the complete official TP4 trunk (layers 0–44). PP or mixed-owner layouts keep the explicit form so each list can begin with that layer's owning stage device. Before the first model weight upload, one fail-closed preflight validates every selected layer's PP owner, attention shards, expert layout, unique runtime group, uniform PRO hardware, and the official block-128 FP8 expert census. The loader snapshots the parsed TP/EP specs and strict transport/arithmetic flags once; every layer executes from that same immutable configuration rather than re-reading process state. Q/K/V and canonical O reduction use real tensor-parallel rank ownership. With native P2P, eager decode, replay-free verification, and the separately gated per-session multi-token prefill keep QK norm, RoPE, KV/cache, and attention rank-local on all 45 registered layers. The 12 global layers use context-linear sharded caches; the 33 SWA layers use one shared absolute-position ring plan over independent per-rank q8_0/q5_1 planes, physically capped at `min(max_ctx, 512 + 4096 + 31)` rows. Hydration copies only the owning cache's aligned live prefix; append/rebase preserves the active transaction's rollback window; attention reads one contiguous last-window view; growth copies only the checkpoint-visible live prefix and preserves transaction generation. Flat-history prefix snapshots, restores, and fanout refuse once either the owner or distributed SWA ring exists. Without native P2P, SWA and global attention retain the owning-stage fallback. Server admission reserves every unmaterialized distributed-KV sidecar on its owning rank, charging full context only for global layers and the bounded physical ring for SWA, then drops that logical reservation layer-by-layer once live CUDA allocation accounts for it. Routed experts preserve the checkpoint's 128x128 E4M3 blocks: TP2 tensor-shards their 1280-wide projections, while TP4 uses expert ownership because 1280/4 cuts those scale blocks. Router, head gate, and replicated shared-expert work stay on the owning PP stage. One `MEMRA_STEP_TP` spec selects the complete per-layer hybrid; operators do not also set `MEMRA_STEP_EP`. Illegal Step variants, head geometry, scale-block cuts, clamped layers, non-PRO hardware, duplicate devices/layers, conflicting EP/TP assignments, or a rank list whose first device is not the PP owner refuse before execution. Every marker reports attention TP and expert EP separately; this is TP4 serving bring-up, not pipeline parallelism, independent replicas, or a TP8 hardware claim. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P` | off | `1` enables correctness-first `cuMemcpyPeerAsync` collectives for Step TP. Startup requires directed peer capability, peer and default-pool access, and an exact 16 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB / 64 MiB size-class ladder in every direction. TP attention projections/cache/O, the TP2 expert program, and TP4/TP8 expert-owner dispatch/return use native peer copies after qualification. Expert activation and route accumulation stay host-canonical unless the separately gated device-arithmetic path is enabled, and every marker labels the choice. Any other nonempty value refuses. This is an opt-in exactness gate with `performance_claim=false`, not a throughput-qualified default. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_BULK_P2P` | off | Strict `0`/`1` experimental transport shape for Step TP; `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1`. It replaces token-row Q/K/V gathers with one dense peer transfer per remote rank plus byte-preserving root placement, replaces token-row O scatters with byte-preserving root packing plus one dense transfer per remote TP8 block, and assembles fixed-row F32 projection chunks with one byte-preserving strided placement kernel instead of one D2D copy per token. Projection kernels and the canonical eight-block O reduction order are unchanged. Official CUDA 13.1/12.8 raw-bit gates passed for both transport changes. On the exact 16K+512 c=1 agentic fixture, they reduced TTFT from `113.252s` to `23.487s` and raised throughput from `3.691` to `10.321 tok/s`; the 16K prefill profile reduced peer copies `61,452 -> about 21` per 4K attention layer and D2D APIs `17,697,224 -> 2,504` across four chunks. The current experimental stage additionally keeps the large gathered-attention, canonical O, and K/V shadow handoffs on the root device instead of round-tripping them through host memory; positions, head-gate control, MoE routes, and final logits remain host-visible. This newest stage still requires official dual-CUDA equality, structural D2H collapse, and high-context serving proof. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DEVICE_ARITHMETIC` | off | `1` keeps TP4/TP8 expert-owner SwiGLU activation and slot-ordered route accumulation on the GPUs after native peer dispatch. It requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1` and a `MEMRA_STEP_TP` TP4/TP8 expert-owner layer; explicit legacy EP, host-bounce, and TP2 projection-sharded experts refuse. The owner uses Memra's host-matching `expf` transcription plus explicit round-to-nearest operations; rank 0 accumulates in the existing token/slot order and performs one final readback. Unset or `0` preserves the host-canonical rollback. This is an opt-in raw-bit exactness path with `performance_claim=false`, not a throughput-qualified default. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_F32_MIRROR` | off | `1` expands each Step TP Q/K/V/O BF16 shard to exact F32 once during model load, releases that shard's BF16 TP copy, and reuses the resident F32 values with the same TP1/TP2/TP4/TP8 canonical chunk shapes. It requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP`; unset or `0` preserves BF16 residency with decode-time expansion, and any other value refuses. The owning-stage fallback weights are unchanged. This is an opt-in measured decode optimization that trades additional rank VRAM for fewer allocations and conversion kernels; it is not enabled by native P2P or device expert arithmetic automatically. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 multi-token prefill door. **TP2 ADMITTED 2026-08-25, UNQUALIFIED:** the shape check named `ranks == 4` while the prefill body is rank-count-generic, so a 2-card native-P2P placement could never open the door even though its O reduction is rank-local. TP4's high-context NO-GO below is a TRANSPORT verdict about that placement's peer-copy count, not evidence about TP2. TP2 carries NO receipts yet — prefill argmax identity and a TTFT measurement are owed before any claim, and the door stays off by default. `1` requires the qualified TP4 placement, native P2P, device arithmetic, and non-FP8 KV; it keeps Q/K/V, QK norm, RoPE, quantized KV append, attention, and canonical O reduction rank-local across all 45 trunk layers. Official layers 3 and clamped 43, 24+29-token continuation, rollback after a real partial commit, retry, and handoff into eager TP4 decode are raw-bit exact on CUDA 13.1 and 12.8. Cross-request concat-prime did not clear its live-server performance gate, so a Step prime-batch call with this door open refuses before cache mutation; the server restores the prompt queues and serves each request through the qualified per-session path. **HIGH-CONTEXT PERFORMANCE NO-GO (2026-08-18):** on one four-card RTX PRO 6000 host with the official `b3d7916f` FP8 checkpoint and an exact 16K+512 coding-agent fixture, Memra c=1 measured `3.6912 tok/s`, `113.252s` TTFT, and `138.707s` wall versus best-valid vLLM 0.27.1 Triton+MTP3 at `139.433 tok/s`, `1.099s`, and `3.672s`. An unprofiled four-chunk gate reproduced `115.554s` inside prefill itself. A partial Nsight trace and the source agree on the wall: three token-row column gathers plus six remote O blocks issue about `61,440` peer copies and matching event waits per 4K attention layer. Do not claim TP4 P2P throughput or enable this door by default; the next gate is a persistent bulk pack/transpose/peer-copy/unpack collective that preserves canonical arithmetic. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_GROUPED_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 routed-expert prefill door. `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL=1`, native P2P, device arithmetic, and the official TP4 expert-owner layout. One model-scoped capacity plan is reused across all 42 routed layers for 16–4096 active tokens, including capacity-backed slabs whose active prefix is shorter than the allocation; Step supplies only layer clamp policy and tensor views. Official 24+29-token prefill is raw-bit exact on CUDA 13.1 and 12.8, cuts the balanced N=5 median from `1.50207s` to `0.57160s` (`2.6278x`), and leaves peak HBM unchanged. A 24/29-token cross-request B=2 concat implementation was also exact, but its balanced live-server N=5 median improved only `0.953301s` to `0.925863s` (`1.029636x`), below the declared `1.20x` retention bar; that execution path was removed. Concurrent requests therefore use the scheduler's per-session grouped fallback. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_X8` | **on for `m<=8`** | Literal `0` rolls short decode/verify calls back to the prior 128/256-token padded launch; unset or `1` uses Memra's own 128-output-row x 8-token tactic. The kernel keeps the existing dynamic `1x128` E4M3 activation quantizer, block-128 E4M3 weights/scales, MMA instruction, and epilogue; it only stops computing padded token columns. Exactness: full ragged battery plus every Step expert width `m=1..8` passed on RTX 5090, and `m=1/4/8` passed independently on all three RTX PRO 6000 cards (zero EXACT bit mismatches; full 254-code E4M3 coverage). Balanced adjacent N=6 on 5090 measured **2.72-3.91x** the old FP8 launch and **2.07-2.91x** Q8; balanced N=5 per arm per card on PRO measured **2.17-3.52x** the old FP8 launch and **1.75-2.64x** Q8 across Step gate/up/down shapes. These are synchronized GEMM-kernel ratios, not end-to-end Step throughput. External implementations informed the operand-orientation hypothesis only; shipped CUDA and dispatch are Memra-owned and have no external header, submodule, or runtime dependency. |
| `MEMRA_FP8_FOLD` | off | ARM A (lane fp8-gemm-arm 2026-08-03). `1` = fold a block-128 FP8 scale grid into ONE per-tensor scale at load (global-amax re-encode, `s = amax/448`) so the per-tensor e4m3 consumers (`MEMRA_ST_E4M3` resident arm + `try_fp8_gemm`) take a Qwen-official / DeepSeek-lineage block-128 checkpoint unchanged. LOSSY where block dynamic range varies — that is the arm's question, not a free win |
| `MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU` | off | ARM B' (lane fp8-gemm-arm 2026-08-03). `1` = block-128 FP8 weights dequant to Q8_0 **on the GPU** at load (`cu/fp8_blk_dequant.cu`) instead of host dequant + host re-encode. BYTE-IDENTICAL to the CPU path (kernel-check `fp8-blk-gpu`: 0 mismatching bytes on aligned + both ragged edges; real-checkpoint 4-prompt argmax MATCH with bit-equal top-5 logits), so decode and dispatch are unchanged — this is load wall only: 35.65s → 9.22s (3.87x, N=3 interleaved) on a 2.65 GB block-128 checkpoint. Unlike ARM A it keeps every block's own scale (Q8_0's per-32 grid is finer than the source 128×128). Opt-in until the 27B-class rig gates run; CPU path stays default |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` | **on** for the native-resident source, off for the stash | **TWO OPERAND SOURCES, TWO DEFAULTS since lane/fp8-blk128-decode (2026-08-05).** Unset = the checkpoint-native `QT_F8_E4M3_BLK` residency grid takes this tile (ON); `0` reverts that route to dequant-per-call; `1` additionally admits the load-time e4m3 STASH source (a duplicate copy next to a Q8_0 slab, spending `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`), which stays opt-in. Same tile, different defaults, because the floors differ — see DIFFERENT DENOMINATOR below. The split matters mechanically, not just editorially: the stash gate is also what admits the duplicate at LOAD time (`model.rs`), so one shared gate would have made the native flip silently duplicate every Q8_0 tensor with an fp8 sibling. P1 option (b) (lane/fp8-mmq 2026-08-04). `1` = block-128 FP8 prefill GEMMs run through memra's OWN per-block MMQ tile (`cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu`) instead of falling to the Q8_0 floor. The checkpoint's e4m3 bytes ARE the MMA A operand (`m16n8k32` e4m3×e4m3; **the form is `kind::mxf8f6f4.block_scale.scale_vec::1X` at the ue8m0 identity scale since 2026-08-06, a 309-TF class at 16.06 cyc/warp-MMA — it shipped on plain `kind::f8f6f4`, which is a 155-TF class at 32.02 cyc, NOT the 381-TF class this line used to claim; see `MEMRA_MMQ_FP8BLK_PLAIN`. Every "not MMA-bound against 381" reading below was taken against a ceiling this kernel never had — the measured TFLOP numbers are real, the ceiling was 155**) and each `[128×128]` block keeps its own f32 scale, so unlike ARM A (`MEMRA_FP8_FOLD`) there is NO weight-side re-quantization and unlike ARM B' (`MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU`) it is not stuck at Q8_0-MMQ throughput. Exactness gate: `fp8_mmq_check` EXACT arm is BIT-IDENTICAL (0 differing bits, 9 shapes incl. ragged n/k) to a host reference of the same arithmetic, with a committed negative control. Admits TWO operand sources (2026-08-05): the block-128 raw-bytes+grid load **stash** (a second e4m3 copy next to a Q8_0 slab, shares `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`) and, since lane/fp8-blk128-decode, the **resident** `blk` grid on a `QT_F8_E4M3_BLK` tensor — the checkpoint-native single copy, no slab and no budget. Same bytes, same grid, same layout contract, so the kernel cannot tell them apart. That second source is what makes it the block-128 class's prefill route: `try_e4m3_blk_prefill` calls it first and falls through to the dequant only on refusal. Refuses any tensor containing an e4m3 NaN code (hardware reads 0x7F as NaN, the host/ARM B' convention as 0.0) — scanned once per tensor, not assumed. **v2 verdict (lane/fp8-mmq-v2 2026-08-04, receipts `research/fp8st-20260804/mmq-v2/LANE-VERDICT.jsonl`): stays default OFF.** v2 restructured the mainloop so the 128-wide scale block is the outer k loop and one chained unscaled accumulator folds `s_blk*dB` once per block (activation scale per-128, tile 61KB→37KB), which moved GEMM-only from v1's uniform 0.81-0.94x floor to parity-class at long context: 27B m=6257 q 1.006 / o 0.989 / gate_up 0.993 / down 0.994 / square 1.023, and 27B m=512 down_proj 1.089x. It does NOT reach ~1.1x, and local end-to-end pp on the 1.7B is 0.9899x (pp512) / 0.9951x (pp6257) because that model's narrow projections measure 0.846-0.894x at m=512. Model-level drift improved ~130x (perplexity +0.11% → +0.0008%) with 3/128 teacher-forced near-tie flips and 2/192 in the 6-draw loop. Occupancy (halved `MMQ_Y`, `minBlocksPerMultiprocessor=2`) and cp.async weight double-buffering were both implemented and REFUTED — their build seams (`MEMRA_MMQ_Y_FP8`/`MEMRA_MMQ_OCC_FP8`/`MEMRA_MMQ_PIPE_FP8`) and dispatch arms were DELETED per the flags doctrine (v0.69.0); the lane rows are the record — re-read them before re-proposing either. Token-tile width is chosen per call by wave fill, not by `out_f` (an out_f threshold mis-picked every 1.7B projection). **DIFFERENT DENOMINATOR ON THE NATIVE-RESIDENT CLASS (lane/fp8-blk128-decode 2026-08-05):** v2's 0.85–1.09x was measured against a floor whose Q8_0 slab was ALREADY RESIDENT — tile vs tile. On the block-128 residency arm the floor must also CREATE that slab every prefill call (27.9 ms/pass measured after the dequant kernel was vectorized), so the tile only has to not be 27.9 ms worse than a kernel it trails by ≤15% on a subset of shapes. Measured 3-arm interleaved N=3 on the 27B: slab 1540.5 / blk-dequant 1449.1 / **blk-MMQ 1553.3** tok/s = 1.0083x the floor with min(C) > max(A), ledger 832 dispatches. Same tile, opposite sign, because the question changed. **DEFAULT FLIPPED for that source (2026-08-05)**, on the exactness cells 6b741068 deferred it on — measured on `prime_cache`, the class that actually dispatches this kernel, with a ledger on every arm (624 = 208 projections x 3 passes = full coverage) and an A==B bit-identical control proving the instrument can see zero where zero is: argmax UNCHANGED and top-10 order identical to the floor's, rms_rel 2.5e-2 on an rms-2.7155 logit vector, teacher-forced NLL on the prompt's own continuation LOWER than the floor's (2.764722 vs 2.787267 = −0.81%) on a tape neither arm produced, +2/511 argmax disagreements against a 50.29% floor baseline = the near-tie class; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS, serve-st-gate + serve-smoke 0 failed, kernel-check ALL GREEN. Post-flip re-measured NAKED (`postflip-*` receipts): the new default dispatches 1040 and reproduces 1553.9 tok/s, `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ=0` dispatches 0 and reproduces the dequant arm's 1449.1 exactly — the seam is real, not nominal. The +0.83% margin was then re-settled on its own two-arm sweep with the arms ADJACENT and the order ALTERNATING (`pairsweep.sh`, N=6 pairs), because a third arm sitting between the two being compared breaks the interleaving law for that pair: N median 1553.0 vs floor 1540.6, min(N) 1548.7 > max(floor) 1541.2 = DISJOINT, 6/6 pairwise wins, mean +12.72 tok/s = **+0.83%** — the pre-flip figure reproduced. The dequant arm is NOT dead code: it is where every tile precondition (in_f % 16, grid dims, scale == 1.0, the e4m3-NaN scan) still falls through, so a checkpoint the tile refuses keeps exact prefill instead of losing the class. |

ON: card0 peaks 78964 (**18923 free**) at 75.97/75.82 tok/s against 76.01/75.99 off — throughput-neutral, token ids identical, **16.4 GB freed** — and the configs that OOMed all run (3-head NVFP4 50.09, 3-head BF16 50.77, W8 80.16, W8+hybrid 81.46). Not a new risk: already validated at 262k (12 simultaneous 262k sessions, teacher-forced byte-identical across the wrap, run-spec K=1..8 PASS — research/ringval-20260810). A dead end recorded so it is not re-run: the MoE SLRU slot cache (`MEMRA_MOE_VRAM_FRAC`, 85% of free VRAM) looked like the culprit but `MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS=256` changed the peak by zero — that cache is gated behind `MEMRA_MOE_CACHE`, unset here. Experimental Step35 owning-stage bounded KV ring (`1` enables; lane/kv256-capacity). This flag controls the legacy owner cache and MTP scratch; native rank-local TP SWA uses its own bounded ring contract under `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P`. SWA trunk layers and the SWA MTP scratch allocate `min(max_ctx, 512 + 4096 + 31) = min(max_ctx, 4639)` physical rows while full-attention layers retain `max_ctx`; Gemma4 and every non-Step35 architecture are unchanged. Absolute sequence lengths stay monotonic; append and reader offsets map into the ring, with the aligned live prefix rebased contiguously at wrap so the audited prime/decode/batched/verify/MTP views keep their existing address contract. With the door open, explicit prime chunks are capped at 4096 (`0` becomes 4096); with it closed, the legacy explicit schedule is unchanged. Flat-history prefix snapshots/restores/fanout are refused with a log line, and lapped affinity/spec rewinds decline instead of fabricating overwritten rows.
