| `MEMRA_B200_MLA_DECODE_ARM=1` | B200 (sm_100a) t<=8 HEAD-PARALLEL, LAUNCH-LIGHT MLA/DSA decode arm (lane/b200-mla-decode-20260902, owner order 2026-09-02 "hardly improve the decode on these cards, before the full 1M"). Investigation finding, stated first because it changed the shape of this door: the nsys motivation for this lane cited 321 cuBLAS gemv-class launches at t=1 attributed to the strided-batched bf16 GEMM `memra_bf16_gemm_sb` (batch=64, n=512, k=256 — the absorb_q shape). Tracing every call site (`hybrid_forward.rs` `mla_tc_prefill_chain`, gated `t >= 16`; the ONLY caller of `mla_bf16_gemm_sb_raw`) shows decode (t=1) cannot reach that path in this checkout, matching the `MEMRA_MLA_TC_PREFILL` row's own "decode untouched" receipt and the 2026-08-30 launch-diet census's own attribution of `memra_mla_attn_gathered_kernel`/`absorb_q`/`decompress_v` costs to a COLD PRIME (t=4626), not decode. No cuBLASLt replacement kernel was built against a path that does not execute at t=1 on this branch — see LANE.md "why not the cuBLASLt kernel" for the full trace and the request back to the spawning session to reconcile its nsys attribution before that work is built. What this door DOES do: `memra_mla_absorb_q` / `memra_mla_decompress_v` / `memra_mla_attn_gathered` already grid `t_q * n_head` blocks (already head-parallel — 64 CTAs at t=1 on the glm5 geometry — contra this lane's own opening hypothesis that the grid was `t` rows), but 64 CTAs under-fills a B200 die (~132-148 SMs). This arm reuses the existing bit-identical-by-construction output-range split kernels (`memra_mla_absorb_q_split_f32` / `memra_mla_decompress_v_split_f32`, MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT's kernels) at a B200-tuned target (~2048 blocks vs the generic door's ~1024, PRO6000-tuned — the per-hardware-arm-selection law), and adds a NEW twin for `attn_gathered` (`memra_mla_attn_gathered_split_kernel`, cu/mla_attn.cu — no split existed for it before this lane) using the SAME output-range technique: every split block reruns the shared score/softmax tile walk IN FULL (identical rounding, since the softmax normalizer is common to every output element) and only the final per-l accumulate-and-write loop is restricted to `[lo, hi)` — bit-identical by the same "which block computes it" argument as the absorb/decompress splits, asserted in `mla_decode_arm_gate.rs`. TRADE-OFF stated plainly for attn_gathered: unlike the independent-output absorb/decompress splits, every split factor here also DUPLICATES the dominant score/softmax compute, so the host policy (`mla_b200_gathered_split_for`, mla_ffi.rs) caps it far more conservatively (factor <= 4) than the absorb/decompress policy (`mla_b200_split_for`, target ~2048 blocks) — this is a genuine hardware question (latency vs throughput bound at t<=8 on a B200 die) this lane cannot answer without the box. Compile-time gated to sm_100a builds only (`cfg!(memra_sm100_tcgen05)`, set by build.rs for `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH=100a`) — on 120a/90a/89 builds `mla_b200_decode_arm_on()` is `false` unconditionally, so the census/flag-doctrine bar ("naked commands full speed on whatever card they run on") holds trivially: the var cannot even engage off-target. Scoped to `t_q <= 8` per the owner's order; wider widths fall through to the generic `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` door or `MEMRA_MLA_TC_PREFILL`. Checked FIRST in `mla_absorb_q`/`mla_decompress_v`/`mla_attn_gathered` (ahead of the generic split door), so the two doors compose rather than race. Announced once per boot (`[mla-b200-decode-arm] engaged`), counted (`mla_ffi::MLA_B200_DECODE_ARM_DISPATCHES`). | **DEFAULT OFF (2026-09-02, lane/b200-mla-decode-20260902).** Correctness: `mla_decode_arm_gate` (crates/memra-engine/src/bin/mla_decode_arm_gate.rs) asserts BIT-IDENTICAL output, shipped-vs-split, at every tested split factor for absorb_q/decompress_v/attn_gathered at t_q in {1,4} on synthetic glm5_next-shaped geometry (64 heads, kv_rank 512, d_nope/d_v 256, d_rope 0, 2048-slot DSA gather over a 32k-row k-pool) — see LANE.md for the run receipt (this gate runs correctness-only on any CUDA device per the rig law; it was exercised on the local 5090, NOT the B200). Compile check: both `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH=100a` and `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH=120a` builds green (the door is dead code, correctly, off-target). Throughput: NO RECEIPT — this is a B200-only mechanism and the B200 box belongs to the spawning session; the A/B (interleaved x5, greedy + vendor-default sampled twin per the never-serve-greedy law, both split factors and both un-split baselines, per the per-hardware-arm-selection law) is the explicit next step, not run by this lane. NOT default-ON for that reason alone. Rollback seam: unset (or off-target build) — read per call. Lane doc: `research/b200-mla-decode-20260902/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_SEL_LEDGER=1` | GATE-HARNESS INSTRUMENT for `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`, never a serving flag (lane/b200-glm5-graph-20260902). Records the routed-MoE selection per layer per token on BOTH arms so `glm5-decode-graph-gate` can assert the device-table arm picks the same experts with the same routing weights the host oracle reads back.| Every other route tap in this engine (`moesd`, `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE`/`WEIGHT_TRACE`, `hidden_trace`) reads the selection back on the HOST, which is exactly what a capture region forbids and why the decode-graph door refuses while any of them is armed; this one is capture-legal by construction — the device arm records with a `memcpy_dtod` into a PERSISTENT per-(device, layer) slot pre-armed before the capture opens (a memcpy node, fixed address, no sync, no DtoH, no in-capture allocation), the host arm records into a plain host vector, and the gate drains the device slots after the token. Changes no kernel, no operand and no launch order on either arm. Module: `crates/memra-engine/src/glm5_sel_ledger.rs`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH=1` | glm5_next WHOLE-RUN T=1 DECODE CUDA GRAPH, per pipeline stage (lane/b200-glm5-graph-20260902, owner order 2026-09-02 "hardly improve the decode on these cards"; target 230 tok/s plain = 4.35 ms/token on a 2x B200 pair). **DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN — no box receipt exists; the rig is exactness-only.** Read PER CALL, so `=0`/unset is a live rollback seam, not a process-lifetime latch. WHY: the nsys census of a plain t=1 token on 2x B200 SXM (GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4, resident PP2) reads ~2,900 kernel launches and ~6 ms of launch/gap at ~2.2 us each inside a ~24 ms token. That is ISSUE cost, and a replayable graph is the only mechanism that removes issue cost without touching a kernel. This family had no graph arm at all because the walk was not capturable: a capture region admits no `cuStreamSynchronize` and no pageable HtoD, and the T=1 walk carried 43 syncs and ~156 HtoD per token (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/host-audit-20260901/LANE.md`). WHAT IS CAPTURED: per stage, the maximal CONTIGUOUS runs of KDA-mixer layers inside that stage's `[lo, hi)` — hc pre/post glue, the KDA mixer, the FFN-input norm, the routed MoE, the shared expert. Those layers carry NO position-derived launch parameter (the conv ring rolls in-kernel, the recurrent state is addressed by pointer), so a run graph is position-INDEPENDENT: captured once per session per stage, never re-captured per token, keyed on `(device ordinal, lo, hi)` with the `cache.pos` it expects next (a rollback/reuse/prefix-restore seam moves `pos` and the pool is dropped, never replayed stale). The KDA recurrent-state ping-pong (`kda::kda_cached`, whose own doc says the host swap is "NOT capture-safe") is handled by capturing each run TWICE, once per ping-pong phase, and alternating the replays — no extra kernel, no copy-back, and the host fields are mirrored after every replay so a fall-back to eager, a prime, or a snapshot sees the pointers it expects. Capture uses `capture_graph_retained_nowarm` (RELAXED mode): stream capture RECORDS rather than executes, and the warmup-free contract exists for bodies with device side effects. Private hc workspace + private f16 scratch are owned by the pool and swapped resident around capture AND every replay; `vws_recycle*` retains into the graph's keeper while a capture is open, so nothing baked is ever re-issued to eager work. A captured run always takes the WORKSPACE form of the walk (`hyper_range_decode_ws_body` against the pool's private `HyperDecodeWs`) whatever `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` says, because a capture needs stable operand addresses: the two hc walks are kept call-for-call in step and their byte identity is gated by `tests/hc_decode_ws_gpu.rs`, but the door makes that dispatch for the caller and says so here rather than leaving it to a counter. WHAT STAYS EAGER, and why: **every MLA/DSA layer.** `HybridModel::mla_attn_cached_pre_wo` derives `slot` from the host `layer.len` and the kpool derives `n_pools`/`select_k`/`width` from it, so its launch geometry changes per token — a captured node would replay last token's geometry. Also eager: prefill, the decode tail's logits DtoH (host sampling), the batched/exact-16 tier, and the spec verify walk, which keeps `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH`. Composition with `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` is refused by name (a sharded KDA layer is not on this path). THE ONE PROGRAM CHANGE THE DOOR MAKES, and its identity argument: the routed MoE stops reading its selection back. The T=1 arm of door D (`vrows_t1_dev`) keeps `sel`/`w` on device, builds the pointer/scale tables with `moe_vrows_tables_from_sel`, and runs the verify-rows kernel pair at `n_pairs = n_used`. That pair is the per-row form of the fused epilogue, which is the per-token form of the sequential `qmatvec_expert_q8` + `ffn_act_lim` + `axpy_into` chain — same g-strided dots, same pre-clamped SwiGLU expression, same slot-ordered `__fmaf_rn` accumulation, same macro folds in the same places; the table values are term-for-term the host loop's (`base + ex*stride` exact integer arithmetic, the same macro planes, one IEEE-754 product for `w * macro_down`). The device MoE arm engages wherever the door is set, INCLUDING on a range whose capture was refused: it is the door's numeric program, not a capture side effect, and keeping the two halves independent means a refusal changes only whether the launches are recorded, never what they compute. At t=1 this is the single-row case of the claim the vrest lane gated at t>=2. FAIL-CLOSED, and this is load-bearing: door D could ignore `promote_worker_h2d` because it required `t >= 2`; the T=1 arm carries that conjunct itself, plus every host-visible selection consumer (`moesd`, `hidden_trace`, `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE`/`STATS`/`WEIGHT_TRACE`/`INPUT_TRACE_DIR`, `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER=0`). PREREQUISITES, refused BY NAME with a once-note on stderr (`[glm5-decode-graph] eager: <reason>`): `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET=1` (without it the shared expert uploads a pageable `vec![1.0; t]` per MoE layer, which a capture region rejects), device sigmoid routing, no armed route/hidden observer, no NVMe worker H2D promotion, no cudarc event tracking (`MEMRA_EVT`), no TP shard, and at least one KDA layer in the range. FIRST BOX RUN (2x B200, int2 head `8c31be2f4`, real artifact, PP2 resident, 2026-09-02) exited rc=1 and found two defects, both fixed before this row settled: (1) the pool's recurrent-state pointer signature was ORDERED, but the replay mirrors `kda_cached`'s host ping-pong swap by design, so it read as \"moved\" every token and forced a re-capture per step on a session where nothing had moved — the pair is recorded UNORDERED now, phase-invariant by construction; (2) the re-capture path dropped the old `StageGraphs` (destroying the exec AND freeing every buffer it baked) while that exec's replay was still outstanding, which the driver reports as `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE` — the stream is drained before the remove and again after. A capture failure now falls through to the byte-identical eager walk for the whole range, prints once, and latches the stage off for the session instead of failing the request. The gate FORCES the invalidation path (re-seats a captured layer's recurrent state mid-run) and fails as vacuous if no re-capture happened. SECOND BOX RUN (2026-09-02) confirmed the phase-invariance fix (the re-capture is now the gate's own forced one, 13 s further in) and isolated the remaining defect to the RE-CAPTURE itself, still `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`. Since cudarc's graph `Drop` discards destroy errors and this error is not sticky, the failing call is one this code makes and propagates — so every failable step in the capture and replay path now names itself before returning: `[glm5-decode-graph] capture-error: call=<which> dev=N stage=[lo,hi) run=i/n layers=[a,b) phase=p recapture=bool stream_capture=none/ACTIVE/INVALIDATED free=X/YMB ledger=bool err=...`, covering pre-begin-status, the synchronize, `cuStreamBeginCapture`, the capture body, `cuStreamEndCapture`+instantiate, `cuGraphUpload`, `htod_i32`, the `memcpy_dtod` into `x_io`, and `cuGraphLaunch`. Two variables were removed at the same time: a re-capture now REUSES the stage's `x_io`, private hc workspace and private f16 scratch (it destroys only the execs), so a rebuild allocates and frees nothing of its own against the stream-ordered mempool the graphs' alloc nodes draw from and the rebuilt graphs bake the same addresses; and a capture body that fails now still ends the capture, so it cannot leave the stream ACTIVE for the next begin. GRAPH-LAUNCH HEADROOM GUARD: below `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` the range early-outs to the eager walk (`[glm5-decode-graph] graph replay suspended:`) — this route HAS a byte-identical eager twin, so it yields rather than failing the request.| RECEIPTS AND WHAT IS STILL MISSING: `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/glm5_decode_graph_gate.rs` (`glm5-decode-graph-gate`) runs 64 greedy tokens on each arm from one prompt and asserts byte-identical token ids AND identical per-(token, layer) expert selections and routing weights (weights compared on their BITS), refusing to pass if the door never replayed. NO BOX RUN EXISTS YET — the 2x B200 pair belongs to the spawning session; counters `GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH_REPLAYS` / `_CAPTURES` / `_LAYERS`. SM120 HISTORY, and why B200 is a different question: `MEMRA_STEP_TP_GRAPH` re-measured **-20%** on the SM120 stack 2026-08-25 (row below), and the reason is stated there — its remainder was dependency LATENCY, 405 small serialized kernels that cannot fill the device, which graph replay cannot recover. The B200 census reads the opposite shape: ~2,900 launches at ~2.2 us of GAP inside a 24 ms token is ~25% of the token spent NOT executing, on a card whose per-launch floor is a larger share of a smaller kernel. The two are only comparable through the launch-floor share, so this door must be priced on the pair with its own interleaved A/B and NOT inherited from that verdict in either direction. FLIP CONDITION: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on 2x B200 (x5 on anomaly), greedy identity AND the vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in both arms. Lane doc: `research/b200-glm5-graph-20260902/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE=1` | glm5_next Q8_1 ACTIVATION-QUANTIZE FUSE (lane/b200-q8-fuse-20260902, launch-diet lever 5): the T=1 mHC decode walk (`hyper_range_decode` / `hyper_range_decode_ws_body`) computes the FFN-input norm (`e.rms_norm(y, post_attn_norm, ...) -> z`), then `hyper_ffn_branch` -> `moe_ffn_il_zq8` -> the glm5 MoE dispatch (`moe_ffn_glm5_ep` / the sigmoid-router grouped arm) re-quantizes that SAME `z` with a standalone `quantize_q8_1` launch — one of the 416 `quantize_q8_1` launches/token the b200 nsys census (`research/b200-q8-fuse-20260902/LANE.md`) attributes to this family, 45 layers deep. The `moe_ffn_il_zq8`/`moe_ffn_glm5_ep`/`moe_shexp_add`/`shexp_gate_up_t1` plumbing to take a PRE-quantized `(zq, zd)` pair already existed (built for a different call site — `add_rms_norm_zq8`'s callers); this door is the missing producer at the mHC-decode call site. New kernel `rms_norm_zq8_f32` (cu/kernels.cu, next to `rms_norm_q8_1`) computes the norm AND emits BOTH the f32 row (still consumed by the router-logits GEMV and the ungated shexp arms) and its q8_1 quantization in ONE launch — BIT-IDENTICAL to `rms_norm` then `quantize_q8_1` (same sum-of-squares reduction order, same `(x*scale)*w` association, same `quantize_q8_1` per-32-block amax/127/round epilogue, copied verbatim from `add_rms_norm_zq8`'s epilogue minus its `a+b` add). `hyper_ffn_branch` gained a `zq8: Option<&(CudaSlice<i8>, CudaSlice<f32>)>` parameter threaded to `moe_ffn_il_zq8`; every OTHER caller (prefill, the dense-batch per-row walk) passes `None` and is byte-unchanged. Removes ONE `quantize_q8_1` launch per hc-decode layer per token when engaged (45 layers on glm5_next = up to 45 launches/token off the ~2,900/token floor); does not touch `cu/qmatvec.cu`, the attn-norm producer, or the KDA mixer's own internal q8 quantizes (other lanes' territory / a separate, unwired chain). | **DEFAULT OFF, pending the box A/B (2026-09-02, lane/b200-q8-fuse-20260902).** Gate: `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/q8_fuse_gate.rs` — chain-vs-fused byte-identical q8_1 output on synthetic glm5 n_embd=4096 rows (plus the KDA/MoE vector widths in the model pack), N=5 per-launch us printed. OPEN FOLLOW-UP: verify rows (t=2..15) and the attn-norm/KDA-mixer chain are separate, unwired `quantize_q8_1` sites — out of this lane's scope (KDA's own matvec dispatch and `cu/qmatvec.cu` are other lanes'). | `MEMRA_MLA_TC_PREFILL=1` | glm5_next TENSOR-CORE MLA PREFILL: the three per-position f32 kernels the launch-diet census named (`memra_mla_attn_gathered_kernel` 139.1 ms + `memra_mla_absorb_q_kernel` 44.5 ms + `memra_mla_decompress_v_kernel` 43.6 ms per layer-chunk = 3 kernels x 11 MLA layers x 2 chunks = 75.8% of a 98%-GPU-busy cold 4626-token prime, ~2.5 TF/s effective, WINDOW-20260830.md §4) are replaced at prefill widths by: absorb and decompress as ONE strided-batched bf16 tensor-core cuBLASLt GEMM each (`memra_bf16_gemm_sb`, batch = 64 heads, the per-head views expressed through ld + strided-batch offsets), and the attention as `fa_mla_gathered_bf16` (cu/flash_attn.cu) — the `fa_prefill_bf16_hd512_sp` body with the axes recast: one CTA per (query, 16-head band), K tiles GATHERED through the query's DSA index list, V aliasing K (NoPE latent rows), online softmax, bf16 MMA f32 accumulate. FORM CHOICE stated for the record: the dual-form MLA law (materialized prefill / absorbed decode) is about DENSE prefill; glm5_next's DSA caps every query at topk+tail rows and selects ONE list per query SHARED ACROSS ALL 64 HEADS, which makes the ABSORBED form the GEMM-shaped one (head axis = MMA m, shared latent rows = one B operand per tile) — FlashMLA's own sparse-prefill geometry — while materializing K/V would give every head its own K plane and destroy exactly that sharing. Trivial-selection queries (visible <= topk: the selector emits the full causal prefix) ride the SAME kernel with the identity gather; no separate dense program. Selection, the latent cache, the q/kv projections, the router, and DECODE are untouched. Engages ONLY when: a gathered selection exists, `d_rope == 0`, `kv_rank == 512` (the kernel stamp), `t >= 16`, non-portable-MMA arch; a cuBLASLt shape decline falls back to the f32 kernels with a once-per-shape `[mla-tc-prefill] DECLINED` line. Engagement announced once per boot (`[mla-tc-prefill] engaged`) and counted (`MLA_TC_PREFILL_DISPATCHES`). | **DEFAULT ON (owner acceptance 2026-08-30, on the two-box A/B receipts; was default OFF at ship, flipped the same day the flip condition was met).** The flip condition ran TWICE — interleaved x5 fresh boots per arm on the 3-card serving shape on BOTH boxes (Server-Edition and Workstation-Edition silicon): zero violations, ZERO argmax flips across 20 boots, TTFD 7.449->2.832 s @4.6k / 9.571->2.986 s @6.5k (box A) and 6.579->2.507 / 8.911->2.863 s (box B) = -62%..-69%, prefill 619-724 -> 1629-2255 tok/s, vendor-default sampled twin -66/-67%, decode untouched (33.09 vs 32.98 and 28.80 vs 28.77 ms/tok), engagement receipted in every ON boot, zero cuBLASLt declines (receipts: research/glm5-prefix-latent-20260830/box-window/, mla-tc-ab arms). `MEMRA_MLA_TC_PREFILL=0` is the rollback seam (read per call). New NUMERIC CONFIG: bf16 operands, f32 accumulate — band-gated, never bit. Gate `crates/memra-engine/tests/mla_tc_prefill_gpu.rs`, RUN GREEN 6/6 (rig 5090, TF32 off, debug, 2026-08-30): kernel-level at full GLM5_NEXT geometry vs a CPU gathered oracle AND the shipped f32 chain — worst 3.228e-3 relative at t in {16, 64} (trivial + sparse + chunked lists), 2.280e-3 tc-vs-f32 at t=4096 with the REAL 2048/4 budget (width 2051) + CPU spot checks 3.229e-3/3.045e-3; band pinned 8e-3 (the bf16 class). ## 4. Diagnostics & test config

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT_GATE=1` | dspark route only: run BOTH partial-accept restore arms (column-stash + snapshot-replay oracle) per partial round and byte-compare the resulting cache state — the same-binary exactness gate for `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ANATOMY=1` | eager MTP draft-step phase timers (glue/attn/ffn/head, cumulative stderr summary every 128 steps). Phase boundaries sync the stream so absolute time inflates — the BREAKDOWN is the signal. Diagnostic only (lane/draftcost-moe, 2026-08-20; row backfilled by lane/dspark-sampled-admission after the flags census flagged the drift) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_STATS=1` | per-layer expert-cache hit/miss/staged-bytes prints (forces the stats-visible dispatch path) |
| `MEMRA_MOESD_DEPTH=<n>` | `target_efficiency` harness only (moesd dual-device sweep, `research/moesd-harness-20260811/`): prime_cache token depth for the target-forward matrix; default 128, floor 16 |
| `MEMRA_MOESD_CACHE_CAP=<n>` | `target_efficiency` harness only: expert-cache entry cap for the moesd target-forward sweep; refuses caps below the matrix's required residency |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_STATS=1` | print cumulative positioned-read snapshots when server requests finish: reads, logical bytes, errors, short reads, config-parse fallbacks (`config_fallbacks`), runtime mmap fallbacks (`fallbacks`), buffer waits, and worker ring-full events. Snapshots are totals, not per-request deltas; do not sum them |
| `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE=<path>` | append (layer, step, expert ids) per decode step — routing-locality analysis (`research/scripts/moe_trace_analyze.py`, 2026-07-07 M3 measurement) and the CO-OCCURRENCE input of the shared expert-placement mint (`tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` --trace; LAW:coactivation-expert-placement). Same coverage laws as its weight twin below: the glm5 EP walk is wired in; the device-routed step TP walk refuses an armed tap by name |
| `MEMRA_MOE_WEIGHT_TRACE=<path>` | append every host-routed walk's per-(layer, forward) selected experts + weights, token-major (`<layer> <t> <expert:weight,...>`, t*k pairs/row). Two consumers: (1) the routing-divergence oracle behind the m-invariance razor (`concat_prime_probe`, `research/concat-prime-exact-20260802/`); (2) the HOTNESS signal of the shared expert-placement mint (`tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` --weight-trace: summed routing weight replaces pick count for seed/frequency/entry-rank choices; co-occurrence comes from the `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` id twin; maps land via `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP` — LAW:coactivation-expert-placement, lane/glm5-ep-place 2026-08-31). Rides the EXISTING host route readbacks (glm5's 42 router readbacks/token — zero new device syncs; the glm5 EP walk is wired into the tap too). Forces the stats-visible dispatch path, and the device-routed step TP walk (whose selection never returns to host) REFUSES an armed tap by name rather than silently dropping its rows — a trace missing whole layers poisons any placement mint built on it. ON receipts: `glm5-tp-gate` arm T — trace ON byte-identical to the banked plain walk in BOTH regimes, token events per MoE layer counted exactly (2*(P+N)). Diagnosis only, never a serving mode |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_LOGIT_TRACE=<path>` | write one bit-preserving real decode router-logit row per layer, with correction bias, original-id active mask, scaling, and normalization metadata. The explicit diagnostic performs a DtoH readback and is not a performance mode |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_REPLAY=<path>` | `kernel-check` input for the served-logit host-vs-device replay cell; malformed, duplicate-layer, selected-id, or routing-weight-bit disagreement fails the required manifest gate |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_DISPATCH_TRACE=1` | print every engagement of Step-3.7's resident zero-DtoH sigmoid dispatch arm, including layer, token count, clamp form, and gate/up interleave state |
| `MEMRA_DUMP_HN=<path>` | append pre-head hidden states per decode step (head-MIPS feasibility probe, offline bound analysis; both decode arms share the door so they stay observably identical). Diagnostic only |
| `MEMRA_F16G_DEBUG=1` | grouped f16 expert lane stage bisect: full NaN/Inf scans of w/act/y plus post-permute/post-scatter bad-value counts — localizes a corrupt stage. Adds D2H syncs; diagnosis only |
| `MEMRA_FA_V4_MAX=1` | force the v4 FA lane at every t_kv (bypass the crossover) — correctness-forcing knob for the fp8 lane matrix (2026-07-12 closure battery) |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT_GRAPH_CHECK=1` | re-run the gemma draft chain eagerly after each graph replay and diff the drafted slots (non-destructive replay-vs-eager bisect) |
| `MEMRA_E4B_GRAPH_GATE=N` (gemma-gate) | E4B graph-door stream gate: `generate()` door OFF then ON on fresh caches, streams must be identical (the warmup-side-effect + exec-update oracle) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_GRAPH=1` | 12B/31B whole-token alloc-free graph door (slotted decode step, captured once, replayed per token). Bit-identical (96/96 stream gate) but −5.6% vs dc-eager on the 12B (campaign closed 2026-07-23: ladder 81.3→84.8 vs eager 89.8; residual is GPU-side graph-exec scheduling, not allocs/launch cost). Experimental door, default OFF |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_GATE=N` (gemma-gate) | 12B/31B graph-door stream gate: eager vs graph token streams over N tokens must be identical + both throughputs printed |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_DRAIN=N` | raise the graph door's drain window above the pinned 1 (EXPERIMENTS ONLY: relaunching the same graph exec before its prior launch completes is ILLEGAL — chunk=4 reproduced ILLEGAL_ADDRESS) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_CENSUS=1` | print the captured graph's node-type census (kernel/memset/memcpy/alloc/free counts) at instantiate |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_CAPTIME=1` | phase-resolved capture cost per capture: `warmup2x / capture-body / instantiate / upload`. The split that killed the Q1 mem-node refactor — only instantiate+upload scale with node count (q27: 4.4 of 34.4 ms), the warmups are eager step executions and are node-count-invariant (`research/graph-allocfree-20260805/`) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_IFLAG={priority,upload}` | re-instantiate the generic captured decode graph under a non-`AUTO_FREE_ON_LAUNCH` flag. Measures the auto-free launch-time mem-pool scan in isolation; measured FLAT on both probe and serve c=1 paths (q27 167.94 vs 167.65 tok/s, N=5 interleaved), so the default stays AUTO_FREE. `upload` is rejected by the driver here — cudarc's `end_capture` instantiates without an upload stream (`CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_WARMUPS=N` | eager warmup runs before `capture_graph` captures (DEFAULT 1 since lane/graph-warmups 2026-08-05; min 1; `=2` = the rollback seam). The win: recapture −38% q27 / −41% q9 (pod) re-minted −42% q27 / −41% q9 on the 5090, decode +~1% both, capture+prime −13ms. Warmup 2's address-stability job (warmup 1 may grow/map the async pool; a stale capture reproduces the #68 baked-address class) is guarded structurally — balanced in-graph alloc/free pairs, retire-on-grow pools, capture-retain on the draft path — and was arbitrated adversarially: `tools/graph-warmup-stress-gate.sh` (pool-growth cycles both directions x10 + overlap arm + forced recaptures over freed blocks, bit-identity vs eager, canary teeth) GREEN at `=1` on the deployment rig. Receipts: `research/graph-warmups-5090-20260805/`, pod `research/graph-allocfree-20260805/logs/warmup-lever-N5.txt` |
| `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2=K` (gemma-gate) | CHAINED batched-verify oracle: prefix tokenwise, then two back-to-back `decode_step_t` calls — per-position argmax must match the tokenwise chain. `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2_DEV=1` runs the device-token verify arm (the spec round's exact path) |
| `MEMRA_ROUND_GRAPH_CHECK=1` | round-graph bisect: run the captured round body EAGERLY (no capture/replay) — splits body-semantics bugs from replay-mechanics bugs |
| `MEMRA_E4B_DCG_EAGER` | E4B door bisect arm: run the dcg step eagerly per token instead of capture/replay — `1` = exact live bucket, `2` = the capture's win bucket (separates bucket-path numerics from the replay mechanism) |
| `MEMRA_E4B_GRAPH_TIMING=1` | per-phase host timing of the E4B graph loop (dtoh-wait / fa_apply / launch sums) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_NODES_DUMP=1` | dump the captured E4B graph's kernel-node inventory (symbol + grid + count) + fa update-unit count at capture |
| `MEMRA_BURST_VCHECK=1` | run the gemma verify-STREAM on each eager round's batch before the eager verify and diff argmaxes + per-layer KV byte sums (the burst verify's in-place gate harness) |
| `MEMRA_GATE_SEED=<n>` | decode-batch-gate synthetic-prompt token-pattern offset (default 0 = the historic prompt). Cross-config drift is near-tie roulette on any single synthetic prompt, so calibration re-sweeps draw several seeds — gate1-config sweeps 6 and FAILs iff >= 4 of the 6 draws diverge before step 3 (the fraction rule; 2026-08-02 5090 recal, `research/gate1-recal-20260802/`). The per-draw step floor of the round-45 H100 rule ("any seed < step 3"; tie flips assumed 6+) did not transfer: 5090 dice land at steps 0/1/3/4, proven dice by strict-equalized bit-identity on the same draws. Plumbing (wrong token/KV) diverges at step 0-2 on EVERY draw — the fraction separates at margin 2 on both sides |
| `MEMRA_GATE_CANARY=1` | decode-batch-gate TEST-ONLY plumbing canary: feeds gate1's batched lane one wrong token at step 1 — every draw diverges early and the fraction rule MUST FAIL (the teeth check for gate recalibrations; also trips strict bit-identity, as a real plumbing bug would) |
| `MEMRA_EXACT16_WHY=1` | print WHY a checkpoint is refused the exact-16 serve tier: `decode_batch_exact16_ok` is an ALL over every matmul in the model, so one class without a bit-exact b16 kernel refuses the whole checkpoint back to chunk 8, silently. Prints `[exact16] REFUSED by L<n>.<tensor> qtype=<q> rp4=<bool>` per refusing tensor (plus architecture / MLA-mixer / MoE-ffn refusals, which are structural and can never be admitted). Default off, no cost when off. Added lane/rp-on-st 2026-08-06, where it found FOUR blocking classes one at a time on real MIXED checkpoints — NVFP4, Q8_0-base, Q4_K, Q5_K — that had kept chunk 16 unreachable for every shipped artifact; without it each "still refused" round was a guess (`research/rp-on-st-20260806/`) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE=0` | skip run-gen's batched-prime gate (#46): the last-position logits of `prime_cache` (the config that seeds real generation and serving) vs the tokenwise reference. A near-tie argmax flip is REPORTED non-fatally (the accepted cross-config drift class — measured 10/144 prompts across the six-model 2026-08-02 sweep, all at tokenwise margins <= 0.70; `research/prime-gate-coverage-20260802/`); a wide-margin flip or out-of-bounds drift FAILs. Dedicated multi-prompt battery: `prime-gate <model> --prompts-file f [--chat] [--strict]` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MAXDIFF` / `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MARGIN` | #46 verdict bounds (defaults 8.0 / 1.0, calibrated on the 2026-08-02 supported-set sweep: legal maxdiff up to 5.5 on gemma Q4_0, legal flip margins <= 0.70). Recalibrate when the kernels under them move — the H100 stale-verdict law |
| `MEMRA_TICK_TRACE=1` | memra-server: per-tick phase timing of the batched scheduler to stderr (diagnosis only) |
| `MEMRA_TTFT_TRACE=1` | memra-server: one `[ttft]` line per completion request with request-parse, HTTP admission, worker queue wait, tokenization, pre-prime setup, prime, first-decode wait, first serialized SSE body byte, cumulative boundary, and total timings. Debug-only; disabled requests allocate no trace state |
| `MEMRA_CONFIDENCE_TRACE=<path>` | memra-server: per-token confidence JSONL (`memra-token-confidence-v1`: reference logprob, top1 margin, entropy). Forces single-session serving and disables KV reuse + spec — a measurement harness mode, never a serving config |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1` | per-slot accept histogram + draft-length histogram |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_DEMOTE_AT=N` | **test door, NEVER set in a deployment**: force the spec→batched demotion handoff when a session reaches N generated tokens, regardless of active-session count, and demote ONLY that session. Exists because the shipped trigger (`active >= MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_HIGH`) cannot be used for an exactness test — load-triggered demotion is nondeterministic in BOTH arrival timing and batch composition, and batched decode is not bit-identical across batch shapes (`fa_decode_batch_seqs_v4` carries one `split_keys` for sessions at different depths, and the batched-linear tier changes with B — pre-existing, found by lane/spec-gate: a spec-OFF discriminator arm diverges from its own solo reference at bytes 2379/1347 on two runs with no gate code involved). The door pins B=1 with no load, which makes the handoff itself — pending flush, `(cache, next_pred)` transfer, position, stream continuation — the only variable. Used by `research/spec-gate-20260806/exactness.py` (arm DEMOTE_SOLO vs REF); logs `FORCED at DEMOTE_AT=N (test door)` so a forced demotion can never be misread as a policy one |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE=1` | per-round wall decomposition of the spec loop (draft / verify / accept+commit) — no tracing, no extra syncs (each phase is naturally sync-bounded); printed once at loop end via spec-stats. Built by the verify-economics lane (2026-08-02, `research/verify-economics-20260802/`) |
| **step37 serving defaults** (not a flag) | **OWNER FLIP 2026-08-27: the gated step37 serving doors default ON, family-armed.** `arm_step37_serving_defaults()` fires at model load when the plan compiles to the SlidingGatedMoe program (hybrid.rs, before any tensor upload or cache sizing reads a door) and flips the unset-default of: `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME`, `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER`, `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL`, `MEMRA_TCOL_FFN`, `MEMRA_SPEC_FA2`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`, `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`, `MEMRA_SWA_RING`, `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4`, `MEMRA_SPEC_HEAD_ROWS`, `MEMRA_Q8T_WONCE`. **`MEMRA_TN_PREJOIN` WAS REMOVED FROM THIS SET 2026-08-29** with the t-row verify program and the `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2` banks it required (see those rows; `research/step37-bankv2-removal-20260829`); it had been unreachable in serving since the bank-v2 boot refusal, so the armed set's behavior is unchanged. `MEMRA_TCOL_FFN` stays armed: its surviving effect is implying the `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ` defer. **`MEMRA_PRIME_TROWS` WAS REMOVED FROM THIS SET 2026-08-27** after the flip broke server priming: the walk's prime omits its sub-32 tail chunk from the distributed kv, so the server refuses before decode ("cache lengths diverged local=445 distributed=416") on any prompt whose length is not a multiple of 32. Isolated on the server route (defaults ERR / `MEMRA_PRIME_TROWS=0` OK); invisible to run-gen, which calls `decode_step_t` on the whole prompt and never enters `prime_cache`. Back to opt-in until the tail append is fixed and gated on the SERVER route. Other families are untouched (default stays OFF; the W8 mirror routing sits inside generic matmul paths, which is why the arm is per-family, not global). Every door is tri-state: `=1` forces ON for any family, `=0` is the kill switch — the rollback seam. RECEIPTS the flip stands on (all interleaved, all on the agentic-8 turn-8 shape, tip b2c722e5a..7a784b67c): plain 81.95 -> full stack K=3 **93.18 tok/s greedy (1.137x, byte tape == plain in every arm)** and **81.79 vs 78.50 sampled (1.042x**, vendor-default temp .6 / top_k 20 / top_p .95, engagement receipts drafted=127 accepted=97); run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with all doors on; prime/TTFT -10%. POLICY knobs are NOT armed here and stay serve-config: `MEMRA_SPEC_K=3 MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=3 MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN=0.5 MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0=1 MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM=<ranks> MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM_NVFP4=1` (trim needs an artifact path; the fence is a serving policy, not a kernel door) |

Its historical receipts (+8.06% median interleaved x5 on the bf16 arm, +7.6% serving-doors config, acceptance 111/88 identical, byte tape `013e01df2c769c0f302232b3`) were all measured WITH the v2 banks armed, a configuration that also carried the text corruption the bisect lane attributed; treat those rows as receipts for a dead configuration. |
| `MEMRA_Q8T_WONCE` | **DEFAULT ON (family-armed) since the 2026-08-27 owner flip — see the step37-serving-defaults row; `=0` kills.** Routes the q8 t-column verify kernels to their weight-once `_tw` twins. THE DEFECT: the `_t` forms put the verify column on blockIdx.y (a full row-grid copy per column) and load weights with `__ldcs` — streaming, evict-first — so the columns' fully-shared weights are re-read from DRAM once per column. Right at t=1, exactly wrong at t>1. NAMED BY THE NSYS KERNEL DIFF (2026-08-27, K=1 doors, per-round vs per-token): `qkv_rp_t` 36.5 vs 21.8 us = **1.67x** for 2 columns, `b4_rp_t` 29.0 vs 20.3 = **1.43x**, where weight-bound scaling says ~1.1x; the MoE pair (1.81x/1.84x) is at its structural union law and attention (1.36x) is fine, so these twins are the remaining walk-vs-plain-tick gap (~1.1 ms wall/round of the unattributed ~1.4). THE FIX: drop the column grid axis, load each weight int4 once, run t independent accumulator chains against it — per-column float program unchanged (same lane-strided blk order, own single-dependence chain, same warp_reduce; the LAW:vl-bit-identity-order-pinning pattern). Covers `qkv_rp_tw`/`b4_rp_tw`/`rows_tw`, t<=8. SIBLING NOT YET COVERED: `matvec_bf16_dual_silu_rows` shows the same 1.44x class on the bf16 shexp and gets its own arm after this one gates. Rollback: unset = the `_t` forms, byte-for-byte prior path. GATED 2026-08-27 (doors+head-rows, tip b2c722e5a): K=1 interleaved x5 off 78.85 mean [76.85, 80.10] -> on **82.54** [81.78, 82.95], +4.7%, no overlap — fixed-K=1 spec now MATCHES the plain tick (82.14) with no gating at all; K=3 69.49 -> 75.67 (**+8.9%**, the excess grows with t exactly as the __ldcs theory predicts); PRIME 25.15 -> 22.56 s (**-10.3% TTFT**) — unpredicted bonus, the chunked prime walks the same `_t` kernels at t=8 where the re-read excess was worst. Canonical tape == plain (83af249419adbd16587b7b53) in EVERY arm incl. both K=3; acceptance identical (K=1 88/111, K=3 121/234); run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with wonce+head-rows ON. Still owed for a default flip: the vendor-default sampled cell |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HEAD_ROWS` | **DEFAULT ON (family-armed) since the 2026-08-27 owner flip — see the step37-serving-defaults row; `=0` kills.** Batches the eager verify tail's LM head over its t columns instead of running it at m=1 once per column. THE DEFECT IT REMOVES: the eager tail loops `matmul(output, row, 1)` per column, so step37's LM head — ~0.49 GiB per card after the rank split — is re-streamed **t times per verify pass**, ~1.07 ms of pure re-read at t=2 and worse at every wider t. That is a large part of why the fixed ladder loses on wall (K=1 81.2 > K=2 73.1 > K=3 62.7 tok/s) even as tok/round rises (1.793 / 2.073 / 2.117), and it is ~43% of the measured 2.49 ms gap between the verify walk at t=1 (14.76 ms) and the plain decode tick (12.27 ms). WHY IT IS NOT THE ULP TRAP THE LOOP GUARDS AGAINST: that comment is about the batched **cuBLASLt** head, and a `FloatBf16` head at 1..=32 rows never reaches cuBLASLt — `matmul` routes it to `matvec_bf16_rows_into` (lib.rs:12248), whose doc states `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` "runs the t=1 decode head program PER ROW (identical dot + reduce), so decode/verify tiers keep the t=1 numeric class"; under the W8 doors both widths route to the q8 mirror and the t-column mirror is documented "bit-identical to t single-row calls". `rms_norm` is row-wise, so norm(t) is per-row identical to t x norm(1). Rollback seam: unset = the per-column loop, byte-for-byte the prior path. GATED 2026-08-27 (doors config, tip 0aac57e87): K=1 interleaved x5 off 77.22 mean [77.01,77.45] -> on 79.99 [79.81,80.14], **+3.6%, no overlap**; K=2 72.81 -> 75.54 (+3.7%); K=3 66.93 -> 69.47 (+3.8%). BYTE TAPE: plain == spec-off == spec-on = 83af249419adbd16587b7b53 (generated text only — hashing the log region falsely FAILED this gate three ways: the tok/s line, the [ttft] line, and the per-run "logit maxdiff" receipt, which legitimately moves with any numeric-path change while output is unchanged). Acceptance 88/111 identical both arms; verify-vs-decode logit maxdiff IMPROVED 9.145e-2 -> 8.716e-2; run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with the door ON. Model correction from the ladder: the saving is ~1.1-1.2 ms/round at every K — flatter than the ~1.07 ms/extra-column prediction (the m=1 read achieves better bandwidth than assumed) — so the fix does NOT re-rank the ladder; K=1 stays optimal. Still owed before the default moves: the vendor-default sampled cell |
| `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME` | **FAMILY-DEFAULT ON since 2026-08-28 — on the server route it is the only prime that works.** Measured on that route (not run-gen, which never enters `prime_cache`): walk = ERR ("cache lengths diverged"), fallback chunked prime = 29 s at ~450 tokens and a **90 s TIMEOUT at 4k**, grouped GEMM = **3.5-4.9 s** with coherent on-topic output. `=0` falls back. Both arms stated. Prime chunks (t>=16, prefill) route the routed MoE through ONE grouped f16 GEMM per projection over the RESIDENT NVFP4 banks (`run_tensor_parallel_routes_nvfp4_prime_grouped`) instead of the per-token device routes. WHY (TTFT lane, owner bar sub-second cold): the t-row walk primes 4,092 tokens in 19.8 s at its widest (GEMV-bound, road closed by the width cell); the generic batch prime's decode-class MoE measures 238-243 s at m=4092; the TP_PREFILL/EP_GROUPED doors land in that same slow path or refuse this layout (cell receipts 2026-08-27); and the CUTLASS sizing rows put GEMM-class expert math at 170-270 TFLOP/s here — a sub-second cold prime by arithmetic. MECHANICS: host CSR from the sigmoid router oracle (the EP arm's router), per-rank pointer tables into the bank shards, `moe_f16g_act` -> `moe_f16_grouped` (direct-from-NVFP4 lane, n_active<=512) -> silu pairs -> grouped down per rank; gate/up column-halves with per-expert macros applied BEFORE silu (nonlinear), down row-shard partials joined in the pinned canonical order, weighted scatter with a fixed slot-order per-token sum (no atomics); shexp via the canonical grouped add. NUMERIC CLASS: the f16-mirror grouped-prefill class other families serve; admission = prefill-KV acceptance gate + ship-shape tape + interleaved wall, never byte identity. Requires `MEMRA_MOE_F16G=2` for the ordered single-kernel lane. Rollback: unset = the walk. **`=0` REFUSED AT SERVER BOOT since 2026-08-28 (owner order): the fallback walk primes at ~57.5 ms/token, so every fresh prompt past ~1500 tokens dies on the 90 s first-token deadline (walk-only attribution arm: every row of a 1500+-token battery timed out). A rollback that cannot serve is an outage with a flag name; the server fails loudly at load (`dead_prime_kill_switch_refusal`, worker.rs) instead of booting the half-dead shape that answers /health while timing out real work. `=0` stays usable offline (run-gen, engine bins)** |
| `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME_SUFFIX` | **ON by default since 2026-08-29, by decision, under the flip bar the previous (OFF-era) version of this row wrote down.** That row closed the first lane OFF because its pre-registered byte-identity gate failed - and then attributed the failure correctly: the m-dependence is shared by EVERY measured prime path including the walk (0/2) and predates the door, so byte identity is not a usable gate for any prime path. It demanded three things of a future flip lane, and all three are now banked: (1) VENDOR-DEFAULT SAMPLED ROWS - the blind, rubric-pre-registered 8-turn quality A/B (`research/step37-sampled-quality-20260828`, rubric committed before any generation, 72/72 valid rows, per-row engagement receipts: gemm `ENGAGED base>0`, walk `WALK base>0`, cold fresh `base=0`): WARM-GEMM sits inside the COLD arm's own self-spread at t4 and t8, with the t8 claim carried at n=16 per arm (the round-1 walk-over-gemm signal collapsed under extension, Mann-Whitney p~0.91); loop/derail DQs are statistically equal across ALL arms including COLD, so they are a model-robustness property, not a door property. (2) THE 8-TURN CACHE-ON TWIN: warm-turn TTFT 0.58 s (door) vs 7.15 s (walk) on the real warm serving shape, zero faults. (3) THE BATCHED PRIME OWN STANDARD, not byte identity: acceptance 0.80-0.86 across all arms with the door arm highest at t8, interleaved arms, zero ILLEGAL/#87/panics in 19 boots, binary md5 in every results header. PRECONDITION SHIPPED FIRST: the SWA-ring checkpoint restore fix (`c9a617ca99`) - real session reuse crosses the grow path before the door matters, and that path panicked the worker until then. WHY IT IS WORTH IT, measured (five-point sweep, `research/gemm-suffix-20260828/PREDICTION.md`): walk continuation 0.2529 s + 5.5978 ms/suffix-token (R^2 0.9976) vs this path at 0.99 ms/token - a 7.97x suffix-slope collapse; warm multi-turn TTFT 0.6-0.7 s vs ~2.3 s door-off at short context, 2.3 s at 36-40k context (30k cell). MECHANISM: does a CONTINUATION prime (`cache.pos > 0` - a rewound session suffix or a prompt remainder split across scheduler ticks) ride the batched GEMM prime like a fresh prompt does? Default/`=1`: yes (GEMM trunk at m = chunk plus grouped NVFP4 MoE). ROLLBACK: `=0` puts continuations back on the walk (fresh primes keep the fast path); `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME=0` is still the whole-path seam. ENGAGEMENT RECEIPT, both directions: `[gemm-prime] ENGAGED t=.. base=..` with `base>0` is the suffix arm, `[gemm-prime] WALK t=.. base=..` counts the other; a log showing neither line is an instrument fault rather than a pass |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_PRIME_BATCH_TSEND` | off — CANARY SEAM restoring the pre-fix chunk-local `seq_end` in the step35 batched prime (`ts[s]`, this chunk's own length, where the request's absolute end belongs). Not a performance knob: `seq_end` is the only thing step35's SWA arm keys on (`swa && seq_end > win`, win = 512 here), so a chunk SHORTER than the window at a NONZERO base selected the UNWINDOWED FA arm over a view the `off` trim leaves at ~`win-1+t` rows — it attended OUTSIDE the sliding window. Worked example at the geometry the affinity lane measured (`base_len` 1440, `t` 254): `off = (1440-511) & !31 = 928`, `t_kv = 766`, and the unwindowed arm hands query 1693 all 766 causal keys from 928 when its window starts at 1182. **This fired on committed code with no continuation involved**: the chunk loop folds only a trailing chunk below `PRIME_MIN_T`, so a FRESH prompt of 4096+k for k in [16, 512) already ended in a chunk of exactly that shape at base 4096. Fixed by threading the request-absolute `seq_end` (`cache.pos + t + queued_after`, computed once above the batched entry, the same chunk-invariant value the walk has used since lane/step35-chunkfix) through `step35_prime_cache_batch` and `step35_prime_batch_layers`; the fix is unconditional, not gated on `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME_SUFFIX`. The seam is chunk- and suffix-VARIANT by construction, which is what gives the suffix byte-identity gate its teeth — the same canary role `MEMRA_STEP35_SWA_TKV` plays for the walk. Receipts: `research/gemm-suffix-20260828`. Never on in a measured default run |
