| `MEMRA_PP_STAGES=<N>` / `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS=<c1,..,cN-1>` (`MEMRA_PP_SPLIT=<layer>` = the N=2 spelling) | pipeline-parallel N-stage runtime (M2 generalization of the M1 seam): decode_step runs the trunk as N stage subgraphs, EACH ON ITS OWN CUDA STREAM, handing the [n_embd] f32 residual through persistent double-buffered boundary slots at every cut (transport per boundary: dtod same-device / cudaMemcpyPeerAsync cross-device, evented TX/RX). Cuts default to the even split. Generic eager arm at any N; gemma4 eager at N=2. **Path coverage as of 2026-08-06** (this list used to say "batch/dc/graph/spec warn-once", which was wrong twice over — see the correction in `pp.rs`'s header): eager **and** batched (`MEMRA_BATCH_PP`) **and** spec verify (`MEMRA_SPEC_PP`) each take their own stage split; `decode_step_dc` and the graph capture that wraps it still run UNSPLIT, and they do NOT warn — `warn_unwired_once` has exactly two call sites and both are gemma4-specific. What protects dc/graph instead is failing closed: under an open door with a sharded cross-device placement they refuse via `pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote` rather than silently peer-reading the remote stage's weights. Deferred-readback API (`decode_step_h_ppn_deferred` -> `PendingLogits`) keeps 2+ tokens in flight — scheduling only, math event-ordered per token | M1 (2026-08-01): BIT-IDENTICAL via pp2-gate, both models, every knob (`research/m1-pp2-20260801/`, `research/m1-inc2-20260801/`). M2 (2026-08-02, 8xH100): ppn-gate BIT-IDENTICAL at N=2/4/8 — SERIAL arm everywhere (the validated mode). PIPELINED (deferred) arm: same-device QUARANTINED (repro'd 35% co-located-stream race; the API refuses 2+ stage streams on one device, `MEMRA_PP_FORCE_SAME_DEV_PIPELINED=1` = soak override only) and cross-device ~1/70 flake (battery-5 dev01) — race root cause OPEN, deferred stays an experimental door until a x100+ clean soak; 1.87-1.88x decode at N=2/4 sharded is the prize (`research/m2-pp8-20260802/RESULTS.md`). Default OFF, zero behavior change unset |
| `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES=<d0>,..,<dN-1>` | ppN stage->device placement: stage s runs on device ds — the serving worker and `run-spec` primary Engine follow dN-1 (the HEAD stage's device: the sharded loader homes the lm head there and the spec round's draft chain + verify-logit consumers run on the primary, so any other pick peer-reads the head every draft token — the v0.72 112.5->17.5 spec+PP-2 regression; non-spec gate/bench binaries follow d0 by design), remote stages get their own Engine/context, KV for each layer range allocates on its stage's HBM (`Cache::new_ppn`), cross boundaries use cudaMemcpyPeerAsync on the publishing stream (M0: 2.8x NCCL at PP activation sizes; cuDeviceCanAccessPeer-guarded, peer-inaccessible = loud FAIL; peer + default-mempool access granted over every device pair in use). Ordinals are in CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES' process-local namespace; unset still defaults the worker to logical device 0. M2 increment 2: the LOADER uploads each stage's layer range (weights + decode mirrors) through that stage's engine — weights live where they run | default unset = all stages on the primary device. Cross-device gates on the 8x box: `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES=0,..,N-1 ppn-gate <model> <N>` |
| `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` | Broken-peer fallback for sharded cross-device PP: replace each cross-stage `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync` activation handoff with source-stream D2H into a geometry-sized page-locked host slot, an event, then destination-stream H2D. Two slots per boundary are allocated once from `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS * n_embd * sizeof(f32)` (64 MiB each for Step-3.7's `n_embd=4096`); decode and batch move only their exact payload prefix. Serving-time peer/pool grants are bypassed. When capability exists, boot diagnostics temporarily enable peer access and production-pool access to quote corrupt bytes, then revoke pool access and disable every probe-enabled peer pair before the host-staged path proceeds; teardown failure refuses startup. The same transport is now the automatic one-way runtime fallback after a native re-probe failure: the owner thread allocates the pinned slots, validates a real D2H/event/H2D row copy at every cross boundary, then atomically publishes host bounce before the next scheduler tick. Fail-closed safety doors reject `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0`, unsplit remote-weight walks, and stream-mode spec's shared token/position UVA reads; serving also disables spec admission plus cross-device prefix/affinity snapshots. | Default OFF at startup: healthy PIX/NVLink hosts retain the existing peer path byte-for-byte. A runtime safety failure may promote it without restart; native P2P stays latched off for the process. Explicit startup opt-in remains available for a known-bad peer host; `research/hostbounce-20260810/`, runtime promotion `research/probehitch-20260812/`. |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE=0` | Diagnostics-only escape hatch for PP-N's default-on byte-integrity gate. Normally every cross-device PP boundary is tested in both directions: a deterministic legacy-allocation 16 KiB `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync` preflight immediately after peer enable, then real stream-ordered `BoundarySlot` TX/RX copies at 1, 8, 16, and `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` (4096) `[n_embd] f32` rows after pool grants and before weight upload. The final boot rung is one maximum production prime handoff (64 MiB at Step-3.7's `n_embd=4096`). Destination bytes are read back and compared exhaustively; logs include the largest clean payload. Native serving gives each runtime width its own staggered copy-count deadline (one width due per 8,192 native boundary TX copies, each width recurring every 32,768), so an overdue idle-only rung cannot block later cheap integrity checks. The maximum-production rung is idle-only before its first measurement; any other rung whose complete owner-thread pass exceeds the fixed 5 ms budget becomes idle-only on later cycles. Idle-only work runs only with no admitted, queued, or constraint-compilation work, after an empty command-channel check; late deadlines collapse rather than burst-catch up. A runnable cheap re-probe defers while a live stream-mode spec session still peer-reads token/position and verify state through UVA: forcing it is unsafe because a mismatch immediately revokes native peer access before host bounce is published, while host bounce covers boundary activations rather than those live spec pointers. Deferrals count once per 8,192-copy interval, not once per scheduler poll. `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE_DEFERRAL_BOUND` controls the consecutive-interval alarm and speculative soft-refusal point (default 4, one complete 32,768-copy rotation). At the bound, operator metric `peer_probe_integrity_degraded=true` and `SECURITY RED` publish, `/readyz.peer_probe_integrity` becomes `degraded`, and new speculative candidates take the existing plain path. Existing speculative sessions finish naturally; native transport is unchanged and no probe is forced under their live UVA state. Their retirement creates a safe boundary where a completed re-probe clears the streak and reopens speculative admission. Before the bound `/readyz` reports `deferred_<n>`; otherwise it reports `ok`. `peer_probe_deferred_total` remains cumulative. If recovery does not occur, drain speculative sessions or restart with `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0`, then require `peer_probe_runtime_reprobes` to increase and the advisory to clear; disabling the probe is never remediation. A runtime mismatch or probe error at a safe boundary is SECURITY RED and one-way latches native P2P off. New admissions run plain after that transport degradation. The worker continues on host bounce only after pinned staging passes its real copy/readback validation; it panics only if that staging cannot be armed or validated. `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE=0` REFUSES a sharded cross-device native placement when `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE!=1`; with explicit host bounce it logs SECURITY RED and increments operator metric `peer_probe_bypassed`. Probe-off remains harmless for single-device or non-sharded diagnostic placements. Operator metrics expose native boundary copies, completed runtime probes, failures, deferrals, current overdue-integrity state, and `peer_probe_degraded_to_host_bounce`. | Default unset/on. Never disable in serving; use only to isolate probe-specific bring-up failures, with `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` on sharded cross-device placement. Design and vendor basis: `research/cx-peerprobe-20260811/DESIGN.md`; large-rung extension and costs: `research/sec9-20260812/`; runtime scheduling/failover: `research/probehitch-20260812/`; bounded deferral: `research/probeobs-20260812/`; bounded self-recovery: `research/probeready-20260812/`. |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE_DEFERRAL_BOUND=<n>` | Consecutive deferred runtime-probe copy intervals allowed before `/readyz.peer_probe_integrity` becomes `degraded` and new speculative candidates are served plain until a completed probe clears the streak. This changes admission only: existing speculative sessions and all plain admissions continue, and it never authorizes a probe under live UVA state. Read once by the worker at startup. | Default `4`; minimum `1` (`0` clamps to `1`); invalid values use `4`. Four is the probeobs one-rotation safety bound; raise only with an explicit integrity-coverage policy. |
| `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0` | rollback to the M1 bring-up weight placement: everything uploads through the primary engine; remote stages peer-read weights over NVLink/PCIe | rollback seam only; sharded placement is the default whenever `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES` is set (gated bit-identical both ways at N=2/4/8) |
| `MEMRA_PP_OVERLAP` | ppN double-buffered boundary slot ALTERNATION: TX(t+1)'s write-after-read guard waits only the previous use of ITS slot, so another wave may enter stage 0 while the prior wave's later stages run. Scheduling structure only — every wave's math is event-ordered. Unset FOLLOWS the dual-PP mode since the 2026-08-11 default flip: unset resolves ON under the naked default (Auto), OFF under `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=0` and OFF under the explicit `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=1` request. Explicit `1`/`0` always wins. PP-3/4 `MEMRA_PP_WAVE=1` consumes this resolved value too; `=0` therefore keeps the serial PP-N rollback even when the wave door is requested. | default follows dual-PP mode (ON naked); PP-2 gated bit-identical (ppn-gate arm 2, decode-batch-gate dual matrix, box1 re-gate soak); PP-3/4 qualification pending |
| `MEMRA_DUAL_PP` | **default ON (Auto) since 2026-08-11 (owner flip)** — dual-active plain-decode for PP-2. Three states: unset = Auto (dual runs exactly where the box1 re-gate validated it — PP-2 fence, double-slot boundary, peer transport, B>=2 — and silently degrades to the serial PP-N walker everywhere else, incl. naked PP-3 and `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1`); `0` = serial rollback seam (also resolves unset overlap OFF, restoring the exact pre-flip naked path); `1` = the explicit pre-flip request, which ROUTES every B>=2 PP-2 call into the dual body so an ineligible placement refuses loudly with the binding quoted reason (single-slot, host-bounce) before any token or cache advance. The worker treats the model's exact batch cap as a per-wave cap and schedules up to two caps in one tick with a balanced midpoint (A=`ceil(c/2)`, B=`floor(c/2)`); c=1 remains the honest serial path. The dual body prepares both slots up front and calls only the always-alternating `prepare_overlap_slots` / `tx_pipelined` primitives. `/metrics.dual_pp` (operator-only): completed slot pairs, per-slot uses, rejected same-slot collisions, timing, topology. `MEMRA_DUAL_PP_TIMING=1` adds CUDA-event spans (never fails decode; excluded from scored runs). | FLIPPED to default after the box1 PRO-pair re-gate (research/dualpp2-20260811/RESULTS-regate.md): bit-identity B=1..5, servestress no-thrash (admission delta serial==dual 0/0/0), 10-boot soak 929/929 golden per arm + 0 slot collisions across 9123 pairs; perf floor receipts research/dualpp1-20260811/RESULTS.md (c8 +20.753% min, rising to c10 +30.5%). Safety regressions: pp.rs flip_* tests, worker.rs flip_naked_default test, kernel-check `dual-pp-default-flip` manifest cell. |
| `MEMRA_PP_WAVE` | **OFF by design.** Strict `0`/`1` PP-3/PP-4 request and prime wavefront. Empty, non-UTF-8, or any value other than exact `0`/`1` refuses. `1` splits a decode tick into at most one balanced wave per stage; one scoped host worker owns each non-head stage and the caller owns the head. Prompt microchunks use the same dependency schedule. Concurrent work shares neither a stage Engine/scratch pool nor a request/cache. Every boundary carries two explicit credits over the existing grow-only slots: upstream cannot reuse a slot until downstream `rx` records `ev_rx` for that exact `(wave,slot)` and returns its acknowledgement. Server admission charges stage-local context and per-stage learned fixed high-water; it projects only missing process-global boundary capacity, including the enabled Step concat-prime maximum, instead of charging already-allocated slots to every session. It requires distinct devices, resolved `MEMRA_PP_OVERLAP=1`, native peer transport, a qualified batched decode rewrite, the stage-split prime path, and a ModelPlan-supported pipeline program. **W4A16 artifacts with preserved BF16 non-expert weights also require `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1`:** the wave decomposition narrows B=4/B=8 cells, and the default f32-expanded cuBLAS reduction is width-dependent. Without the row-wise BF16 program the engine refuses before mutating caches. Generic dense/non-Step concat-prime is excluded on cross-device PP and falls back to individual wavefront primes. Any failed mutating wave taints all participating caches and they cannot retry, demote, or enter a reuse pool. `=0` is the rollback to the existing serial PP-N walker; PP-2 continues to use `MEMRA_DUAL_PP`. Operator metrics: `/metrics.pp_wave` ticks/cells/real host-walker overlaps. | Unmeasured behavior does not default ON. CPU gates prove strict parsing, complete/no-alias wave coverage, exact slot-credit acknowledgements, bidirectional worker failure/closure propagation, whole-walk/deferred-window ownership, N-stage cache move/taint behavior, admission arithmetic, sampled/masked wave engagement, metric isolation, build integration, and the W4A16 BF16 refusal. Exact RTX PRO 6000 topology gates are still required before a default. HY3 receipt 2026-08-30: BF16 door off failed every B=4/B=8 and sampled/masked logit row; `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1` restored bit identity with live overlap. Design: `docs/decisions/PRO6000-MULTICARD.md`. |
| `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` | ppN rollback seam to the increment-1 same-stream boundary (two plain dtod copies per cut, no per-stage streams/events/devices; generalized to N) | rollback only; the per-stage stream choreography is the default when the door is open |
| `MEMRA_PP_EXIT_PUBLISH=0` | **rollback/control seam, DEFAULT ON** (lane/glm5-accrace 2026-09-01): a ppN body that returns to its caller publishes EVERY stage stream to the caller's stream on the way out (`PpNRt::publish_all_to` — event waits, never a device sync), not just the producing last stage. Wired at the hc prime (`prime_cache_hyper_ppn`), the glm5 spec verify walk (`glm5_verify_rows_ppn`) and the glm5 per-stage rollback (`glm5_verify_rollback`). WHY IT IS ON: a body's terminal drain settles the last stage, and the TX-wait chain reaches each earlier stage only as far as its `ev_tx` — every earlier stage's stream still holds the tail its stage-scope locals enqueue when they drop under the override (`pos`, boundary residual, per-layer transients, a verify round's ckpt clones). The caller then resumes and ALLOCATES, and cudarc's drops carry no read guard, so the pool could hand the caller a block whose stage-stream lifetime had not retired. `=0` restores the pre-lane program exactly and is a KNOWN-RACY control arm, never a serving configuration. Read per call, never memoized, so a gate A/Bs both arms in one process. | Measured on the local 5090 under deliberate capped host load, per-stage streams on one device: with `=0` the hc ppN PRIME of a fixed 24-token prompt returned THREE distinct logit fingerprints inside a SINGLE process (32/110 primes non-canonical against the `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` truth, 11/11 canonical there), and `glm5-spec-ppn-gate [E forced-rejection sweep K=7]` silently lost one acceptance (14/42 -> 13/42) with the e2e tape diverging, 3-6/20 reps. Default ON: 0 deviations and 0 sweep failures. New gate arm `P0 prime-determinism` is the standing teeth. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/accrace-20260901/LANE.md` |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_PP=0` | **rollback seam, DEFAULT ON** (pp2-batch 2026-08-06): with the ppN door open the BATCHED decode step takes its own stage split (`decode_step_batch_ppn`) exactly as the eager step does. `=0` sends the batched path back through the unsplit body, where a sharded cross-device placement is then caught by `refuse_unsplit_if_remote` (the 28x peer-read regime) rather than running silently. Read per step and never memoized, so the bit-identity gate can A/B split-vs-unsplit IN ONE PROCESS against the same loaded weights | Exactness: `decode-batch-gate --mode pp` 0 differing bits on all 7 configs (dev01/dev10/singledev/split5/N=4/q27/wide). Cost: 0.5-1.5% at B=4/8/16, transport 0.986-0.997x of the seam. `research/pp2-batch-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PP=0` | **rollback seam, DEFAULT ON** (pp2-spec 2026-08-06): with the ppN door open the spec VERIFY forward (`decode_step_t_core_ppn`, T=K+1) takes its own stage split. `=0` sends verify back through the unsplit trunk walk, caught by the same guard. Read per verify call, never memoized, for the same in-process A/B reason. **This is exactness plumbing, not the serving engagement policy** — `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` decides whether PP-2 admits spec, and defaults it off on throughput evidence. | Exactness: `decode-batch-gate --mode ppspec` 7/7 arms ALL GREEN. The #87 reverse-publication fix closed the old fatal concurrency placement (212/212 requests at c=2..8); v0.72 made placement orders throughput-symmetric. Spec remains callable through `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0`, but loses every current PP-2 c=1/2/4 policy cell. `research/pp2-spec-20260806/`, `research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`, `research/specplace-20260808/` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PP=0` | **rollback seam for the PRIME (chunked prefill) stage split** (lane/pp-leverb 2026-08-08): with the ppN door open the chunked prime takes its own per-stage range walk, like the eager/batched/verify steps. `=0` sends prime back through the unsplit whole-trunk walk. Unlike the other four paths prime keeps **NO** `refuse_unsplit_if_remote`: its unsplit walk over a sharded placement is the measured **22% amortized peer-read tax** (m=4096 amortizes the weight reads — not the decode 28x cliff), and it is the `prime-split-gate`'s REFERENCE arm, so it must stay callable. Read per call, never memoized (the gate A/Bs both arms in one process) | Gate: `prime-split-gate` (`ppsplit`/`ppsplitc` fast-gate rows) — split-vs-unsplit bit-identity (last-row logits, h_seed, [T,n_embd] hiddens, teacher-forced decode steps over the primed KV) PLUS the `pp::PRIME_SPLIT_CHUNKS` liveness counter. Registered RED 2026-08-08, **GREEN at the walker commit** (`prime_chunk_ppn`): SPLIT BIT-IDENTICAL + LIVE at T=4883, canary teeth proven. Perf: pp4096 **141.4 → 266.1 tok/s (1.88x)**, N=5 interleaved. `research/pp-leverb-20260807/` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PIPE=0` | rollback seam for PP-2 prime **chunk pipelining** (lane/cx-pipeline-prime 2026-08-08). Unset keeps the pipelined schedule when the prime stage split has at least two chunks; `=0` retains Lever B's serial chunk walk over the same effective chunk boundaries, including the naked PP-2 auto geometry above. Scheduling only: the serial split remains the bit-exact arithmetic oracle | Gate extends `ppsplit` with serial-split vs pipelined replay plus concurrent-active-walker `pp::PRIME_PIPE_OVERLAPS` liveness; receipts `research/pipeprime-20260808/` |
| `MEMRA_PP_ALLOW_UNSPLIT_BATCH=1` | MEASUREMENT DOOR: re-admits **all four unsplit decode paths** — `decode_step_batch`, `decode_step_dc`, the graph capture that wraps dc, and `decode_step_t*` spec verify (one door, one shared guard `pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote`, because it is one measurement question) — under an open ppN door with a sharded cross-device placement. Since 2026-08-06 that combination **refuses** (fails closed), because the batched trunk has no stage split — it walks all layers on the primary stream and peer-reads every remote stage's weights each step. Exactness is unaffected (peer reads return identical bytes and all three `decode-batch-gate` gates PASS), so the previous behavior was a silent perf cliff with a green battery: measured **7.4 vs 208.9 tok/s at B=1 (28x), 47.4 vs 657.0 at B=8 (13.9x)** on 2x RTX PRO 6000 over Gen5 x16 P2P. Same fail-closed precedent as the deferred-pipelined arm's same-device refusal | pp2-hardening 2026-08-06, receipts `research/pp2-hardening-20260806/` (RESULTS.jsonl, `logs/batchcost/`). Non-measurement escapes: `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0` (weights all home — restores full speed, forfeits the capacity PP-2 exists for) or close the pp door for batched serving. **Superseded in part the same day**: "wiring a real stage split is the open weeks-class item" was true when this door landed, and is no longer — `MEMRA_BATCH_PP` (batched) and `MEMRA_SPEC_PP` (verify) both ship split and default ON, so the door now only covers `decode_step_dc`, its graph wrapper, and explicit `*_PP=0` rollbacks. Gate: 20 arms across 5 binaries, 0 unexpected results — incl. `devices=0,0` NOT refusing (over-broad-predicate check) and `run-spec` acceptance rates identical with the override on vs the door shut (proving it is a door, not a behavior change) |
| `MEMRA_EAGLE` / `MEMRA_EAGLE_ALIGN=0` | EAGLE draft lane (run-eagle bin; ALIGN=0 = un-shifted MTP-style pairing A/B) | experimental lane, not on the daily path |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_DRAFT_GRAPH=1` | gemma draft chain replays as ONE captured CUDA graph (keyed kr/rung/window-regime; pos slots + g_seed are eager-filled graph inputs) | steady-state perf ~0 (launch tax hidden at 96.7% busy) — exists as the BURST enabler; capture-retain allocator mode ships with it (2026-07-12) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_SPEC_BURST=<M>` (+`_DRAFT_GRAPH=1`) | pre-issue M full spec rounds with ONE host sync per burst (verify-stream + device accept/seed/rollback/ring on the round_stream generics) | EXACT (stream 128/128 short+depth, 26B+31B) but PERF-NEGATIVE single-stream — no draft/verify overlap materializes on one stream and fixed-K costs the adaptive policy (26B short 304 vs 379; 31B 74 vs 88). Default 0. Stage 2 = second-stream speculative draft(N+1) under verify(N) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_ROUND_GRAPH=1` | the WHOLE gemma spec round (draft chain + stream verify + device accept/seed/rollback/commit + `spec_adapt_k` device adaptive depth) captured once per regime, ONE graph launch per round | EXACT (26B 64/64 short + 128/128 depth, 31B 64/64) with BETTER round dynamics (tok/round 5.33 vs 4.74) but PERF-NEGATIVE at fixed shapes (−10/−11% interleaved, 2026-07-13) — the replay pays fixed k_cap drafts + fixed-width verify, not launch gaps (the enqueue-ahead law, 4th confirmation). Door kept for the exec-update-width phase 2; 31B pair pending a valid window |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_BURST_GRAPH=1` | capture the gemma DRAFT CHAIN as one graph per (k_cap, dc bucket, window regime) inside the spec burst loop (`gemma_spec.rs`) | experimental door beside `MEMRA_GEMMA_SPEC_BURST` — same lane, same verdict class; default off |
| `MEMRA_G4PLAIN_EAGER=1` | gemma4 PLAIN-serving graph loop bisect: run the step body eagerly every step (no capture/replay) — splits "body semantics wrong" from "replay mechanics wrong" (the round-graph law; `gemma_spec.rs`) | bisect seam, not a config |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER=1` | downgrade the unknown-pre-tokenizer **hard load error** to a loud per-load WARN, then run the `qwen35` split anyway (`memra-tokenizer/src/lib.rs`; supported: `qwen35`, `qwen2`, `deepseek-v3`, `gemma4`, `glm4` — gemma-4's absent `pre` key is allowlisted explicitly, on a tensor-census audit, not by falling through; `glm4` is recognised from the tokenizer.json Split regex, because the GLM checkpoints carry `pre = default`) | **Produces WRONG TOKEN IDS by construction.** Before 2026-08-19 this was the DEFAULT behaviour and it was silent: one `eprintln!` per process, then a fall-through to the qwen35 split — the model loaded, generated fluent text, and every id was wrong. Same fluent-and-invisible class as the GGUF chat-template mint trap. Wrong ids poison goldens, parity fixtures, acceptance counts and every quality number downstream. Set it ONLY for deliberate bring-up experimentation on an unported family, and never for a run whose numbers will be quoted (lane/engine-hardening-20260819) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_FA_ONE_PROGRAM=1` | route dense-gemma SWA prefill attention through the WINDOWED arm at EVERY prompt length instead of switching program at `t > sliding_window` (`hybrid_forward.rs gemma_fa_one_program()`) | **Names a live prefix-restore exactness violation, and does not fix it by default.** Below the switch the SWA layers attend unwindowed on f32 operands, above it windowed on bf16, so rows `[0..932)` of a 932-row prime and of a 1048-row prime differ in **59 of 60 layers** — the published prefix KV depends on total prompt length. `=1` collapses it to one program so it stops depending on length. **Default OFF** because collapsing changes the bytes short prompts get today; the flip is an owner call (lane/gemma-restore-exactness-20260819) |

### Diagnostics for the gemma prefix-restore arc (lane/gemma-restore-exactness-20260819)

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_KVPROBE_AT=<n[,n…]>` | digest the FIRST `n` rows of a LIVE cache, with `n <= cache.pos` allowed (`worker.rs kvprobe_ats()`). `prefix_cache_state_digest` refuses `n != pos`, which makes it unable to answer the question the arc turns on: are the KV bytes a COLD 1048-row prime writes for rows `[0..932)` the same bytes a 932-row prime wrote — and therefore published, and therefore restored? Unset = the probe costs nothing |
| `MEMRA_KVPROBE_LAYERS=1` | with `..._AT` set, also emit the per-layer digests instead of the whole-state digest alone (`worker.rs kvprobe_layers()`) — how the 59-of-60-layer divergence above was localized |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_FAULT=alloc-fail\|flip-demote` | host-tier fault injection (lane/kv-host-spill-20260830), default unset = off, so the tier's FAILURE paths are executed by a gate instead of asserted in prose. `alloc-fail`: every pinned host alloc reports failure, exercising the loud latch-off (`[prefix-host] TIER DISABLED`, `prefix_host_rejected_allocs`, no pageable fallback). `flip-demote`: flips one K byte of the first demoted plane AFTER the demote digest is recorded, exercising the `MEMRA_KV_HOST_VERIFY` promote mismatch (`[prefix-host] VERIFY FAILED`, entry dropped, cold path serves). Consumed by `tools/kv-host-spill-failure-gate.sh`. NEVER set on a serving box: `flip-demote` intentionally corrupts host-tier bytes (harmless only because the gate pairs it with verify-on, which refuses the promote) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_OOM_FAULT=<n>` | **default absent/0 = OFF by design**: step-OOM fault injection (lane/kv-battery-fixups-20260831). Forges a QUOTED synthetic CUDA OOM (`CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY (synthetic: MEMRA_STEP_OOM_FAULT diagnostic door)`) on the next `n` phase-(a) session steps, at exactly ONE injection point (the step dispatch; the step itself is skipped, no device work runs on a card the gate is pretending is full). Everything downstream is UN-DOCTORED production logic: the park-vs-honest-error match, the `oom_teardown` fence, `park_requeue`, the `MEMRA_STEP_OOM_RETRIES` budget. WHY IT EXISTS: the 2026-08-31 pod battery proved the `[admit-oom] step OOM parked session back to queue` branch UNREACHABLE from outside the process: four refusal layers (KV-capacity 429, VRAM defer, TTFT deadline on queued requests, boot-time pool reservation) catch every external pressure shape first (darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/battery-20260831/tenancy-gates/RESULTS.md`, T2), leaving the branch whose multi-active form was fleet-fatal before lane/step37-vram-admission with no executable gate (loud-failures / executed-failure-path laws). `1` proves the park branch (fresh session parks LOUDLY, the fire line names the door, requeues, re-primes, answers 200); `n > MEMRA_STEP_OOM_RETRIES` walks one session into the bounded-retry honest error. Both arms weighed: default OFF because an armed door fails real customer steps by design; unset/`0`/malformed (loud warning) are byte-identical to today. Receipts: the `[admit-oom] MEMRA_STEP_OOM_FAULT fired` line, the arming WARN at first use, the door name quoted inside every downstream error line, `step_oom_parks` in `/metrics`. NEVER set on a serving box |

| flag | door | status |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_WGMMA=1` | hand-rolled wgmma GEMM v0 (unpipelined 64x64 tile, `wait_group<0>` every 32-K step) instead of MMQ | ~3x SLOWER per launch at m=512 model shapes (the "688us ref" was a pp2048-shape figure); default stays MMQ until a pipelined version beats it N=5. Correctness pinned regardless — kernel-check's wgmma case is cfg-gated, not env-gated (`lib.rs wgmma_gemm_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_P1=1` | non-windowed prefill FA P1 arm at hd256: the engine-study body (FA2 schedule + boundary split + swizzle), bf16 pre-convert | opt-in until the qwen battery (`lib.rs fa_prefill`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_PP_W2=1` | 2-warp/32-row CTA tile for the f32 pp prefill FA — doubles grid.x, bit-identical per-row math, pure coverage trade for the 6.25%-occupancy starvation | ncu probe 2026-07-26; also disables the batched varlen FA arm while set (`lib.rs`, `hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SP16=1` | gemma FA-decode split ladder alternative: t_kv-keyed {16, 64, 128} instead of the mixed default | measured on 188-SM rigs only (rig-divergence law — the 5090 ladder keeps its validated default); probe seam (`lib.rs fa_split_keys`) |
| `MEMRA_Q80_CA=1` | q8_0 `rpca` cp.async-staged weight-ring matvec (needs in_f % 1024 == 0) | MEASURED NEGATIVE on H100 2026-07-26 (181.8 vs plain rp 185.5 — the smem round-trip exceeds the latency it hides for 8-bit direct-dp4a); opt-in for the corpus |

| `MEMRA_Q40_MR=1\|2` | q4_0 m=1 / fused-t=1 rows-per-thread override | default mr1 at the plain m=1 site; the fused-t=1 site defaults per-model (`FUSED_MR1_DEFAULT`: dense gemma mr1, MoE mr2) — env wins both (`lib.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_Q40_BV=base\|r2\|ms\|sm\|la` | force ONE q4_0 batched-verify variant (the q4_0 twin of the documented `MEMRA_MMVQ_BV`/`MEMRA_KQ_BV`) | `ms`/`sm`/`la` are force-only measurement arms — ALL FLAT/NEGATIVE 2026-07-13, never auto (`lib.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_BVAR=<variant>` | force the batched-variant pick for the WHOLE process across qtypes — the sm_90a re-tune sweeps this seam empirically (auto heuristics were tuned on sm_120) | layout stays safe: an rp weight only accepts rp variants (`lib.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_RMS_BLOCK=<n>` | force the rms-norm kernel block size (default = the per-model `RMS_BLOCK_DEFAULT`) | tune seam (`lib.rs rms_block()`) |

| `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_ROWWISE=1` | force Qwen35-class spec verify onto the per-row correctness reference (T full weight reads per layer) instead of the t-parallel arm | t-parallel is the default for the qwen35 batched-serving numeric class at t<=16 — Qwen35 dense AND Qwen35Moe since 2026-08-20 (lane/draftcost-moe; rowwise on the 35B-A3B measured ~5.6 ms/drafted token, the (K+1)-plain-steps wall). Non-class archs and t>16 take rowwise anyway; ROWWISE=1 stays the exactness oracle/rollback seam (`spec.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_W8A8_SIM=1\|2` | W8A8 numeric-class PILOT on the f16 lane via fake-quant: `1` = weight-side int8, `2` = + per-TOKEN int8 activation fake-quant (slow host roundtrip) | pilot instrument for the owner-gated w8a8 accuracy decision (§7 refuted-list context); never a serving mode (`f16_ffi.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_COPY_H2D=1` | worker-mode spill: promote completed positioned reads to H2D at safe boundaries on a dedicated caller-thread stream (H2D starts while CPU workers finish later reads); enables the t=1 disk-prefetch promotion in the MoE forward | experimental, default off (`spill_pread.rs`, `moe_cache.rs`) |

### The DFlash draft-head block (`memra-engine/src/dflash.rs`; gemma-gate cell AND the shared drafter behind the `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC` serve route)

DFlash is the block-diffusion drafter bring-up (DFLASH-BRINGUP-PLAN.md, 2026-07-13): a 5-layer
qwen3-class mini-transformer drafting a 16-token block in ONE non-causal forward, conditioned on
the target's tapped hidden states. The gemma-gate cell is armed by **`MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH=<dflash dir>`**
— which REFUSES when combined with `MEMRA_DRAFT` or `MEMRA_GEN_CORPUS`/`_OUT`
(different drafters / different runs; the wrong branch would win silently — 2026-08-16 incident).
The same `dflash.rs` `DflashDraft` is ALSO what the serving-side dspark route loads
(`MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1`, worker.rs — one `DflashDraft` per non-gemma model; catalogued in §5),
so this file does reach HTTP traffic when that door is armed.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC` | `q4` | drafter weight precision: `q4` = all q4_0 (DEFAULT since the head-trim lane 2026-08-25, owner-ratified — PRO 6000 gate x3 157.9 vs q8 152.4 spec tok/s at unchanged acceptance 0.662/0.656, ALL EXACT; 5090 masked-head ABBA 83.85 -> 87.10 E2E tok/s); `q8` = all q8_0 (1.6 GB, the pre-flip default = rollback seam); `mixed` = bf16 attn+fc (the ctx-conditioning path) + q8_0 ffn (~2.2 GB); `bf16` = all bf16 (parity runs, no target). Boot prints `[dspark] precision=<resolved> (MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC set\|unset)`; unknown values, including the measured-defective `q5`, refuse rather than silently falling back to q8. |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T` | 8 (clamp 2..block) | verify width: the drafter always drafts the full block (its trained mask pattern) but only the first vt rows go through target verify — t=16 rides the untuned b16 tier (~65 ms/verify) while b8 rides the tuned r2 tier, and with ~2.7 committed/round the deep positions almost never survive. Exactness unaffected (verify still decides every committed token) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT` | on | adaptive verify width (the MTP accepted+1 recipe): next round verifies one past this round's accepted run, clamp [3, cap]; `0` = fixed width. On a head-carrying dspark checkpoint the resolved default policy is `confidence-slot` (below); `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0` is read as an explicit fixed-window request and keeps the ladder-family arm |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_HARVEST` | checkpoint census | draft-row harvest convention for DFlash-family block drafters: `dspark` = shifted all-rows (anchor row's output = draft 1), `dflash` = historical mask-fill rows 1..b-1. Resolution is FAMILY-keyed first, then strategy-keyed (v0.100 train): a DFlash2 checkpoint is mask-fill BY CONSTRUCTION (reference `dflash_generate` harvests rows `1-verify_size:`) — it always harvests `dflash`, and `dspark` on a DFlash2 checkpoint REFUSES loudly (census-keyed, never env-keyed). Every other checkpoint: UNSET resolves from the CHECKPOINT's own training-strategy census (`architectures` naming a DSpark class / `dflash_config.projector_type == "dspark"`) — owner-ratified flip 2026-08-20 after B1 (mismatched convention = the accept 2.9→1.43 collapse, DSPARK-POSTMORTEM-20260820). Unknown values refuse; the resolved harvest is printed at drafter load (`[dspark] harvest=… (checkpoint census dflash2=… strategy_dspark=…)`) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VT` | `confidence-slot` (head census) | verify-window policy: `confidence-slot` = longest draft prefix whose EVERY slot clears tau on the accept-rate head's own sigmoid (owner directive "take only high confidence offers"); `confidence` = cumprod-survival threshold; `ladder` = the reactive accepted+1 ladder. UNSET defaults to `confidence-slot` when the checkpoint carries the accept-rate head (owner-ratified H4 flip 2026-08-20: 94–98% of fixed-8 accept at wall ≥ ladder, exactness 11/11) and to `ladder` when it does not (nothing to schedule with) or when `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0`. Resolved policy printed at drafter load (`[dspark] verify-window=…`) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VT_TAU` | 0.5 | confidence threshold in (0,1) for both confidence window arms, including the resolved default (H4 cell-3 tau ladder: τ=.5 is the knee for the slot arm; τ=.35 is the wall-max point for the survival arm). Bad values refuse |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_BATCH_SMOKE=N` | off | dsv4 CPU dspark gate (`dsv4_dspark_gate` bin) only: truncate the batched-vs-sequential comparison to N new tokens — a fast-machinery smoke for the gate itself, never a serving knob. Arrived with the it5 merge; unmasked when the v0.98 scope-note carve-out narrowed to `MEMRA_DSV4_*` (main CI's census caught it — the narrowing doing exactly its job) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH=N` | unbounded | dsv4 serve door (lane/dsv4-flash-revival-20260822): cap the per-round draft depth on the spec route — exactly the bench drivers' depth policy, exposed at the serving seam. Identity-gated: every depth reproduces the plain route token-for-token (rung-1/rung-3 receipts in darklanes) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT=off\|fixed@K\|slot@TAU` | off | dsv4 serve door: the confidence-window verify policy (dspark vtconf H4). `off` = full-depth ladder verify; `fixed@K` = fixed window; `slot@TAU` = per-slot confidence window at tau (owner-directive per-slot arm; the head emits MARGINAL accept probs so per-slot is the correct reading). Default stays `off` until the owner ratifies the flip — recommended `slot@0.5` per the banked H4 verdict |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU=T` / `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_FLOOR=F` | 0.5 / 0 | dsv4 serve door: tau and window floor for the `slot` policy when `MEMRA_DSV4_VT` names it without inline values |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER=dspark` | unset (trunk-only, plain routes) | dsv4 serve door: load the checkpoint's own DSpark drafter (~10.7 GiB resident on the second card) and arm the spec routes; unset = drafter absent, plain decode only (route keying reads drafter residency) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES=a,b` | `0,1` | dsv4 serve door: which two CUDA devices carry the PP-2 stack (`Dsv4Gpu` is two-card by construction); unparseable = boot refusal |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_DEFER_READBACK` | on | engine-bundle slice 2: the dspark verify runs with DEVICE tokens and no blocking chain readback — one merged DtoH sync per round instead of two (draft-chain read + verify argmax read). `0` = the two-sync host-token arm (rollback seam; byte identity between the arms is pinned by the E2E gate, and the device-token embed rides the same `embed_gather_u32_t` rows). Refuses under a PP stage split (no stage-split arm) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH` | ROUTE-SPLIT: serve route ON, bin arm off | engine-bundle slice 3 + graphs-serve lane: replay per-(segment, vt) CUDA graphs for the dspark verify's consecutive LINEAR-layer runs, plus the full-verify (vt, rung, hi) graph when a round rides one seqs rung (captured from the same `qwen35_tparallel_linear_layer`/fa-layer bodies the eager arm runs — no second copy of the math; pointer tables refreshed per verify). SERVE ROUTE DEFAULT ON since v0.103 (owner-ratified 2026-08-22 on the §10 serve-lifetime battery: crossover K=36–43 requests, steady −0.357 ms/round, session wall −1.55..−1.65%, byte-exact 240/240 ×3 interleaved boot pairs; pool = model-owned, shared across sessions, multi-GiB at storm-complete; the pool magnitude is per-export and unknown until observed on the serving box (the 2026-08-31 refuted-read pass killed the earlier 8,852 MiB q38 figure as a portable claim)) — `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH=0` is the kill-switch (eager walk, byte-identical), `MEMRA_DSPARK_VG_MAX=0` the finer freeze valve. COMPOSITION CONSTRAINT (measured on the v0.103 train battery): the door rides the slice-2 deferred path ONLY, and the confidence-slot vt policy (the v0.101 default on accept-rate-head checkpoints under adaptive) keeps the legacy readback order — so on a head-carrying drafter at the adaptive route default the pool is structurally inert (0 captures, exactness unchanged); ladder/fixed arms (`MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0`, `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T=k`) with markov/plain-chain drafters engage it. MEASURED CORRECTION (lane/graph-launch-guard-sweep-20260831, dev-box census runs): a DFlash2 drafter does NOT engage the pool on either arm; the DFlash2 propose branch never builds the deferred device chain (dflash.rs serve + bin arms set `deferred` only on the markov/plain-chain branch), so on the q38+DFlash2 serving shape the pool prints ENGAGED, takes ZERO captures, and run_full/run_segment never launch. The MTP route (`MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH`) replays the same pool through the same walk and is the caller that exercises it on GDN exports. BIN ARM stays opt-in (`=1`) by its own measured disposition: at gate scale the mean is FLAT (109.2 vs 109.4 — the capture toll is never repaid over a 256-token run). dspark-only; MTP untouched; refuses to co-arm with the ROUND-STREAM verify. ADMISSION ACCOUNTING (2026-08-23, lane/hermes-perf-fixes): the serve gate charges the pool's projected remaining growth (`dspark_vg_debt_projection`: remaining capture slots x the MARGINAL bytes a new key adds to the device graph-mem pool, two-point measured) on top of the transient reserve, so sessions admitted while the pool is cold no longer overcommit the VRAM it will hold. CHARGED BY STRUCT since lane/graph-launch-guard-sweep-20260831: the MTP route's `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH` door fills the SAME model-owned pool and no longer escapes the term behind the old dspark-route gate; `MEMRA_DSPARK_VG_MAX` doubles as the reserve's tightening valve (=0 zeroes it with the freeze). MARGINAL, not mean, by on-box measurement: with the q38 pool's reservation flat at ~33.6 MiB across captures 1..3 a mean-based reading extrapolated the pool's ONE-TIME shared allocation and charged 8,556/4,261/2,830 MB of phantom reserve — an over-reserve that can refuse admissions that would have fit. Until growth is measurable (single observation) the charge is bounded to one more pool's worth. GRAPH-LAUNCH HEADROOM GUARD (lane/graph-launch-guard-sweep-20260831, extending step37 defect 3): below the 256 MiB `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` driver-free floor every replay round yields to the byte-identical eager walk, one grep-stable `[dspark-vg] graph replay suspended:` line (once per process); unguarded, cuGraphLaunch segfaults inside libcuda on an exhausted card with zero log lines |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_MARKOV` | on | greedy on-device Markov semi-AR chain over the block (logits_k += W2(W1[prev realized token])) when the drafter carries the markov tensors; `0` = plain parallel block |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_EMB_SCALE` | off (raw) | `1` = feed the drafter sqrt(n_embd)-scaled embed rows — whether z-lab's gemma4 training fed scaled or raw is not visible from the reference; acceptance arbitrates |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_FA` | off (sdpa_naive) | set = block attention through `fa_prefill` instead of the naive SDPA (the fa arm returns behind this seam once its kernel is fixed + parity-gated) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_NOROPE` | off | set = skip rope on the draft q (bring-up bisect arm) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_DUMP=<dir>` | off | stage dumps (ctx_features, per-layer q/k, layer outputs; f32 LE) for the oracle parity bisect |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_DEBUG=1` | off | per-round debug prints (first-round block state etc.) |

## 6. Research platform (MTP-heal, dual-shape)

memra's second shape is a research platform. The first protocol (MTP-heal) measures MTP draft-head
acceptance at FULL PRECISION as the ceiling, then on the NVFP4 daily GGUF — the delta is the quant
hit on drafting. Recorded in the dual-shape lane notes, archived 2026-08-17.

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_FULL_PREC=1` | FULL-PRECISION LOADER MODE (default OFF). Bypasses the standing loader law (large BF16/F8 → Q8_0/NVFP4 re-encode, the "Float-poison" tripwire — the very re-encodes this mode must NOT do). Everything loads Float; large 2D bf16 matmul weights stay **bf16-resident** (`GpuTensor::FloatBf16`) with dequant-on-use, so the 9B (~18GB bf16) + f32 activations fit 24GB instead of blowing to ~38GB as an all-f32 materialization. Compute rides the Stage-A f32 oracle path end to end (`MEMRA_FAST=0`-class). **SLOW IS FINE** — this mode is for the exactness ceiling, not speed. Also forces the EAGER spec draft (CUDA-graph capture can't enclose cuBLASLt f32 GEMV or the dequant alloc) and disables `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` (the ceiling wants the model's natural full head; the trim gather is Quant-only anyway). The Float-poison tripwire warnings are correct here and are suppressed. |

Per-slot acceptance for this protocol comes from the existing **`MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1`** (§4) — its
`per_slot=[...]` line is the deep-K decay profile; no separate flag was needed.

**Acceptance battery harness** (`tools/`, no GPU-free CI — runs on the rig):
- `acceptance_battery.sh <model> <out.jsonl>` — fixed prompt set (p1/p2/p3) + the 8-turn agent loop, N≥3, one JSONL row per (prompt,K,run). `FULL_PREC=1` for the bf16 ceiling arm, plain for the NVFP4 arm.
- `agent_loop_acceptance.sh <model> <out.jsonl> <arm>` — the 8-turn accumulative agent-loop protocol (recreated in-repo; feeds each turn's output forward).
- `acceptance_parse.py` — one run-spec invocation (merged stdout+stderr, `MEMRA_SPEC_K=<k>` + `MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1`) → one JSONL row.
- `acceptance_delta.py <bf16.jsonl> <nvfp4.jsonl>` — the deliverable delta table: per-(prompt,K) median acceptance, ceiling vs quant, and the hit.

```
# bf16 ceiling:
FULL_PREC=1 tools/acceptance_battery.sh /data/ai-ml/hf-models/qwen35-9b-hf out-bf16.jsonl
# NVFP4 hit:
tools/acceptance_battery.sh /data/ai-ml/hf-models/qwen35-9b-nvfp4-gguf/Qwen3.5-9B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF.gguf out-nvfp4.jsonl
# delta:
tools/acceptance_delta.py out-bf16.jsonl out-nvfp4.jsonl --json summary.json
```

## 7. The H100 / sm_90a lane (merged from `backend/sm90a-boot` rounds 26-36; unified-tree rounds 37-49)

On an H100 the arch auto-detects (`MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH=90a` forces it). Every promoted
config below followed the repo law:
harness proof -> engine seam -> full battery (kernel-check pins, greedy/stream identity,
decode/prime/graph gates, interleaved x5 A/B) -> default-ON with a `=0` revert. The
evidence ledger is [ARCHITECTURE-H100.md](../ARCHITECTURE-H100.md); the one-command
battery (`tools/validate-h100.sh`) was retired with the Hopper CI lane on 2026-09-02 —
run the same gates directly.

### Promoted defaults (Hopper; `=0` reverts each)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_GDN_MMA` | ON (90a + 120a) | GDN K4/K5 chunk pair on mma tensor cores (bf16 W/k mirrors); kernel-check pins both configs. sm_120a naked default since lane/moeprime-nvfp4-direct (2026-08-21): bf16 HMMA is sm_80-class PTX; measured +5-8% on a RTX PRO 6000 (ornith15 pp14715) and +1-2% on the local 5090 (q38-27b), interleaved both orders. One const default (`gdn_mma_default_on`) feeds all three read sites — a partial flip arms the mma pre-work while the scan runs scalar (measured net loss) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_WGMMA` | ON (90a only) | K4+K5 FUSED wgmma kernel (`gdn_k45_wgmma`) + K2 on wgmma; Y/Ssnap tensors never materialized; qb16/Pb16 mirrors; nested inside the mma config. Official prefill +2.56%, batched prime +3.8%. HARD-gated to Hopper builds (2026-08-21): the wgmma asm bodies exist only at `__CUDA_ARCH__==900` (`MEMRA_K45_REAL`) — on other arches the kernel compiles empty, so the env force is refused rather than silently producing garbage |
| `MEMRA_FA3` | ON (90a) | FA3 full-attn prefill: TMA swizzled Q/K/V ring + wgmma, 4.8x the mma kernel (v11) |
| `MEMRA_L2_V2` | ON | l2-norm v2 with in-epilogue bf16 mirror folds (kb16 + qb16 — the standalone mirror passes are gone) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_DB` | ON | de-broadcast q/k at num_k heads (`hk` plumbed through the chunked chain) |
| `MEMRA_EMBED_DEV` | ON | device-side embed gather (host dequant + htod per token gone; +12.4% official lane) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_GRAPH` | ON at budget >= 48 (>=180 SMs) / >= 256 (everything else) | hybrid graph door in `generate_with`: capture/replay decode over the batched-prime cache. Official decode 190.3 -> 220.7 tok/s (+16%); bit-identical (graph-decode-gate, 256 steps x 16 fa buckets). `1` forces at any budget. KEY 256 -> 48 ON >=180-SM RIGS ONLY (q27 deep dive 2026-08-05): the 256 key was the E4B amortization rule, never a measured crossover — every <=128-token generation (incl. the board shape) ran eager on 188-SM silicon where the crossover is actually ~48 (two-model sweep, N=3 interleaved: q8 +3.80% / nvfp4 +7.72% at n=128, receipts `research/q27-deepdive-20260805/`). SM-GATED at 180 because the 48 key does NOT transfer to the 82-SM laptop 5090 (same protocol: -1.61% at n=128, negative through n=512, `local5090/` receipts) and the 132-SM H100 / 170-SM desktop 5090 are unmeasured at sub-256 budgets — those rigs keep the 256 key their board rows were measured with; widening below 180 needs an on-box crossover sweep |
| `MEMRA_CONV_FUSE` | ON | carried-ring conv + SiLU + GDN repack in one pass (bit-identical class) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_VL` | ON | varlen batched cores: ONE launch per stage for all B sequences (bit-gateable vs per-seq) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_F16G` | ON (mode 2 BOTH arches, silu class — 90a flipped 2026-08-02 at 152.6% of cublas; sm_120a sk + direct tiles) | grouped f16 expert prefill lane (round 49 promotion): q35 board prime 5490 -> 8380 (+53%) at 41/41 dequant coverage — the last board loss flipped. Round 50: the gemma-MoE (gelu) site defaults OFF per model (g26 board-2048 prefill -8.3% under the round-49 default; `=1`/`=2` still opens it). sm_120a MODE-2 default (2026-08-02, lane/f16g-default-rearb): with the IQ4_XS/IQ3_S direct loaders the sk visitor beats the int8-MMA MMQ tiles on the IQ banks too — naked q35 board-2048 4023 -> 5356 (+33.1%), KAT pp512 2029 -> 2988 (+47.2%) / pp2048 2973 -> 3886 (+30.7%), decode flat (t>=16 floor), o35b + g26 dispatch-unchanged (sha-identical), x5/x3 interleaved (receipts `research/f16g-default-rearb-20260802/`). AUTO-KQUANT (mode 3, lane/q4k-expert-prefill — sk only where MMQ rejects the layer; Ornith-35B Q4_K board-2048 1098 -> 3454, 3.14x) stays reachable as `=3`, the rollback seam. Modes + full history in §5; `=0` reverts |
| `MEMRA_PP_F16` | ON (90a), per-model; UNSET elsewhere = capacity-keyed auto | f16 prefill mirrors on the cuBLASLt lane (dequant-once at load, decode untouched — m>=16 arm only). With the env unset, `pp_f16_capacity_ok` (f16_ffi.rs, zoo-fusion arc 2026-08-17) admits mirrors iff free VRAM covers the admissible f16 mass + 8 GiB serving headroom (measured c8 agg +37%, ttft −70% on the 31B downQ6K trunk; 24GB rigs refuse by construction); an explicit env keeps absolute priority. Coverage classes: Q8_0 (2026-07-26, pp512 +80%), Q6_K (round 47, q27 pp2048 +64%), Q4_K + Q5_K (round 49, q27 pp2048 +54% at default budget). PER-MODEL argmax-gate arbitration (round 45): the qwen Q8_0 dense class defaults OFF (the f16-vs-int8 numeric gap flips a real-prompt top-tie deterministically — gate-violating defaults don't ship); gemma + the MoE hybrids hold MATCH and keep mirrors. `=1` forces (diagnostic), `=0` kills everywhere |
| `MEMRA_PP_F16_BUDGET_MB` | 32768 | VRAM cap for the f16 mirrors, layer-order prefix; admission passes ordered Q6_K -> Q4_K -> Q5_K so full Q6_K coverage (the ~10x dequant-GEMM replacement) is the floor. Machine-specific config: q27 serving at 43008 measured pp2048 6564 (+33% over default; +Q5_K full stack 8063). On 80GB the f16-mirror budget and the KQRP decode mirrors compete for the same ~15GB — per-box serving configs choose (round 49b) |
| `MEMRA_PP_BF16` | **OFF** | Resident-BF16 tensor-core prefill GEMM (cuBLASLt `CUDA_R_16BF`, `cu/f16_prefill.cu memra_bf16_pp_gemm` -> `f16_ffi::bf16_tc_gemm`), taken in `linear_bf16_chunked_inner` at `m >= 16 && !exact && canonical_chunk_rows.is_none()`. WHY: a BF16 checkpoint carries no Q8_0 fp16 mirror, so `matmul_group`'s f16 fast path (which requires `GpuTensor::Quant { f16: Some(_) }`) never admits it and every prefill projection dequanted the FULL weight to f32 and ran an f32, non-tensor-core GEMM plus a fresh 2x-weight f32 buffer per call. Measured on the step37 prime (2026-08-28, `MEMRA_PRIME_PROF=1`, 4096-token chunk): norm+qkv 507ms + o_proj 311ms at ~15-20 TFLOP/s/rank on a 250+ TFLOP/s card. Needs no mirror and no extra VRAM — the checkpoint bytes are already the k x n col-major operand the TN form wants. NUMERIC CONFIG: weight bytes untouched (strictly closer to the checkpoint than the f32 round-trip it replaces); rounding enters only at the activation f32->bf16 cast. Decode never reaches it (m<16 arm). ROLLBACK SEAM: unset or `=0` restores the f32 dequant path everywhere. DEFAULT DECISION: **OFF, and now with the receipt that keeps it off.** The argmax gate RAN 2026-08-28 on a stable oracle (spec off; with spec on the oracle was itself nondeterministic) and the door FAILS it: at `max_tokens=1` — one forward, no cascade possible — the first-token argmax FLIPS on curve-1000, arm0 `'Got'` vs arm1 `' behavior'`. First token is identical on the other three real prompts (agentic8, curve-0128, curve-0400), whose full-generation divergences at char 30/1212 (0.8%), char 30/115 (26%) and char 1118/1152 (97%) are consistent with tie-break cascade. Engagement proven both ways: arm1 `[bf16-tc] ENGAGED`=47 distinct prime shapes / DECLINED=2, arm0 =0, and all six rep-pairs were byte-identical within each arm. THE BAR THIS MISSES IS THIS FILE'S OWN: `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES` and `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` are numeric-class doors admitted on run-gen **argmax MATCH** with a stated logit-maxdiff class (DEV_ROUTES: identical argmax at logit maxdiff 2.826e-3). A door that moves the first token on a real prompt is not in that class. Re-opening it needs either a shape restriction that provably avoids the flip, or a logit-delta cell showing the flipped position is a near-tie plus an owner decision to accept argmax movement for the 11% — not a rerun hoping for a friendlier prompt set. Receipts: `~/.lanectl/control/20260820T052756+0300-step37-nvfp4-2card-swap.md` GLM5_NEXT RE-OPENING ARM (lane/glm5-tc-trunk-prefill, 2026-08-29, the prefill-gap plan's L2): glm5's trunk (34 KDA layers' `kda_q/k/v/out`, 33.5M-element checkpoint BF16 kept unquantized by the mint's precision split and the keeplist fix) reaches this door ONLY when bf16-resident: `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1` loads those tensors `GpuTensor::FloatBf16` and this flag then takes `bf16_tc_gemm` at m >= 16; without MMV the trunk loads as f32 2-D `Float` (the loader-law warning) and prefill rides the f32 non-tensor-core GEMM, the measured 15-20 TFLOP/s cliff that is the dominant share of the residual ~9 s in the post-L1 10.25 s TTFD at 6,470 tokens (BOX-AB 2026-08-29). Under MMV, m in 16..=32 takes the MMV rows path before this door by dispatch precedence (tiny prompts only; correctness unchanged). The low-rank pairs `f_a/f_b/g_a/g_b` and `b_proj` sit under MMV's 2M threshold, stay exact f32, and remain a small f32-GEMM residue by design. ENGINE-SIDE GATE, green and red banked in `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tc-trunk-prefill-20260829/` (`crates/memra-engine/tests/glm5_bf16_tc_trunk_prefill_gpu.rs`, 5090, TF32 off): reference band vs `memra_reference::kimi_delta_net_layer` on the mixed-residency fixture at t in {16, 64, 4096} with value-identical weights (worst green rel 3.796e-3, bar 8e-3 at 2.1x headroom), engagement anchored on the new `f16_ffi::bf16_tc_dispatches()` counter at the invocation (4 per mixer call, 0 below the knee), decode byte-identity at m in {1, 2, 15} (flag-ON output BIT-identical to the expansion program), and three red arms: transposed weight bytes (rel 1.708e0), the dropped activation cast expressed as raw f32 bits fed as bf16 (rel 7.98e37), and the decode-leak predicate mutation (m >= 16 loosened to m >= 1: the byte-identity gate FAILED as designed, banked as `gate-red-decode-leak.log`, reverted). A per-boot `[bf16-tc] flag=on\|off` announce (printed in both arms) was added in the same lane. FLIP CONDITION for glm5, pre-registered: box A/B interleaved x5 on the serving placement with the arms factorized (MMV=1 vs baseline, then MMV=1 + this flag vs MMV=1 alone, so each flag carries its own receipt), TTFD + prefill/decode tok/s, the sampled vendor-default twin, the max_tokens=1 first-token argmax gate on the three real prompts, and on any flip the 8-draw vendor-default census proving the position is soft, handed to the owner for the accept/hold call (the acceptance class the owner ratified for glm5 on 2026-08-29: MEMRA_BF16_MMV and MEMRA_MOE_GROUPED_PREFILL). Default stays OFF; the glm5 serving env pins it only after the owner call. The step37 verdict above stands untouched: this arm adds receipts for a different family and makes no generic-model claim. OWNER ACCEPTANCE FOR GLM5, 2026-08-30, on the L2 box A/B bundle, verbatim: "accept if it can really work well on 2 cards. if the ttft and the tok/s will be too slow, we will be back for bigger fleet. but as it seems, 4 card for this model economy is not justifiable, just for the noise of supporting new model." Default form follows the step37 MMV precedent: the repo default stays OFF, and the GLM SERVING RECIPE (research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/BRINGUP.md, "THE SERVING SHAPE IS 2-CARD") pins MEMRA_PP_BF16=1 together with MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1. The measured rows behind the acceptance (interleaved x5, three factorized arms, engine bac42f759d, research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tc-trunk-prefill-20260829/BOX-AB-L2.md): door at fixed residency TTFD 7.46->6.81 / 8.89->8.17 / 10.26->9.41 s (-8.1 to -8.7 percent, non-overlapping spreads), prefill 620/624/630 -> 679/679/687 tok/s, sampled vendor-default twin the fastest row (6.62 s, 698.8 tok/s), first-token argmax gate 30/30 MATCH with boot-deterministic outputs (census not needed), decode C-vs-B within noise (the m>=16 predicate held on the box as on the rig), engagement 12 ENGAGED shapes / 0 DECLINED, and the flag pair enables the ADOPTED 2-card full-residency placement whose greedy outputs are byte-identical to the 3-card arm. |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PROF` | **OFF** | Diagnostic only: sync-bounded per-phase wall inside `step35_prime_batch_layers` (norm+qkv / attn / o_proj / moe), printed as `[prime-prof]` once per prime call. Absolute time inflates (each mark synchronizes the stream) — the SPLIT is the signal. Exists because two inspection passes failed to locate a 3.8 s/4096-token chunk against a ~1 s compute budget and nsys cannot capture through the server's GPU worker thread. Default OFF: it serializes the layer walk and has no product value. `=1` enables |
| `MEMRA_F16G_BDB` | **OFF** | Double-buffered B tile in the direct-from-quant 128-row MoE GEMM (`moe_kq_sk128v_kernel<QT, BDB>`). The shipped schedule keeps ONE B buffer, so the weight-tile dequant (`kq_store`) sits between two `__syncthreads` and cannot overlap the mma. `=1` gives it two buffers, ONE barrier per k-block instead of two, and issues kb+1's dequant after the barrier so it runs behind this kb's mma. Same tiles, same accumulation order — a schedule change, not a numeric-class change, so it gates bitwise through kernel-check rather than through an argmax battery. Smem 63 -> 72 KB, which leaves `occ128` at 1 (measured `[moe-sk-form] occ128=1` before and after), so the arm isolates the serialization and moves nothing else. CONTEXT (2026-08-28, step37 prime): the grouped GEMM is 90% of the MoE bucket and 44% of a 4096-token chunk, at 14.7 TFLOP/s/rank; tile form, occupancy, padding and register spills are all ruled out as levers. ROLLBACK SEAM: unset or `=0` restores the shipped single-buffer schedule; both instantiations stay compiled so the arms are one env var apart. DEFAULT DECISION: OFF until its A/B and bitwise gate land. Receipts: `~/.lanectl/control/20260820T052756+0300-step37-nvfp4-2card-swap.md` |

### Serving (memra-server)

One-class correctness default (2026-08-13): ordinary serving uses the generic batched decode
program at B=1 as well as B>=2. The eager B=1 fusion program and GraphSession replay are each
solo-only; changing co-residence used to switch a live request between their FP composition and
the batched composition. That changed real token ids in Step35 and Q35-MoE, then reproduced the
Q27 restored-hit failure exactly: controlled peer arrival selected the historical 11-token EOS
hash at 50 and 225 ms, while B1FAST=0 held restored hits to one hash. Both solo-only programs now
require explicit `=1` and are fixed-solo measurement doors, not load-bearing defaults. Q35-MoE
retains its code-level eager-path exclusion as defense in depth.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH` | 6 | max sequences per cross-request prime batch. Complete fresh prompts remain eligible for every supported batched-prime architecture. For dense models, the scheduler also batches one bounded continuation chunk per eligible cold session per tick; this prevents N cold primes from serially holding decode-ready rows. Routed-MoE carried chunks stay on per-session primes: Q35's frozen mixed c=4 cell showed both cold misses selecting EOS at 26/60 only after repeated continuation batches, while serial and one whole-fresh batch controls reached 60/60. Re-enable that class only after a realistic multi-chunk + serving-decode gate qualifies it. `=1` is the full rollback seam and restores per-session serialized cold primes. Dense Q27 retains continuation batching: the coldhol kneeraise harness moved the clean-throughput knee c12 -> c16, and the later frozen sold-shape N=5 requalification retained c16 with 825 prime-batch calls and pooled c4 hit-TTFT p50/p95 18.497/19.820 ms. Its c4 output median was 144.245 vs the old 144.462 tok/s (`-0.150%`), which the strict reducer preserves as a regression flag despite both targets remaining SELLABLE. Receipts `research/coldhol-20260812/`, `research/coldfix-20260812/`. (B=8 measured within spread of 6.) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_MAX_T` | 2048 | max per-seq tokens eligible for batch (raised 320 -> 2048 round 35: the old crossover was measured on pre-wgmma cores; batched now wins at every tested T) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_HOLD_MS` | 4 | batch-formation hold for staggered arrivals. The same bounded window is the post-retirement refill grace: when an interactive completion opens a client-concurrency slot while cold prefill remains, cold prefill waits briefly for the replacement request to cross the HTTP/channel boundary. On the frozen sold shape this restored pooled Q27 c4 hit-TTFT p95 from the broken 269 ms class to 19.820 ms against the 21.565 ms envelope while retaining the c16 knee. `=0` disables both holds. |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_GS` | OFF | `1` lets a fixed-solo greedy interactive session ride GraphSession replay. Replay is eager-program bit-identical, but it must degrade when a peer arrives; that would reintroduce the load-history numeric-class transition the default closes. Use only where admission guarantees the request remains solo. Round-35 history: promotion captures over an already-primed cache (`graph_session_from_cache`); the older token-wise re-prime was a 3x end-to-end loss on long prompts (6.4 -> 2.8s measured). **REACHABILITY CORRECTION (2026-08-13, hermes `a5f9431efe0a30a2` confirming an orchestrator read): this door admits RESTORED-HIT and POOL-RESUME sessions ONLY, never a cold chunked-prefill one.** The eligibility test requires `prefill_done && generated.is_empty()` at tick top, but phase (c) sweeps every `prefill_done` session into the batched decode set and emits token 1 in the SAME tick cold prefill completes — so a cold session never starts a tick in that state. The previous wording here ("promotion happens after chunked prefill") described an unreachable path. Left unwidened deliberately: `MEMRA_GS_MIN` below records the door as a net loss at every measured length post-B1FAST, so there is no perf case for making cold promotion reachable, and widening it would need a program-crossing guard (mid-request promotion is the early-EOS corruption class) |
| `MEMRA_GS_MIN` | 384 | minimum generation budget when `MEMRA_SERVE_GS=1`; inert at the default. **Do not lower on sm_120/82 SM**: after the eager B1 fast path landed, the graph door was a net loss at every measured length — N=3 `agg_tok_s` −6.93% @ 128, −3.97% @ 256, −2.60% @ 512, −1.24% @ 1024, 0/3 wins each, matching its ~340 ms capture cost. The pre-fast-path positive sweep is stale. Receipts: `research/servepath-p2-20260805/` |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_B1FAST` | OFF | `1` opts an eligible fixed-solo tick into `decode_layers_eager`, the same m=1 fusion trunk used by `decode_step_h`; unset, `0`, and other values keep B=1 on the generic batched body used at B>=2. The eager arm is not a kernel-tier fix: m=1 already used MMVQ, while the fusion chain changes FP composition. Its historical fixed-solo benefit was +8.33% q9 / +5.19% q27 decode-only at c=1 (N=5), c=8 flat; PP-N B=1 measured up to +15%. Those numbers remain valid opt-in measurements, not the correctness default. The default flipped after three independent load-history failures: Step35, Q35-MoE EOS at 15/17/25, and dense-Q27 exact 11-token EOS under controlled restored-peer arrival. `decode-batch-gate` config mode now pins default B=1 vs B=N bit identity; strict and PP arm 4 explicitly enable the eager path to retain its `decode_step_h` bit-identity coverage. Q35-MoE stays ineligible even under `=1`; gemma4/e4b, m3, and verify-exact remain excluded. Receipts: `research/servepath-p2-20260805/`, `research/b1fix-20260810/`, `research/q35bug-20260812/`, `research/eosclass-20260813/` |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_OVERLAP` | OFF / unwired | Reserved name for a future c=1 decode/readback overlap lane. The current server has no reader or scheduler wiring for this variable, so setting it has no effect. Do not describe or deploy it as default-on until the worker call, exact model/hardware/HTTP campaign, and rollback receipt land together. `PendingBatchStep` in the engine is substrate only, not a served mechanism. |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_BATCH_CAP` | auto | batched-decode chunk width door (engine assert + scheduler chunking, clamp 1..32). Unset = per-model policy: **16 on exact-16 tier models** (`decode_batch_exact16_ok` — every matmul has a bit-exact b16-class kernel; the step scopes verify_exact so the m>=16 GEMM/MMQ arms never fire), else 8. The predicate is an ALL over ~500 matmuls, so ONE class without a b16 refuses the whole checkpoint back to chunk 8 — diagnose with `MEMRA_EXACT16_WHY=1` (§4). Tier coverage widened 2026-08-06 (lane/rp-on-st) with b16 twins for NVFP4, Q8_0-base, Q4_K, Q5_K: before that every real MIXED checkpoint (NVFP4 MLP + Q4_K qkv + Q5_K gate + Q6_K output + Q8_0 ssm) was refused, GGUF and FP8-ST alike, and the Q8_0 arm was reachable only via the q8rp mirror's `_rp` twin. Inc3 receipts (5090, 9B Q8_0+mirror, `research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/`): gate2 bit-strength PASS at chunk 12/16 (s32+s160), 32-seq engine tick +12% vs chunk 8 same-mirror (chunksweep x5). Chunk widths above the exact tier remain a MEASUREMENT DOOR: m=16 GEMM/MMQ classes bit-shift logits at step 0 (maxdiff ~1.3-2.3e-1) and m>16 has no exact kernel class at all. HISTORY: the pre-inc2 H100 probe (`research/darklane-serving-20260801/`) measured cap 15/16 flat-or-worse on the then per-seq-serial-bound tick — that verdict was re-swept after inc2's z-batched attention made the tick weight-stream-bound (the stale-verdict law) |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVSAMPLE` | ON | `0` = host-sample every batched-tick row from last_logits (the pre-2026-08-01 tick). Default: greedy-no-penalty rows sample via device argmax (bit-identical to host argmax), pure-temperature rows via seeded per-row gumbel draw (distribution-equal, batch-composition-independent — decode-batch-gate gate3); top-k/top-p/min-p rows ALSO filter and draw on device (filter_stats floor + filtered gumbel, the lane/devsample-topkp extension — `devsample_meta`, worker.rs; gated by decode-batch-gate gate3d: batched-vs-isolated stream identity on mixed metas + per-draw survivor-set admission, hermes sweep 2026-08-23). Sampled penalty rows join that path through `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVPENALTY`; greedy-penalized and constrained penalty/filter compositions retain the host oracle. Kills the measured host temp-sample at 248k vocab (1.34-1.36 ms/row, ~45% of the B=8 9B tick) |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVPENALTY` | OFF | `1` moves sampled requests with repetition/frequency/presence penalties onto the device while leaving ordinary device sampling unchanged. `Sampler::accept` maintains exact sparse counts over the configured sliding window; the batched epilogue uploads only unique positive-count `(token,count)` entries, applies every row's independent coefficients in one ragged CUDA launch, then runs the existing filter/Gumbel draw. Raw logits are copied before mutation and remain the only bytes parked for reuse, so a resumed request cannot double-penalize. Qwen3.8's vendor non-thinking defaults (`temperature=.7`, `top_p=.8`, `top_k=20`, `presence_penalty=1.5`) are the motivating production shape. Greedy penalties stay host-side to avoid a new argmax numeric class; constrained penalty/filter composition stays host-side pending its own gate. Qualified on RTX PRO 6000 Server Edition with `sample-check`, the causal decode-batch gate3d tooth, both-model kernel/generation/spec batteries, and interleaved N=5 serving curves: Qwen plain c1/c16 46.49→68.66 / 94.95→300.93 tok/s; Ornith plain c1/c24 79.46→176.78 / 118.77→651.77 tok/s. Naked Qwen keeps spec c1 neutral and wins after policy demotion; penalty-neutral Ornith remains structurally inert. The engine default stays off because this evidence does not qualify other GPUs or board variants. Qualification requires that deployment's own launcher smoke; an owner-authorized transfer canary may opt in sooner only with a persistent explicit rollback and without inheriting the measured performance claim. Receipts: `research/pro-device-penalty-sampling-20260824/`. |
| `MEMRA_CONSTRAIN_HOST` | off | `1` = constrained (`response_format`) sessions keep the v1 HOST mask path: full-row logits D2H + host -inf mask + host O(n_vocab) sample, no graph promotion, no spec (the merged-v1 shape; 117 vs 194 tok/s receipts `research/constrained-20260803/`). Default (constrained-full, 2026-08-03): the packed llguidance bitset H2Ds per step (~31KB) into a stable per-session device buffer and `mask_logits_f32` bans on device before the SAME device-sample/lean-logits/graph/spec paths unconstrained sessions ride (kernel-check `mask_logits_col` bit-identity arm; battery `research/constrained-full-20260803/`). Oracle/A-B seam only |
| `MEMRA_POSTTHINK_CEILING` | 0 (off) | `<tokens>` = forced-close guard for POST-THINK constrained sessions (lane/step37-postthink-grammar, 2026-08-30: `response_format` on think-forced templates such as step37, where think runs unconstrained with every end-of-generation id banned and the grammar engages at the template's think-close token sequence). Past this many think tokens the phase-1 mask collapses to exactly the next close token, so the sampler is forced to walk the close and the grammar engages; same mask seam on the host, batched-device, and graph paths. Default OFF by design, with measured receipts: never-closed thinks are the MODEL'S OWN think-length tail, not an EOS-ban artifact (paired plain-vs-constrained twins on the same prompts: plain closed 7/16, constrained 8/16, zero pairs where only the constrained arm failed to close), so the honest lever is the request's `max_tokens` (measured natural closes on real agentic prompts: p50 2119 / p90 3554 / max 3900 think tokens; at max_tokens 12288 the JSON gate passes where 4096 truncated). ON is an operator opt-in for guaranteed-JSON deployments: at ceiling 256 the forced close fired 4/4 exactly at 257 think tokens and yielded valid JSON 3/4; the 1/4 miss was the documented unbounded-grammar whitespace-degeneration face (`{` then whitespace to budget), so pair a ceiling with a bounded schema and a value at or above the natural p90. `finish_reason: length` inside think stays an honest receipted face either way. Receipts `research/step37-postthink-grammar-20260830/`. Runtime parameter, per-deployment |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT_MASK` | ON | `0` = the drafter samples UNMASKED under a grammar (the pre-2026-08-04 shape). Default (lane/draft-mask): a constrained SPEC session clones the session's llguidance `Matcher` once per spec round, advances the clone with each proposed draft token, and bans the grammar-illegal ids in the draft head's own logits — in-graph via `mask_logits_col` on the captured draft chain (mask presence is a capture-time shape, so a parked graph of the other shape is dropped), permuted through `d2t` for trimmed FR-Spec heads. Proposals are legal by construction, so the verify-side truncation backstop stops cutting: `gram_cuts` 3/12, 3/15, 1/10, 28/30, 18/25 -> **0/N on every cell measured**. Emitted stream is unchanged by construction (verify + target sampling still decide what's emitted) — gated ON==OFF byte-identical on 7 cells, greedy and seeded-sampled, plus unconstrained 6/6 vs the pre-lane binary. Bounded tight schema: acceptance 0.561 -> 0.651, 216.6 -> 227.5 tok/s (+5.0%, N=3); clone costs 0.002 ms/round, mask 0.001 ms/position. Cells whose drafter already proposes legal tokens (json_object, loose prose) move inside noise; unconstrained is inert. Receipts `research/draft-mask-20260804/` |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_PHASE` | off | `1` = sync-bounded per-phase accumulators inside decode_step_batch (12 phases, printed by decode-batch-bench). Shares rank the tick; the added syncs inflate the total — diagnosis only |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_FA` | ON | `0` = per-seq fa_decode loop in the batched tick (the pre-inc2 launch train). Default: one blockIdx.z=sequence `fa_decode_vec_q_seqs_v4` launch + one combine per layer via the per-step K/V pointer table — per-seq bit-identical (kernel-check seqs pins; decode-batch-gate strict s160). Auto-falls back per step for non-v4 rows (t_kv<96, fp8-KV, hd!=256) or an `fa_split_keys` rung straddle inside the batch (batched-tick inc2, 2026-08-01: B=8 engine +19.7%) |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_APPEND` | ON | `0` = per-seq KV-append loop in the batched tick. Default: one z-batched `append_quantize_kv_q8_0_q5_1_seqs` launch per layer through the same pointer table — cache bytes bit-identical (kernel-check pin). fp8-KV rides the per-seq g-module path |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_LEANLOGITS` | ON | `0` = full [B, n_vocab] logits D2H every batched tick (the pre-inc2 tick). Default: device-sampled rows skip the D2H; the last row parks on-device per cache (`Cache.last_logits_dev`) and is D2H'd once at retire for the KV-reuse pool (the audited last_logits consumer). decode-batch-gate gate3c pins lean==full bitwise. Serving: 9B temp-0.7 c=8/16/32 medians 654/657/659 tok/s vs 565/519/508 lean-off (H100, N=4, `research/batched-tick-inc2-20260801/`) |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_DEDUP` | ON | `0` restores independent cold primes for same-window fanout (rollback/A-B seam). Default: interactive cold requests with the same exact `(model, tenant-scoped cache namespace, >=64-token prefix)` prime one budget-capped common prefix; siblings deep-copy the resulting snapshot, receive exact cached-token credit, and hold one entry lease each until retirement. Token similarity is never evaluated across tenant/salt boundaries. Step35 suffixes keep their per-request continuation-prime path because its fresh PP batch core deliberately refuses carried caches. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` | derived: 2 entries, boot-clamped | CROSS-REQUEST PREFIX CACHE byte budget (0 = off). With no override, memra derives two full-`MEMRA_CTX` snapshots of the largest loaded model: the sum of every trunk full-attention layer's K+V bytes per token times context, plus fixed trunk recurrent state. MTP/NextN head blocks are excluded because prefix snapshots retain only trunk state. It clamps that request to CUDA driver free VRAM measured after model load minus the 1.5 GiB serving-transient reserve. A valid explicit value remains authoritative and unclamped, including `0`; the value is binary MiB. Token-prefix-keyed compact device snapshots share one worker-global byte budget; `(model, cache namespace)` partitions lookup visibility only. Eviction is byte-SLRU by default, while `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_POLICY=lru` restores plain global LRU. A NEW session sharing a >=64-token prompt prefix with cached traffic skips that prefill — FOR TWO-PLANE (kv + recur) MODELS ONLY. A `StatePlan::LatentKvCache` model (glm5_next: 34 KDA + 11 MLA/DSA layers) takes no part in this by default: snapshot capture refuses loudly (`[prefix-cache] snapshot failed (latent (MLA/DSA) KV planes are not carried by prefix entries); prefix not cached`, `cached_tokens` 0 on every request — re-receipted on the deployed 3-card shape, `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/spec-battery-20260830/`), and glm5 pays a FULL prime per request; the skip-prefill sentence above is not a glm5 claim. Carrying the latent planes is `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT` (below), default OFF with the restored-suffix defect class named in its row. `prefix_cache_skips_budget` counts entries too large ever to fit, while `prefix_cache_skips_pinned` counts temporary refusal beside live leases; both are operator-only `/metrics` fields and the first whole-budget refusal is a loud server warning. Dual-PP intersection (2026-08-12, hermes 4beb2984): the naked dual-active placement and prefix snapshots are validated together on the box1 PRO pair — byte identity 3/3, hit TTFT 1.3ms, 0 slot collisions (`research/prefixmoney-20260812/raw/box1-20260812T005749Z`). Ring-ON (`MEMRA_SWA_RING=1`) and host-bounce placements still refuse/disable snapshots. Cold prompts seed full-prompt entries at prefill-done; additionally, when the LCP taught nothing, a plain chat-turns request parks a grid-aligned snapshot at the end of the FIRST message (the render-stable system prompt — `first_message_boundary`, worker.rs, lane/moebatch-q35moe 2026-08-21; shapes with tools/think/tool turns return no boundary and keep the full-prompt seed only). With `MEMRA_PREFIX_PARTIAL_RESTORE=1`, a transformer-only miss with a long LCP copies the position-addressed K/V rows through that split and primes only the suffix, so request 2 reuses it. Hybrid conv/SSM and routed-MoE mid-entry splits refuse observably and retain the cold-prime/snapshot boundary-learning path (request 3+ hits); exact whole-entry boundaries remain reusable. Sessions win over unpinned cache residency (allocation failure evicts every unpinned entry and retries), while hit/fanout leases protect their entry until retirement. Entries bind the full `(model, cache namespace)` identity, internal layout version, per-plane row strides, and bounded source sizes before any restore copy. Cached-vs-fresh exactness is gated 16/16 partial + 16/16 full (`research/prompt-cache-20260802/`). Usage exposes `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`. Entries are isolated by the request's optional `cache_salt`; no salt preserves the `""` single-tenant namespace (`docs/SERVING.md` "Per-tenant isolation"). |
