| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_POLICY` | `slru` | Prefix-cache eviction policy. `slru` (default) = byte-budgeted probation/protected with promotion on first reuse (`research/slrucache-20260813/`: hot-set reuse 107/120 -> 115/120, hot thrash 13 -> 5, post-promotion hot thrash exactly 0, all hits byte-identical). `lru` = plain global LRU: `capacity_victim` evicts the GLOBAL oldest entry across both segments. **Fixed 2026-08-13** — until then this seam was a near-no-op that did the opposite of its purpose: it only forced `protected_pct=100`, and since there was no policy branch in `capacity_victim` (probation-only) while entries still entered probation and still promoted on reuse, a 100% protected share merely set `protected_target_bytes = budget` so `rebalance_protected` could never demote — every entry that earned one hit became permanently unevictable and each newcomer was selected as its own victim. An operator reaching for this flag during a hit-rate collapse therefore deepened it. Regression test: `prefix_cache_lru_policy_evicts_the_global_oldest_not_only_probation` (verified RED against the pre-fix behavior). **The rollback exists because SLRU is NOT universally better:** `research/slrutarget-20260813/` found a real losing shape — after a complete hot-cohort turnover a stale protected cohort traps the new cyclic cohort in probation and the hit rate goes to **0% where plain LRU reaches 75%**, reproduced at both 4,096 and 49,152 MiB and for Q27-only, Q35-only, and worker-global paired entries. Note `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_PROTECTED_PCT` is NOT an LRU selector (it only sizes the protected share, 1..99); this flag is. Until live telemetry shows which shape our traffic has, an operator whose cache hits collapse needs one flag to get LRU back. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_PROTECTED_PCT` | 80 | Prefix-cache SLRU protected share by resident bytes; integer 1..99, with malformed/out-of-range values falling back to 80. New entries enter probation and promote only on reuse. Protected overflow demotes protected LRU; capacity pressure evicts probation LRU first. Probation may borrow unused protected bytes, so the total `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` budget remains usable and individually fitting large entries are still admitted. A pinned fanout snapshot that cannot fit without crossing the protected share is served from its private session copies but not retained. The byte ceiling, oversized-entry refusal, tenant `cache_salt` visibility, live pins, and emergency flush semantics are unchanged. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT` | **OFF (default)** | `=1` arms latent (MLA/DSA) plane capture + restore in prefix entries (lane/glm5-prefix-latent, 2026-08-30; design: `research/glm5-prefix-latent-20260830/DESIGN.md`). WHY IT EXISTS: `PrefixEntry` carried only `kv` + `recur`, and a `StatePlan::LatentKvCache` model (glm5_next: 34 KDA + 11 MLA/DSA) keeps its whole retrievable history in `cache.latent`, so a restore served a one-row attention history while `cached_tokens` reported N of N and the model fabricated fluently (`research/prefix-restore-toolcall-20260828/FINDINGS.txt`: 12/12 cold vs 0/6 restored tool calls, constant 152.6 MB entries at zero bytes/token). The parent lane's guard refuses capture/restore/plain-affinity on latent caches; this flag makes entries CARRY the planes instead: the f32 latent rows (unquantized, the maxdiff-oracle class), the resident k-pool keys + `index_pools_ready` (the keys TRAVEL — a rebuild is impossible under the shipped `MEMRA_DSA_INDEX_RING` tail ring, whose read-once rows are overwritten by design), and the `len % pool` live tail-ring rows. Honest entry cost on glm5_next: 152.6 MB fixed KDA state + 23,936 B/token (~349 MB at 8k ctx); the derived `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` budget books the latent per-token term only while this flag is on. OFF arm (default): byte-for-byte the parent lane's posture — capture refuses loudly, restore refuses, glm5 pays a full prime per request; this is also the ROLLBACK SEAM (unsetting the flag refuses even plane-carrying entries wholesale and the cold path serves). ON arm: capture carries, restore ADMITS ONLY entries whose latent slots are populated — entries minted before the flip keep refusing per entry, loudly. The spec-boundary publisher refused latent caches at the source until lane/glm5-prefix-latent2 (2026-09-01) gave it a latent arm: with `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX=1` (its own row) a glm5 spec session's boundary capture carries the latent tails and the publisher slices the append-only planes live; without that flag the source refusal stands verbatim. Whole-entry restores only: mid-entry latent splits refuse in `partial_prefix_decision` in their own right (an interior boundary's index tail is unrecoverable). DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN, not by caution-in-general: the failure class this replaces was a fluent wrong answer behind a truthful-looking counter, so the flip condition is pre-stated — restored-vs-cold BYTE IDENTITY on the glm5 raw p5/p7 greedy probes on real hardware (the honest cold bank, REBASELINE.txt R3), the unguessable-tool round trip restored, the 8-turn larger-prompt multiturn cache twin with per-turn TTFT + cache-engagement receipts, TTFT-at-depth, and the guard's refusal observed becoming an admit only for carrying entries. Mechanism gates (rig, exactness): `gpu_latent_plane_*` in `crates/memra-engine/tests/latent_prefix_plane_gpu.rs` (snapshot/restore bit identity, ring continuation parity, red mutations) and the worker predicate tests beside `a_latent_bearing_cache_is_refused_by_the_prefix_capture`. |
| `MEMRA_HYPER_SUFFIX_PRIME` | **OFF (default)** | `=1` lets a CARRIED suffix (prefix-cache restore, reuse hit, continuation) on a hyper-connections trunk (glm5_next — `plan_backend::decode_batch_unconverted`) ride the PRIME program instead of tokenwise `decode_step` (lane/glm5-prefix-latent2, 2026-09-01). WHY IT EXISTS: the worker's two prefill sites carry an `eager_mono && carried` veto built for gemma4, whose engine REFUSES a pos>0 prime; glm5_next's engine does not — `prime_cache_hyper` is positional by construction (pos from `cache.pos`, KDA state via `cache.recur`, MLA/DSA via the latent planes; every chunk after the first inside one cold prime already runs exactly this program), yet the veto sent every restored glm5 suffix through the t=1 decode program at ~33 ms/token, 10-14x a cold prime — the parent lane's PRODUCT-SUSPECT defect (`research/glm5-prefix-latent-20260830/box-window`, box B: suffixes 469/1407/1899 tok predicted 15.5/46.4/62.7 s at 33 ms/tok, measured 15.10/46.13/62.49 s). A prefix hit MUST NOT be slower than a miss; with the veto in place it was, which is why `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT` could never flip. ON arm: both prefill sites lift the veto for hyper trunks (`carried_suffix_primes`), the H11 hit re-seed and hit-LCP deepening re-arm for them (the suffix is now prime-provenance, so the R16 chained-provenance refusal's ground dissolves), and every engagement logs `[suffix-prime] ENGAGED: carried suffix N ... primes at base=B` (the deploy-gate grep target — a TTFT number alone cannot distinguish the prime program from a fast box). OFF arm (default): byte-for-byte today's tokenwise path, plus a `[suffix-prime] DECLINED (MEMRA_HYPER_SUFFIX_PRIME off)` receipt on hyper trunks in BOTH prefill sites — once per admitted request (so once per TURN in a multi-turn conversation and once per fanout sibling), never per token (the parent lane's C2 defect was only ever inferred from TTFT arithmetic; now it is grep-able). ON-arm fallback honesty (PR #93 review findings 3a+5): any prompt token of a hyper trunk that still walks tokenwise decode_step under the flag (budget starvation, sub-floor suffix, any future prime-branch veto) logs `[suffix-prime] TOKENWISE (flag on)` once and sets a per-session provenance bit that makes BOTH capture sites (prefill-done seed + lcp-split insert) refuse to publish, loudly — the restore+decode_step chained-provenance entry R16 (research/cacheinval-20260813) refuses can no longer be published by inference-gap, only prevented by checked invariant. gemma stays vetoed regardless of the flag — its refusal is load-bearing (the engine kills pos>0 primes). DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (new-flags law): the ON arm changes the suffix's numeric program (chunked prefill vs t=1 decode), so it ships dark until the box battery banks restored-vs-cold byte identity ON THE CONTINUATION (real conversations, plain + spec routes) plus the owner-law 8-turn larger-prompt cache twin with per-turn TTFT + `cached_tokens` receipts and a cache-bust control; the launch recipe arms it together with `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT`. Rollback seam: unset the flag (per-boot read, no persistent state). Wiring gate: `suffix_prime_carveout_wiring_is_live_in_comment_stripped_source` (worker tests). Receipts: `research/glm5-prefix-latent2-20260901/` |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_STABLE_BOUNDARY` | **0 = OFF by design** | STABLE-BOUNDARY PLAIN CAPTURE (lane/bench-debts-20260901, competitive-bench engine debt 1: promote starvation). `1` arms the plain prefix-cache boundary capture at the render-stable LAST-turn boundary (`plain_checkpoint_boundary`: just before the last turn-marker run, grid-aligned, W1-handled) on BOTH the miss path and the shallow-hit path, deepest-wins against the LCP-taught and first-message arms, `has_key`-deduped. WHY: the prompt-end SEED's key carries the template's live generation header, which the next re-rendered turn replaces (the pi-rewrite divergence documented at `plain_checkpoint_boundary`), so under organic multi-turn churn the demoted population is unmatchable and the host tier starves: the competitive bench measured 1 promotion against 28-49 demotions per N56 cell while 425 of 432 device hits landed on the 64-token shared-system-prompt entry (darklanes `research/competitive-bench-20260901/RESULTS.md` §6/§7; the single promote, a grid-aligned boundary entry, re-materialized 21,056 tokens / 782 MB in 27.9 ms vs a 27.4 s median re-prefill). With the flag on, every cold turn mints an entry the SAME session's next turn can whole-entry hit on the device tier or promote from the host tier; the admit-time probe order (`host_promote_candidate`: consult host on a device miss OR a device-shallower hit) is unchanged and now invocation-locked by unit tests. The prompt-end seed keeps its exact-resend role; when the stable boundary sits within `prefix_seed_deepen_min()` of prompt end (the common header gap), `prefix_seed_deepens` skips that seed on its own, so the flag does not double-mint. STATED TRADE: an armed `snapshot_at` excludes the session from the in-batch fanout and prime-batch paths (the boundary-stopped prime is per-session), the same exclusion `ckpt_at` and the existing LCP/first-message arms already carry on most cold chat traffic; `solo_widen_fresh` (2026-08-28) keeps the widened solo prime. This is the spec tier's `MEMRA_SPEC_STABLE_BOUNDARY` law (frozen-boundary finding B4, 2026-08-21) ported to the plain capture side. DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-identical to today by construction (no call site changes an armed boundary; unit-locked by `stable_boundary_and_host_probe_wiring`); ON is unmeasured on serving hardware, the named GPU gate (PENDING, next hardware window) is the organic-churn promote-rate cell: replay the bench N32/N56 shape with `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` armed, require promotions to track demotions on live sessions (not 1:28), cached_tokens growth per turn, and the 8-turn larger-prompt cache twin regression-free, receipts to darklanes `research/competitive-bench-20260901/`. Rollback seam: unset (or `0`). Receipts: the `[prefix-cache] stable-boundary capture armed at N of M prompt tokens` line; unit cells `host_probe_fires_on_device_miss_and_on_shallower_device_hit`, `prompt_end_seed_key_never_matches_the_rerendered_next_turn_but_a_stable_key_does`, `stable_boundary_arm_is_deepest_wins_and_floor_checked`. |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` | **0 = OFF by design** | PREFIX-CACHE HOST SPILL TIER (lane/kv-host-spill-20260830), in binary MiB of pinned CACHEABLE host RAM. The tier FEEDS the device cache and the restore path is untouched: a device capacity eviction demotes the entry's bytes VERBATIM (already q8_0 K / q5_1 V at rest, no requantization anywhere) into pinned host memory; a later probe that misses the device pool but exactly matches a host entry (same exact-token / PC-ISO key rules, same `PREFIX_CACHE_MIN_TOKENS` floor) re-materializes a normal device `PrefixEntry` and serves through the existing `prefix_restore_at`, byte-lossless by construction. Host entries mirror device entries field for field (KV planes, conv/ssm, MTP draft plane, dspark draft tail, boundary logits and hidden, `layout_version`; a version mismatch is refused at both demote insert and promote). Plain byte-LRU on the host side (demotions arrive SLRU-ordered from the device tier). ARM OFF (`0`, the default): byte-identical to a binary without the tier, nothing is ever demoted or promoted; this is also the rollback seam, one env flip. ARM ON (`<MiB>`): boot-clamps to `/proc/meminfo` MemAvailable x 0.6 with a LOUD warning when the clamp bites (swap-storm law backstop); a pinned-alloc failure is loud and LATCHES THE TIER OFF for the process (never a silent pageable fallback). CO-TENANCY NOTE: multiple memra stacks will share one host (fleet direction 2026-08-30), so the operator sets an explicit per-stack budget; the 0.6 clamp is a backstop, not the sizing mechanism. Copies run synchronously on the CUDA owner thread (HY3 spill law) and every demote/promote logs its duration; cumulative `prefix_host_demote_ms`/`prefix_host_promote_ms` plus entries/bytes/demotions/promotions/rejected-alloc counters are operator `/metrics` fields. Model exclusions ride the upstream `prefix_snapshot` refusals for free (step37 SWA ring, glm5 latent planes: no such entry can exist to demote). The emergency `evict_all` flush still DROPS bytes (an alloc-pressure tick must not stall behind gigabytes of D2H); an overlapped copy-stream variant is a named seam on `Engine::dtoh_u8_into_pinned`, not built. Default OFF because the tier is unmeasured on serving hardware: local unit tests + CPU-side gates are green, and the pod battery (tick-stall receipt from the `*_ms` counters, cached-vs-fresh identity with a host-tier arm, forced-tiny red test, failure-path battery via `tools/kv-host-spill-*.sh`) is PENDING; receipts land in darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/`. |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_VERIFY` | **0 = off** | Host-tier byte-losslessness diagnostic (lane/kv-host-spill-20260830): `1` computes `prefix_entry_state_digest` (sha256 over the entry's logical trunk state: KV planes at the boundary + conv/ssm) on the DEVICE entry at demote, stores it with the host entry, and re-computes it on the re-materialized device entry at promote. A mismatch is a loud `[prefix-host] VERIFY FAILED` line, drops the host entry, counts `digest_mismatches`, and serves the cold path; a match logs `verify ok`. Gate/diagnostic arm ONLY, documented as too slow always-on: the digest D2Hs every plane byte on both sides of the round trip, which for GB-class entries doubles the copy cost. Both arms plus the mismatch failure path are exercised by `tools/kv-host-spill-failure-gate.sh` (pod battery pending; receipts darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/`). Rollback seam: unset (the default). |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_TENANT_PCT` | **50 by design** | PER-TENANT HOST-POOL SHARE CAP (lane/kv-tenancy-compaction-20260831, tiering spec Arc B / section 0.5): one tenant's maximum share of the `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` byte budget, integer percent 1..=100, keyed on the same PC-ISO metering row as the `tenants` receipt (`t:<tenant>` under a keyring; the raw `cache_salt` otherwise). A demotion that would push the tenant's resident bytes past `budget * pct / 100` EVAPORATES (checked BEFORE the D2H copy, so it also skips the PCIe trip) instead of demoting; the tenant's own resident entries are never evicted to make room, and an exact-key re-demote charges only the replace delta. DEFAULT 50, both arms weighed: 50 means one tenant can hold at most half the pool, so a second tenant always finds real capacity the moment it starts demoting (the single-tenant-occupancy hole the spec triage pulled into Arc B; the whale-tenant priority-inversion risk named at Arc D), and it matches the fleet direction the tier was built for (co-tenant multi-tenant hosts, owner 2026-08-30). The costs are stated, not hidden: a genuinely single-tenant box leaves half the host pool idle, and a tenant sitting AT its cap keeps its earliest-demoted set (new prefixes evaporate; promotes and exact-key replaces still refresh; within-share LRU displacement is the named Arc D follow-up). Single-tenant deployments set `100`, which DISARMS the check by contract: the share then equals the whole pool and the global byte-LRU governs that boundary exactly as before this flag existed (evaporation would otherwise replace evict-oldest at the global bound, a strictly worse discipline). Malformed or out-of-range values (0, >100, junk) fall back to 50 with a loud `[prefix-host] WARNING`. Receipts: `prefix_host_tenant_rejects` in `/metrics`, the boot line's `tenant share cap N% = M MB`, and the per-evaporation `[prefix-host] demote evaporated at the tenant share cap` log line (the pre-copy hook's exact production text; the insert-path backstop's `skip demote: tenant` line is a defense-in-depth arm production demotes never reach). Unit cell: two-namespace fill where one namespace hits its cap and the other still demotes (`host_cache_tenant_share_cap_evaporates_one_tenant_and_still_demotes_the_other`); the two-tenant contention cell under real demote pressure is a pod-battery gate (`research/kv-tenancy-20260831/REPORT.md`). |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_HANDOFF` | **empty = OFF by design** | HOST-TIER DEPLOY HANDOFF (lane/host-tier-deploy-warmth-20260901): filesystem path of the cross-deploy handoff file; empty/unset keeps the tier process-lifetime and a blue/green flip starts cold, exactly the pre-flag behavior (darklanes TRAP:host-tier-empties-on-deploy: post-flip fresh-empty pool measured on box12 2026-09-01, cold re-prefill 34.1 s at 82.5k tokens vs 0.467 s warm, research/stress-campaign-20260901/RESULTS.md). Set the SAME path on both slots of a box. Two engine commands ride it: `Cmd::ExportHostHandoff` (deployment admin `POST /admin/kv-host/export`; serve-deploy calls it on the DRAINED old slot after the edge flip) first DRAIN-DEMOTES every evictable device prefix entry into the host tier (the freshest KV state on a draining slot lives on the device) and then serializes the tier newest-first to the path, atomically (.tmp + fsync + rename), synchronously on the worker thread BY DESIGN (a drained slot has nobody to stall); a slot still carrying traffic refuses unless forced; `Cmd::ImportHostHandoff` (`POST /admin/kv-host/import`, or automatically at boot when the file already exists, the sequential-deploy shape) validates the header and then re-materializes ONE entry per scheduler tick (promote-class stalls, bounded by the largest single entry; the idle wait caps at 1 ms so a traffic-free slot keeps dripping), through the ORDINARY `HostPrefixCache::insert`: budget LRU, tenant share cap, and identity checks apply exactly as to a demotion, and the promote/restore path is untouched. Integrity is layered and fails closed: every frame carries a sha256 over its payload (corrupt frame = loud skip, never a bad restore; the stream stays in register), the header is digest-checked, a `PREFIX_ENTRY_LAYOUT_VERSION` mismatch refuses the WHOLE file (a deploy that changes the entry layout starts cold by design, and says so), per-model artifact stamps (file count/bytes/max-mtime stat walk, deliberately not a byte hash) refuse a model whose weights changed under the same name, files older than 3600 s refuse as leftovers, and the importer UNLINKS the file at completion AND at refusal (consume-once). `MEMRA_KV_HOST_VERIFY=1` digests carried in entries survive the handoff and still gate the next promote. The format is same-host by construction (native endianness; blue/green share the box). Receipts: `prefix_host_handoff_exports` / `_imported_entries` / `_imported_bytes` / `_skips` in /metrics plus `[prefix-host] handoff` log lines. Unit cells: round-trip over every field class, corrupt-frame skip with stream re-registration, truncation abort, header corruption/staleness/layout/stamp refusals, wiring (`host_handoff_tests`). The GPU/box gate (real blue/green flip with verify-on and a warm-vs-cold TTFT twin post-flip) is PENDING and named in the lane. Rollback seam: unset. |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_HANDOFF_MB` | **0 = whole resident pool** | Newest-first byte cap on the exported handoff entries, in binary MiB (lane/host-tier-deploy-warmth-20260901). The uncapped default is a deliberate decision: the pool is already operator-bounded by `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB`, and the handoff exists to carry exactly what the tier holds; at measured NVMe+sha256 rates a full 64 GiB pool writes and re-reads in the low minutes, inside the deploy tail, while the new slot is already serving (the import is a background drip). Set a cap on boxes where that tail is too long: selection walks the LRU from most-recent and keeps the newest entries that fit under the cap (an oversized entry is skipped and scanning continues, so the cap fills rather than stopping at the first big entry); skipped entries are counted in the export report (`skipped_over_cap`) and simply serve cold after the flip. |
| `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE` | **0 = OFF by design** | AGENT-PAUSE KV DEMOTION (lane/kv-pause-demote-20260831, tiering spec Arc E). A response whose generation ends in a COMPLETED tool-call block is a session about to pause for a client-side tool round trip. `1` (with the host tier armed, `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB > 0`): the retire path arms a pause candidate; after `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE_MS` untouched, the worker's per-tick sweep demotes the session's boundary state to the pinned host tier instead of waiting for SLRU pressure, freeing device cache budget for the pause's duration. Two shapes demote: the exact-fed PLAIN continuation-pool park (its boundary is first published through the existing `prefix_snapshot` capture machinery; ONLY a published host copy, or the tenant-cap evaporation that is the tier's law, releases the parked device cache), and the deepest resident device prefix entry prefixing the committed tape (demoted BY REFERENCE, removed only after the host copy publishes). A request arriving before the timer cancels: the resume consumes the park, or the admit probe touches/pins the entry, and the demote loses cleanly (the promote-vs-demote race matrix is unit-tested). The tool-calls signal is a WORKER-SIDE tail predicate (`toolcall::tail_ends_with_tool_call`, the same close markers the streaming parser owns) predicting the HTTP layer's `finish_reason:"tool_calls"`; both divergence directions are safe and documented at the predicate (a conservative miss just waits for SLRU; a false positive costs one demote round trip, never bytes). The tape's TRAILING STOP IDS are trimmed before the check (`pause_tail_window`, the session's exact stop set): the natural tool_calls finish lands the stop id after the close marker (`...</tool_call><eos>`), and the 2026-08-31 pod battery proved that shape is 100% of qwen3.8's real tool pauses; the untrimmed v1 predicate armed 0 of 6 natural turns (the same requests with `stop:["</tool_call>"]` armed 2/2), a "conservative miss" that made the flag a no-op on its target workload (darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/battery-20260831/pause-gates/RESULTS.md`, FINDING 1). SPEC/DSPARK parks are OUT OF SCOPE by design (live engine sessions: draft scratch, captured graphs, sampler state; spec Arc C scoping); their device boundary entries still demote. Vision sessions never arm (they enter no reuse tier); SWA-ring caches inherit the upstream flat-history snapshot refusal. DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-identical to today by construction (zero retire-path work, the sweep never runs). The pod battery RAN 2026-08-31 (darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/battery-20260831/pause-gates/RESULTS.md`, memra `09bcd66c9`): the pause MACHINERY is green (round-trip verify 17/17 `verify ok` + 8/8 repeat-deterministic, co-run decode tax -2.32% median with resume latency FASTER on 4 of 5 A3 gap buckets, failure paths executed, 8-turn twin regression-free), but on a documented DEVIATION (`stop:["</tool_call>"]`) because the v1 predictor armed 0/6 natural turns (FINDING 1, fixed by the `pause_tail_window` stop-id trim above). Still pending before any ON: the natural-shape arm receipt on the fixed predictor. Rollback seam: unset (or `0`) restores today's behavior exactly. Receipts: `prefix_host_pause_demotes` / `prefix_host_pause_cancels` in `/metrics` (demotes are a subset of `prefix_host_demotions`), plus `[prefix-host] pause armed` / `pause demote` / `pause cancelled` log lines. |
| `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE_MS` | **5000 by design** | Delay before an untouched tool-calls-paused boundary demotes (companion to `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE`). SET FROM MEASUREMENT, not convenience: the A3 session census (darklanes `research/kv-fastband-20260830/a3-census/RESULTS.md`) puts in-session gaps at p50 1.9 s with 56% of gaps under 5 s, so an UNCONDITIONAL eager demote would pay a host round trip on the majority of tool pauses; at 5000 ms the demote fires only on the long tail (p90 1.9 min, p99 6.7 min) where the device bytes would otherwise idle for minutes against a ~21-42 ms promote. The timer rides the worker's existing tick/recv_timeout machinery (no thread); an idle box wakes exactly at the nearest deadline. `0` demotes at the first sweep after retire, the deliberately A3-refuted unconditional shape, kept as a gate/diagnostic arm so the pod battery can force the demote path without waiting. Malformed values fall back to 5000 with a loud `[prefix-host] WARNING`. Predictive re-upload before the tool returns (the TokenCake pattern) is OUT OF SCOPE for v1 and named as the follow-up in the lane doc. |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX` | **0 = OFF by design** | KV BUDGET FLEX (lane/kv-flex-20260831, tiering spec Arc G): on a co-resident box (orn: ornith + qwen3-embedding-8b + qwen3-reranker-8b in ONE process) the chat model's device prefix cache may BORROW the idle headroom above its configured floor, and it SHEDS that borrow the instant capture-surface traffic arrives. Mechanism: the one static device-KV partition in the process is `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` (session KV is already dynamic, admitted per request against live free VRAM, and capture prefills are transient sessions through the same gate, so sessions flex by construction; the cache does not). With `1`, the effective insert budget becomes floor + grant, the grant re-derived once per scheduler tick as effective-free VRAM minus `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_GUARD_MB`; a capture request's arrival in the admission pass synchronously evicts the borrowed residency back to the floor BEFORE any of that request's admission math (lane gate included), so embeddings/rerank see exactly the headroom a no-borrow binary would give them (the zero-tax-by-construction law, direction inverted per Arc G); at session-pressure insufficiency the borrowed slice sheds FIRST, upstream of the nuclear `evict_all` ladder, preserving the floor residency the operator budgeted. Borrowed bytes EVAPORATE on shed, never demote (the `evict_all` law: an admission-tick shed must not stall behind D2H copies). SINGLE OWNER: `PrefixCache::total_bytes` stays the only byte accountant and the scheduler-owned `KvFlex` is the only grant writer; borrowed occupancy is derived (total minus floor), never double-booked, and the wiring is pinned by invocation-anchored unit cells (`kv_flex_single_owner_wiring` and siblings). DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-identical to today by construction (the grant static stays 0 and `kv_flex_effective_budget() == prefix_cache_budget_bytes()`); ON is unmeasured on serving hardware until the Arc G pod battery lands its three gates (shed-transition p99 under a synchronized embed burst, THE number; embed/rerank latency unchanged vs the no-borrow arm; chat-session correctness with flex armed). Rollback seam: unset (or `0`) and redeploy. Receipts: `kv_flex_borrowed_bytes` / `kv_flex_sheds` / `kv_flex_shed_ms` in `/metrics` (ms per shed = shed_ms / sheds), the `[kv-flex] on:` boot line, and per-transition `[kv-flex] shed (capture arrival)` / `[kv-flex] shed (admission headroom)` log lines; lane receipts in `research/kv-flex-20260831/`. |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_GUARD_MB` | **4096 by design** | Effective-free VRAM the flex grant never touches (companion to `MEMRA_KV_FLEX`). The shed is what protects capture admission; the guard bounds the mid-tick transient classes that allocate between grant refreshes (prefill/FA workspaces, capture prefill slabs: the `SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE` class plus margin). 4096 MiB is the fleet VRAM margin the orn launcher already budgets (deploy/ornith15 box arithmetic). Malformed values fall back LOUDLY to the default. |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_HOLD_MS` | **2000 by design** | How long the flex grant stays parked at 0 after a capture arrival (companion to `MEMRA_KV_FLEX`). An embeddings array admits one worker request PER INPUT, so a burst is many arrivals; the hold keeps re-borrowing inserts from interleaving with the rest of the burst, and every arrival re-arms it. Expiry alone re-opens the grant at the next tick refresh. Malformed values fall back LOUDLY to the default. |
| `MEMRA_FIRST_TOKEN_DEADLINE_GATE` | **0 = OFF by design** | FIRST-TOKEN DEADLINE ADMISSION GATE (lane/bench-debts-20260901, competitive-bench engine debt 3: deadline behavior under thrash; also the streaming analog the D2 shadow report named, darklanes `research/d2-shadow-20260831/RESULTS.md` §7). `1` arms a feasibility check at the worker's admission seam (post-tokenize, the D2 G1 placement; interactive lane only, capture routes exempt, re-evaluated on every defer tick): when `(exact prompt tokens + the live prime backlog: the sum of every active session's unprimed prefill_queue tokens) / MEMRA_PREFILL_FLOOR_TOK_S` exceeds the request's REMAINING wire deadline x1.5 (`admit_predict::FIRST_TOKEN_DEADLINE_MARGIN_PCT`, the same pessimistic-floor margin posture as the non-streaming gate), the request is refused with the shed contract's 429 (`EngineError::rate_limit_after`: rate_limit body + Retry-After clamped 1..=60 s, never billed, request not admitted, lane reservation released). WHY: the competitive bench's N56 cells (1.64x KV oversubscription) cancelled **70-74% of turns at the 90 s wire ceiling**, each cancelled turn burned its full deadline queueing behind the aggregate prime backlog and then discarded the prefill it had already bought, deepening the thrash (darklanes `research/competitive-bench-20260901/RESULTS.md` §6/§7); the existing handler-side `shed_deadline` cannot see this shape because its backlog gauge is the handler->worker queue, which stays ~0 while 56 admitted sessions grind inside the 64-session cap. The wire deadline rides `Request::wire_deadline`, stamped at all four surface submissions beside the receipt id (two lib.rs handlers + the shared surfaces body) and carried across step-OOM park replays via the Session. STREAMING INCLUDED BY DESIGN: the wire deadline bounds exactly time-to-first-token for a stream, so this is the honest streaming counterpart of `MEMRA_NONSTREAM_DEADLINE_GATE` (which stays static prompt+max_tokens arithmetic, 400-class, non-streaming only, ON); this gate is load-dependent and therefore 429-class (retryable), never a 400. This is NOT admit-predict enforcement: the D2 shadow predictor stays log-only and unratified; this gate refuses only on deadline-infeasibility arithmetic the caller can act on (retry later or raise timeout_ms). DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-identical to today (the gate block never runs; a thrashing box keeps converting infeasible waits into 90 s 408s); ON is unmeasured on serving hardware, the named GPU gate (PENDING, next hardware window) replays the bench N56 shape gate-ON vs gate-OFF and requires: deadline-burn rate (408s + client timeouts) down, goodput (served turns/hour) up, zero refusals in the N12 fits-easily cell, and Retry-After honesty receipts; receipts to darklanes `research/competitive-bench-20260901/`. Rollback seam: unset (or `0`). Receipts: the `[first-token-gate] id=<request_id> refused: est_first_token_ms=... prompt=... prime backlog ... remaining deadline ...` line; unit cells `first_token_wait_infeasible_arithmetic`, `first_token_deadline_gate_wiring`, and the shed byte-compat gate `admit_predict_reject_matches_shed_contract` (the same constructor). |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW` | **0 = OFF by design** | PREDICTIVE-ADMISSION SHADOW receipts (lane/d2-engine-gaps-20260831, darklanes Arc D2, shadow analysis `research/d2-shadow-20260831/RESULTS.md`). `1` logs ONE `[admit-predict]` line per interactive request at its first decisive admission consideration, carrying the D2 contract's P-tenant-p95 verdict: slot check first (`inflight(model) >= cap`), then the KV book (`kv_hat(request) + sum of in-flight kv_hats > budget arm`, where the arm is `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_BUDGET_MB` or, unset, the boot-derived value; see that row), with `kv_hat = B/token x (prompt + predicted_completion + 8) + fixed` from the admission cost model's own coefficients. The predicted completion is the causal per-(tenant, model) p95 over a rolling 512-sample ring (20-sample floor, global per-model fallback, clipped to the request's effective max_tokens; restart-volatile BY DESIGN: it is admission-shaping state, not a ledger). Line fields are locked by test (`verdict_line_locks_fields`): `id`, `tenant`, `model`, `verdict` (admit/reject-slot/reject-kv), `reason` (which estimator arm), `prompt`, `predicted_completion`, `kv_hat`, `booked_bytes` (the predictive kv_hat book: full predicted-completion KV per in-flight request, charged at admit), `booked_real` (the engine-charge book: the request-cost estimates the real VRAM gate used at each admit; the dual book is the stress-campaign-20260901 FINDING 3 calibration, so false-reject analysis reads which arm lied instead of a silent re-tune), `inflight`, `cap`, `budget_bytes`, `retry_after_s` (what an enforcing 429 WOULD send: earliest predicted in-flight completion, clamped to the shed contract's 1..=60 s), `exempt`. LOG ONLY, nothing is rejected; every request continues into today's never-rejected FIFO exactly, and the per-request cost is fixed-size arithmetic plus one O(512) select (admission-path budget honored). DEFAULT OFF, both arms weighed: OFF is byte-identical to today (the verdict blocks never run); ON is a measurement act whose false-reject bar the owner has not ratified (D2 RESULTS §6); the ENFORCING mode does not exist in this build at all, so the flag cannot reject even if misread. Rollback seam: unset (or `0`). Receipts: the `[admit-predict] shadow armed:` boot line, per-request `[admit-predict]` lines, `admission_inflight`/`admission_booked_bytes` in `/metrics` (operator scope; those two gauges run flag-independent). |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_BUDGET_MB` | **unset = derived at boot** (lane/admit-predict-calibration-20260901) | The box KV budget arm the shadow KV check compares against, in MiB (companion to `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW`). SEMANTICS AMENDED after the prod stress campaign (darklanes `research/stress-campaign-20260901/RESULTS.md` FINDING 3): the original "deployment derives it by hand" contract went stale the moment another lane's deploy changed the box config (box12 c32: 164 reject-kv against a pre-devpenalty 61,580 MiB arm on traffic that served 200). UNSET now means the worker derives the arm at boot from the engine's OWN numbers: `effective_free_bytes` at boot (the VRAM gate's read: driver free + pool-cached, taken after model load and boot calibration) minus the device prefix-cache budget (`init_prefix_cache_budget`, configured or derived) minus the admission transient reserve (max calibrated per-model floor, never below the 1,536 MiB static constant; `MEMRA_ADMIT_RESERVE_MB` wins where set), saturating at 0. A config-changing redeploy therefore re-derives automatically. A SET value remains the explicit operator override and wins outright. The `[admit-predict] shadow armed:` boot line states the resolved value plus `budget_src=` (env-override, or derived with all three formula inputs, or unset with the failure reason). Only when the env is unset AND the boot free-VRAM query fails does the KV arm stay unarmed (verdicts admit/reject-slot only, `budget_bytes=unset`), preserving the offline-analysis escape hatch. Both arms weighed: derive-by-default is receipted by the same campaign that refuted the hand-derived value; the derivation is logging-only arithmetic (shadow never rejects), so a wrong derivation costs a mislabeled shadow row, never a request. Rollback seam: set the env explicitly. Tests: `derived_budget_arithmetic`, `resolve_budget_precedence`, wiring anchors in `admit_predict_shadow_wiring`. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_EXEMPT_TENANTS` | **empty by design** | Comma-separated tenant ids that are enforcement-EXEMPT for predictive admission (companion to `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW`; D2 gap G7). SOURCE OF TRUTH is the console's `INTERNAL_ENGINE_TENANTS` list (darklanes `workers/console/src/activity.ts`); the deployment sets this env FROM that list so probe/watchdog identities never read as customer rejects (a 429'd probe reads as an outage, the unmetered-free-pass lesson). Matching is on the tenant row identity (`auth::meter_key`, `t:` prefix stripped). Exempt tenants still enter the in-flight book as LOAD; their would-reject lines carry `exempt=1` and the confusion matrix reports them separately as instrument noise. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_PARTIAL_RESTORE` | **OFF (default) since 2026-08-13** | `=1` arms immediate LCP-boundary partial restore FOR MEASUREMENT ONLY. **The lane that built this mechanism returned NO-GO** (`research/lcprestore-20260813/RESULTS.md`, merged 6249b0096): source->restored K/V state SHA-256 MATCH at splits 64/512/2048/4374, yet generated output BYTE-DIVERGES from a genuinely-cold request at splits **512 and 2048** (verbatim BYTE MISMATCH reducer lines committed), and `research/splitiso-20260813/` (verdict **BOUNDARY-IDENTIFIED**) then named the cause: it is not alignment, tiling, page, or split position, but the worker's own numerical-program fork `eager_mono && carried` — a cold Gemma request runs ONE monolithic `gemma4_prime` while a partial-prefix hit must run every suffix token through T=1 `decode_step`. Prime attends over transient pre-quantized/bf16 Q/K/V operands that are NOT part of the prefix-cache object, so matching K/V with mismatched output is expected: restored rows are the complete *cache* state but not the complete *decode program*. Divergence appears wherever that always-present perturbation crosses a greedy argmax, which is why failures are content-sensitive islands rather than a geometric threshold. The mechanism shipped default-ON in that merge by mistake and was flipped OFF the next day (hermes `e993b2839ea565f2`); it stays off until a suffix-prime path exists that is bit-identical to the cold program (the legal shapes are: refuse partial restore, or re-prime the suffix through the SAME monolithic program cold requests use — never a mid-request program switch). This is a DISTINCT defect from the Q27 dense restored-hit 11/60-token EOS (`research/cachesize-20260813/raw/attempt7-cache-hit-eos/`): `research/splitiso-20260813/RESULTS.md:206-212` states explicitly that it does not close that class. Both are instances of CLAUDE.md §One numeric program per request. When armed, it applies only to transformer-only entries with a non-empty suffix; hybrid recurrent and routed-MoE mid-entry splits refuse. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_SPLIT_TRACE` | off | `1` D2H-hashes every logical K/V byte plus position/len mirrors at a cold split snapshot, partial source, and restored destination. Exactness-gate diagnostics only; it synchronizes and is intentionally expensive. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = SAMPLED prefix-cache hits serve on the PLAIN path (the v0.93.0 posture), leaving greedy restores untouched — the one-flip fleet rollback for lane/sampled-hit-spec. Default ON: a whole-entry hit re-arms a warm spec session for sampled requests too. WHY THIS IS THE DEFAULT: v0.93.0 shipped the restore greedy-only and the DE deploy window then measured 3 cache hits / 3 plain downgrades / **0 restores** on a customer tenant, because API traffic is sampled by default (`temperature` 1.0, `main.rs default_temperature`) — the headline feature was inert for exactly the traffic that pays. The greedy-only premise ("a sampled hit's first token must be host-sampled from the boundary logits") did not survive the code it protected: at the time the COLD sampled spec burst did not host-sample its own first token either — it argmaxed `prime_logits`, as did every continuation burst (`MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY` fixed that on 2026-08-19; both paths now DRAW that token from the same row at Philox counter 0, so the identity below is preserved by construction instead of by both sides being greedy). Sampled spec is DISTRIBUTIONALLY exact, not byte-equal to plain sampling (`MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP` row). So the refusal preserved no property; it only split one request shape across two sampling programs (cold: spec, cached: plain). The restored session's seed rule and Philox counters ((0,0) at admit, as in any fresh session) are bit-identical to a cold session's, which is what the gate measures: per seed, the full-cover sampled hit's bytes AND its accepted/drafted counts equal the cold sampled leader's. The penalized-sampled refusal this row originally carried was **LIFTED 2026-08-19** (lane/sampled-spec-quality): the burst's penalty window now spans the session (`MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`), and a restored session's `committed` is the whole prompt, so a penalized restore reproduces the cold leader exactly like every other sampled cell. It returns, named, under `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION=0`. Gate: `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh qwen …` sampled cells, plus its `MEMRA_HITGATE_TEETH=1` arm which asserts THIS door holds sampled hits plain. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-HIT.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a sampled spec burst emits an ARGMAX at its own boundary (the pre-2026-08-19 posture); greedy is byte-unaffected either way. WHAT A BOUNDARY TOKEN IS: a burst's FIRST emitted token does not come from the accept walk — it comes off a logits row that already exists (the prime's last row on a cold burst, the row after the last committed token on a continuation burst, the prefix-cache entry's boundary row on a converted hit). All three were `argmax` in BOTH sampling regimes, so a sampled stream took a greedy token once per burst — measured, not estimated, on 18 real agent prompts at the API's own default temperature (1.0), 256 tokens each: **3.52% of generated tokens were boundary tokens at c=1 (one per 28.4 tokens — below the 1-in-32 the burst size suggests, because bursts also end on EOS, budget and admission yield), and 48.8% of those draws returned a DIFFERENT token than the argmax they replaced ⇒ 1.71% of a customer's tokens came from the wrong distribution.** At c=8 the rate falls to 0.50% for a reason worth knowing: the spec admit/demote gate (`MEMRA_SPEC_GATE`) keeps only 2 of 18 requests on the spec path under load, so the exposure is CONCENTRATED in the low-concurrency interactive tier rather than diluted by it. Customer-visible corollary: at temperature > 0 every request's FIRST token was the greedy token, identical for every seed. Default ON draws the boundary token from the request's own filtered/penalized target using the SESSION's Philox stream (never a second stream — that would be a fresh distributional bug), which is also what keeps a restored session's seed bit-identical to the cold session's. Oracles: `sample_check` arm 9 (20k draws, TV against the CPU-referenced target with the Monte-Carlo floor printed, plus teeth cells that FAIL the argmax rule and an unpenalized target), and `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh` (all three sites fired, deviation observed, per-seed hit==cold identity intact). Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = each spec burst restarts the penalty window from the `prompt` slice it is handed (the pre-2026-08-19 posture). That is the defect: a continuation burst is handed NO prompt, so `repetition_penalty` / `frequency_penalty` / `presence_penalty` silently reset at every burst boundary and saw an empty window for the majority of a sampled stream's tokens — i.e. penalties did not do what the API contract says over a long stream. Default ON seeds the window from `committed ++ prompt` (the last `max(penalty_last_n, 64)` tokens), which is what the plain sampler's own history does, and additionally counts the burst's boundary token (pre-lane it was never recorded at all). Byte-identical to the old posture for a cold turn-1 burst at the default window, so the change is confined to continuation bursts and restored sessions. This door also gates the penalized-sampled prefix-cache restore: with it shut, `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`'s penalized refusal comes back and names this flag. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESUME_SAMPLER` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-20** | `0` = whole-session spec resume goes back to the pre-lane probe that compared prompts and never samplers (`SamplerIdentity::legacy_admits`) — the rollback door AND the within-binary A/B arm for the refusal's cost. Default ON: a parked whole session's committed state is sampler-shaped in more places than the draft graph (penalty window, Philox stream position, draft plane), so a resume whose request sampler differs from the identity that shaped the parked session is REFUSED AND NAMED (`spec-reuse REFUSED: parked session's sampler differs on <field>`), on all three probe tiers; admitted resumes are tagged `[sampler=equivalent]` vs `[sampler=unchecked]`. `seed` is carried but deliberately NOT compared: the only parked state that bakes it (the sampled draft graph) is already re-keyed on it (`SampledGraphKey`), and an omitted seed draws fresh per-request entropy, so comparing it would refuse every seed-omitting sampled conversation for zero soundness gain (pinned by `seed_alone_does_not_refuse` / `seed_alone_still_rekeys_the_draft_graph`). This is a POLICY door, not an exactness door — with it shut, `SampledGraphKey` still refuses the cross-regime graph launch. `/metrics`: `spec_pool_sampler_refusals`. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SESSION-RESUME-PREDICATE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a sampled prefix-cache restore re-arms spec regardless of load (the un-guarded lane/sampled-hit-spec posture). Default ON admits a sampled restore only when the request is **SOLO** — nothing else in flight — and refuses otherwise, **named**: `[spec-restore-guard] sampled restore REFUSED: demand N > SOLO watermark 1 (band LOW=l HIGH=h; projected active=a, queued=q, http in-flight i)`. WHY, measured on box1 (2 x RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB, 27B NVFP4+MTP, sold shape = 4 860-token shared prefix, interleaved passes): the lever is **1.623x** solo at 192 output tokens (115.44 vs 71.12 tok/s, engaged 5/5 vs 0/5, `cached_tokens=4860` both arms so the delta is decode-only) and **1.350x** at c1 on the 60-token sold row — but **0.669x at c2** and **0.813x at c16**. The crossover is between c1 and c2: two concurrent sampled spec sessions already cost a third of aggregate throughput, because phase (a) steps each burst in a serial host loop and phase (c) excludes spec sessions from batched decode, so two of them serialise what would otherwise be a 2-wide batch. MECHANISM, and why the guard is sampled-only: admission (`choose_spec_k`, K=0 above LOW) and the tick demotion sweep (`spec_gate_high`) both decide 'busy' on the MTP band, and **sampled sessions are excluded from the demotion sweep** (the handoff would move generation to a batched row with its own sampler history and Philox stream, an unmeasured distributional seam). So a sampled restore admitted while the box was quiet rides the serial spec queue until the request ends, and nothing can clean up after it; a GREEDY restore in the same position IS handed back the moment load arrives, measures 1.00x at c16, and is left untouched. The watermark is SOLO rather than the band's LOW=2 because LOW=2 was measured on a 5090 with a short greedy shape (c2 1.08x WIN there) and admits exactly the c2 shape that loses hardest here — the same SOLO rule `gspec_k` and sampled/non-demotable dspark apply. The band is kept as a CEILING (`min(LOW, 1)`), so a PP-2 placement that admits no spec admits no restores. DEMAND is `max(worker-visible, HTTP in-flight)` read as LATE as possible (at the refusal site, after the restore's own 312 MB KV copy): reading `active + queue` at tick top leaves the HEAD of an arriving fan-out unrefusable, and one engaged row is worth ~9.6% of a c16 wave's aggregate. An operator `MEMRA_SPEC_K` pin or `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` owns the whole policy and bypasses the guard. Gate: `tools/spec-cache-gate.sh` with `MEMRA_CACHEGATE_TEMP>0` — its sampled verdict derives the watermark from the server's own boot line, requires the refusal line above it, and requires the 1.05x recovery at/below it. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-LOAD-GUARD.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a spec session restored from a prefix-cache hit publishes nothing (the pre-2026-08-19 posture). Default ON: after the restore feeds its prompt suffix, the session captures its OWN prompt-end boundary and the worker's existing publication sweep publishes it, so the next turn of a growing conversation hits a STRICTLY LONGER prefix. Pre-lane, publication was armed for cold sessions only (`spec_resumed == 0`) and both engine capture sites require a non-continuation burst, so a namespace learned exactly ONE boundary and turn 3+ could never advance it — multi-turn spec paid a constant, not a growing, share of prefill. WHOLE-ENTRY ONLY: the captured boundary is the restored session's own prompt end, so the republished entry satisfies `entry_pos == fed_len` and the rolled-back mid-entry partial-restore class stays closed. Costs one `Cache::snapshot` per restored session with a suffix (GDN conv/ssm copy) plus one cache entry, and a failed snapshot is silent (publication is an optimization). Gate: the growth cells of `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh` (turn 3's cached prefix > turn 2's, byte-identical to the spec-off twin, and the teeth arm asserts the pre-lane stall). Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_STABLE_BOUNDARY` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-21** | `0` = the spec tier's turn checkpoint and restored-session republication go back to PROMPT-END (the pre-lane posture) — the rollback seam and the toothed multi-turn gate's broken arm. Default ON ports the plain tier's 2026-08-09 stable pre-generation boundary law (`plain_checkpoint_boundary`: checkpoint BEFORE the last turn-marker run) to the spec tier, in three places: (1) `turn_ckpt` is captured at the stable boundary — a prompt-end spec checkpoint includes the template's live assistant-generation header (`<\|im_start\|>assistant\n<think>\n`), which every re-rendering client replaces, so `affinity_match` diverged ~2 tokens BELOW the checkpoint and spec affinity declined 100% of multi-turn agent traffic (measured: `spec-affinity: declined (history diverged at 6811 of checkpoint 6813)`); (2) WARM bursts (affinity-rewound / pool-resumed sessions priming their own delta) also stop at the new turn's stable boundary and refresh `turn_ckpt` there, mirroring the resumed plain session's prefill tick; (3) a restored session's extended-entry republication (`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH`) captures at the stable boundary instead of prompt-end AND installs a live `turn_ckpt`, so on a hybrid (whole-entry restores only) the published entry can actually serve the next re-rendered turn — pre-lane every republished entry's poisoned 2-token tail made the hit boundary FREEZE at the first lcp-split entry forever (measured: cached 6811 of 38,228 prompt tokens at turn 8 = 1.5% by turn 7 vs 98.6% on the plain arm; TTFT 10–13.6 s vs 0.36–4.5 s; spec LOSES 2.8–3.0× wall from turn ~3). Costs one extra `Cache::snapshot` per turn (GDN conv/ssm copy — the plain tier already pays it). Gate: the darklanes multi-turn gate (`research/multiturn-cache-20260821/`, cached_tokens must grow per turn + TTFT stays in the plain-with-cache class; the `=0` arm must FAIL with the frozen boundary named). Receipts: darklanes `research/multiturn-cache-20260821/FRSPEC-FIX-20260821.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ROUND_PROF` | off | `1` prints the WHOLE spec round's wall every 32 rounds. Added 2026-08-25 because the parts did not add up on the step37 TP2 stack: the eager draft step measures 1.27 ms (`MEMRA_SPEC_ANATOMY`: glue 92 / attn 280 / ffn 222 / head 670 us) and the t=2 verify walk 25.6 ms (`MEMRA_TCOL_PROF`: attn 10.1 + ffn 15.3), yet a K=1 round takes 177 ms — spec 9.9 tok/s against 76 plain, flat across K, head count and draft-vocab trim at 63-74% acceptance. Diagnostic only; no syncs added beyond the host readbacks the round already does. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ANATOMY` | off | `1` = eager draft-step phase timers (glue/attn/ffn/head, sync-bounded, one cumulative print every 128 steps) — the diagnostic seam the draftcost lane profiled with. Adds phase-boundary syncs while on; diagnostics only, never a serving default |
| `MEMRA_STATE_COPY_BATCH` | on | batch the dspark round's GDN state snap/commit copies into indirect-table launches (engine-bundle slice 1; the 192+112 per-round DtoD copies collapse to a handful) and the slice-3 slab commit's two-copy restore; `0` = per-layer view copies (the pre-bundle arm, kept as the rollback seam — byte-identity between the arms is gated by the dspark E2E cells) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_BOUNDARY_TRACE` | off | `1` names every sampled boundary draw on stderr — `[spec-boundary] site=<cold-prime\|burst-tail-commit\|burst-tail-nopending\|flush-pending\|restore-suffix-feed\|restore-full-cover> sampled=<id> argmax=<id> deviates=<0\|1>`. Diagnostics only (no behaviour change); it is how the boundary RATE and the deviation rate are measured rather than estimated, and the hit gate asserts on it. Costs one extra device argmax per boundary when on. |

### Tuning / diagnostic seams (sm_90a lane)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH` | 120a | build arch: `90a` = the Hopper lane (wgmma/TMA kernels compile; portable-PTX correctness path rides along) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SPLIT` | ctx-adaptive ladder | fixed FA-decode split size; ladder re-swept on 132-SM H100 round 35 — holds (sp32 +0.35% = noise) |

### Refuted on this lane (mechanism in ARCHITECTURE-H100.md; no flag shipped)

Monolithic + piecewise prime CUDA graphs (Lt address-variant kernels); W8A8/fp8/CUTLASS/
Lt-autotune prefill GEMMs at m=512 AND m=2048; FA3 shapes v6-v9; 12 k45 scheduling forms;
K3 solve ILP-split AND tf32 inverse-product; v6 exchange-free k45; Q8_0-EXACT int8 GEMM
(triple-refuted: per-block rescale 5.4x naive, 17x pipelined — ptxas C7517 serializes
cross-bank GMMA register reads); FA3-TMA-ring k45 rebuild (refuted by composition).
The remaining single-seq prefill residual (~27% vs vLLM's INT8 GEMM class) is an
owner-gated accuracy decision (w8a8-class numerics change model outputs).

(arXiv 2607.05147) — S_{j+1}·T(j) > E[tok](j)·t_draft with profiled t_draft/t_verify EMAs.
Measured FLAT: never cuts at accept ≥ 0.8 (31B depth: identical rounds/accepts, −0.4%
noise), par on the 26B depth high mode (278 vs 281, −1.2%); the fixed 0.7 self-keyed cut
already covers the low-accept case. Arm deleted; note: the survival estimator used the
current-conditional-as-proxy — a learned estimator (the paper) could cut smarter.

## Step TP doors (LIVE flags — the step37 2-card serving stack; not part of the removed ledger above)

These rows were appended after the removed-flags ledger and are live doors, not concluded
ones — filed under their own heading so a flag audit scanning the live sections sees them.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES` | off | Strict `0`/`1` device-resident NVFP4 routed-expert program. The historical TP2 arm shares one q8_1 quantization per rank across every selected expert; its existing receipt remains 7.8 -> 11.1 tok/s on the named two-card Step configuration. The artifact-selected W4A16 EP arm is family-independent and covers 1–128 active token rows, including the scheduler's complete decode batch and ordinary concatenated prompt batches: each rank owns a contiguous expert range, peer-reads the active input prefix once (grow-only prime/cache allocations may be wider than the current tail), rounds gate/up inputs and the down input at the declared BF16 boundaries, uses the frozen host-expf transcription, and scatters down rows into canonical token/slot positions in the model device's peer-accessible pool. One root kernel reduces every token's slots in original order at every batch width, so decode and speculative verification retain one route-reduction parenthesization. Batches wider than 128 rows retain the host-canonical oracle. `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` selects this backend automatically only when the source declares W4A16; the legacy explicit route still requires native P2P and owner-first devices. Default OFF outside `auto`; bounded-logit, sampled c=1, and sampled concurrency gates are required before a performance claim. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_TIMING` | off | `1` prints cumulative wall-clock counters for host-visible MoE routing, W4A16 whole-expert EP, the shared expert, the NVFP4 device routes program, and rank-local TP attention decode, plus an 8-phase split of the v1 attention driver (pos/qkv/normrope/gate/append/attn/oproj/shadow; each phase lap drains every stream, so the split perturbs absolute wall but attributes queued work honestly). Diagnostic only; never on in a gated or measured-for-receipts run. |
| `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` | off globally; W4A16 `auto` on | Strict `0`/`1`. At LOAD, large (>=2M elements) 2D BF16 checkpoint tensors stay bf16-resident (`GpuTensor::FloatBf16`, raw bytes, 2 B/w — halves both VRAM and decode read traffic for every preserved non-expert weight: shared expert, lm_head, owning-stage attention, dense FFN); the MoE router (1.18M) deliberately stays exact-f32 so routing never moves. `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` selects this door before load for its W4A16 expert-parallel backend; explicit `0` is the logged rollback. At DECODE (m=1, non-exact, non-canonical) those projections ride a one-block-per-row bf16 matvec (`matvec_bf16_f32acc`, f32 accumulate, deterministic tree reduce, same bits<<16 expansion contract as `deq`); ordinary m>1 prefill rides the chunked expansion path. W4A16 distributed decode/verify is the exactness exception: for t=1..32 its shared expert uses the row-wise dual-SwiGLU and down twins at every width, avoiding the former t=1-fused versus t>1-split numeric-class drift. Numeric-class door vs the f32-expanded cuBLASLt program — default OFF outside automatic W4A16 placement, run-gen argmax gate + 12-boot battery (DEV_ROUTES acceptance class). The step TP canonical-chunk ranks are explicitly excluded. Measured with the full v2 stack: 25.45 -> 27.22 tok/s, head 1.40 -> 0.74 ms/token. **STEP37 NVFP4 QUALIFICATION 2026-08-28** (box tree `7700e0b68`, memra-server `58f5fa92660e`, run-gen `20f9273487eb`). IT ENGAGES, heavily, AND IT WAS ALREADY ON: the step37 serving env (`agentic8.sh` ENVV) pins `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1`, so the 78.6 tok/s stable baseline ALREADY contains this door and it is NOT headroom toward the 90 tok/s goal. Engagement census of the NVFP4 checkpoint: 534 BF16 2D tensors at >= 2M elements (21.66 GB) qualify, namely attention q/k/o/v, `share_expert` gate/up/down, the three dense-FFN layers, `lm_head`, `embed_tokens` and the MTP heads; the 288x4096 router (1.18M) falls under the 2M threshold and stays exact f32 as designed, and the step TP canonical-chunk ranks are excluded by the `canonical_chunk_rows.is_none()` guard in `linear_bf16_chunked_inner`. A load-time announce (`[bf16-mmv] RESIDENT`, added to `model.rs` in this change) makes engagement a RECEIPT instead of an inference: 325 admits per boot with the door on, 0 with it off, in all 10 speed cells and both gate arms. It exists because the first sweep's `grep -c 'bf16.mmv'` returned 0 in BOTH arms, which was a missing-line receipt defect and not a no-engagement result. SPEED, interleaved x5 on curve-0400 (arms alternating within one stretch of box life, vendor-default sampling, spec off): OFF 58.19/58.58/58.73/58.55/58.81 median **58.58**, ON 78.06/78.42/77.65/78.04/78.56 median **78.06**, delta **+19.48 tok/s (+33.3%)**, per-arm spread 0.62 and 0.91. Read that as the cost of REJECTING the door, not as an available win. ARGMAX GATE PASSES, the one `MEMRA_PP_BF16` failed: with `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0` (spec on makes the oracle itself nondeterministic on this model) at `max_tokens=1`, one forward with no cascade possible, the first token is IDENTICAL between arms on all four real prompts (agentic8, curve-0128, curve-0400, curve-1000). The instrument rejects empty completions explicitly, because this is a THINKING model whose generated bytes all arrive in `message.reasoning`, so two empty results would otherwise compare equal and report a false PASS; every first-token cell was repeated within its arm and reproduced. Full-generation divergence appears on three of the four (char 4/115, char 9/854, char 719/1118) and is ordinary tie-break cascade, not a gate result. RUN-GEN PRIME GATE, both arms, four prompts: `verify-prefill argmax=70935 decode argmax=70935 MATCH` in all 8 cells, at logit maxdiff **1.965e-3 to 2.699e-3** with the door OFF and **8.779e-2 to 9.807e-2** with it ON. State that class honestly: roughly 20x the 4.2e-3 that `MEMRA_STEP_TP_QKV_FUSED` carries, but an order of magnitude BELOW the 9.798e-1 to 2.055e0 that the weight-PRECISION door `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` carries. The arithmetic explains the middle position: bf16 to f32 expansion is exact (bits<<16), so weight VALUES do not move and only the per-row reduction order does, but the door moves it on 325 tensors across all 45 layers instead of one projection family, so the drift is BROAD rather than deep. DEFAULT DECISION: the repo default stays **OFF** and the step37 serving env keeps pinning it ON. Reasons, stated: measured serving reality already depends on this door and rejecting it costs 33%, and it now carries the argmax receipt its sibling lacked, but the acceptance bar in this very row also names a 12-boot battery that has NOT been run, and per the flag-default law a default flip is the owner's decision with its receipts attached. REMAINING ADMISSION STEP: the 12-boot battery at the full serving env, in the 12/12 form the `MEMRA_STEP_TP_GRAPH` row documents (`MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2`'s battery, the other prior example, is REMOVED and its battery is discredited: it counted boots and throughput while the door corrupted text), after which the default is the owner's call. Receipts BANKED in `research/step37-bf16-mmv-20260828/`: `mmvgate.txt` (argmax gate), `mmvab5.txt` (interleave x5), `mmvprime.txt` with `prime-gate-lines.txt` (prime gate, both arms, extracted per prompt). Measured on the vast.ai spot box at `/root/mmv*.txt`, copied into the lane because a spot box closes and a receipt pointer into it dangles. GLM5_NEXT ACCEPTANCE, 2026-08-30. The L2 lane's factorized box A/B (research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tc-trunk-prefill-20260829/BOX-AB-L2.md, engine bac42f759d) measured this door alone on glm5 (arm B vs baseline, interleaved x5): 148 RESIDENT tensors per boot (34 x kda_q/k/v/out at 33.5M, 12 x indexer.attn_q_b at 6.3M, embed/head members; the 288x4096 router stays under the 2M threshold, exact f32, routing never moved), decode 21.3-22.0 -> 26.0-26.1 tok/s (+18-22 percent, non-overlapping spreads), prefill neutral, VRAM -10.0 GiB across the PP3 stages, load ~35 s faster (no load-time trunk dequant), first-token argmax gate 15/15 MATCH vs the f32 baseline with boot-deterministic outputs (no census needed). It is the residency prerequisite for MEMRA_PP_BF16's glm5 tensor-core prefill arm and the enabler of the adopted 2-card full-residency placement (88.7/88.9 GiB at load where the f32 trunk missed by 650 MiB). OWNER ACCEPTANCE 2026-08-30, verbatim: "accept if it can really work well on 2 cards. if the ttft and the tok/s will be too slow, we will be back for bigger fleet. but as it seems, 4 card for this model economy is not justifiable, just for the noise of supporting new model." Default form per this row's own step37 precedent: repo default stays OFF; the GLM SERVING RECIPE (BRINGUP.md, "THE SERVING SHAPE IS 2-CARD") pins MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1 together with MEMRA_PP_BF16=1. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DEV_ROUTER` | off | Strict `0`/`1`: routes the step TP MoE selection on device (`moe_router_sigmoid_topk`, the single-GPU dev arm's router) and feeds the device-routed expert program — the per-layer host logits readback (the last per-layer host sync in the MoE path) disappears, which is the prerequisite for whole-token CUDA-graph capture. Selection tie-breaking may differ from the host router: numeric-class door, run-gen argmax gate + 12-boot battery. Requires `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1`. Default OFF. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_QKV_FUSED` | off | Strict `0`/`1`: inside the v2 decode driver, ONE launch per rank computes all three rank-local QKV projections (`matvec_f32_qkv`, block-per-row over the F32 mirror, f32 accumulate, deterministic tree reduce) instead of ~12 chunked cuBLASLt calls per rank. NUMERIC-CLASS door (per-row reduction order differs from cuBLASLt): measured greedy tie-flip at depth 58/64, gate argmax MATCH, maxdiff class 2.4e-3 -> 4.2e-3. Requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2=1`. Acceptance = run-gen argmax gate + 12-boot battery (DEV_ROUTES class). 22.88 -> 24.99 tok/s on the 2x6000 pair. Default OFF. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2` | off | Strict `0`/`1` v2 rank-local Step decode-attention driver: the same kernels, operand values, and canonical O reduction order as the v1 driver, restructured onto a persistent per-runtime workspace with evented cross-stream ordering (pp.rs `BoundarySlot` discipline). Per layer per token it performs exactly one cuMemAlloc (the returned output row), no host round-trip, and no host stream sync — v1 spends 81% of its 1550us/layer wall on those classes (see the phase timers). Requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_F32_MIRROR=1` (refused otherwise: the bit-exactness claim against v1 holds per residency class) and the replicated decode input. Prefill and every non-attention program are unchanged. Gate: v1-vs-v2 greedy token identity + the run-gen prefill/decode argmax gate across a boot battery. Default OFF. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DCW` | off | Strict `0`/`1` device-counter-windowed step TP decode attention: KV append at `row = len_d - base_d` in-kernel, `inc_i32` advances the device counter in-stream, and `fa_decode_vec_q_v3_dcw` derives the attention view from the counters (one-partition law: at matching `(bucket, split_keys)` bit-identical to the host-planned path). Rebase tokens and sub-vec-floor contexts fall back to the host-row path. Requires the v2 driver + fused doors. Gate: 200-token identity vs the dcw-off tape + 12-boot battery. Default OFF. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_GRAPH` | off | Strict `0`/`1` whole-token stitched CUDA graph for step TP decode (increment B/C): every e/rank/root section of a token captures as an alloc-free child (no warmups — bodies carry dcw appends), assembled as one multi-device parent, launched once per token; bucket changes retarget the SAME exec via kernel/memset param updates (M1 path) instead of rebuilds. Eligibility: dcw + fused + dev-router doors, depth >= 96, no pending rebase; eager fallback otherwise. Host bookkeeping overlaps the replay; commit skips len-mirror sets (`commit_tp_kv_transaction_external` — the in-graph incs own the counters) with a rank-stream fence at eager->graph seams. Gate: 12-boot 200-token identity battery (12/12, 2026-08-21) + 400-token identity through 22 bucket retargets. Measured 40.6 tok/s vs eager 42.0 at depth 424 (interleaved x3): the launch itself costs ~1.3 ms/token at ~405 children — the door earns its keep with the multi-token replay loop, not plain decode. **RE-MEASURED ON THE CURRENT STACK 2026-08-25** (that verdict was taken when a token cost ~24 ms; today it costs 13.2 ms with ~6 ms of non-streaming remainder, so the tax was being weighed against a different total): ctx 4081, interleaved x3, token ids identical in every arm — eager 75.99 / 75.98 / 75.84 vs graph 59.39 / 61.27 / 61.09 tok/s, **-20%**. The remainder is dependency LATENCY (405 small serialized kernels that cannot fill the device), not launch overhead, so graph replay cannot recover it. Default OFF. GRAPH-LAUNCH HEADROOM GUARD (lane/graph-launch-guard-sweep-20260831): below `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` the token-graph step early-outs to the eager token step (`[step-tp-token] graph replay suspended:`) and the routed-prejoin graph door falls through to the eager routes path (`[step-tp-routes]` line); both once per process, byte-identical twins |
