| `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2` | **REMOVED 2026-08-29** | The slot-major (v2) TP expert-bank layout door. REMOVED from the engine, owner order, after its ON arm was proven to CHANGE GENERATED TEXT in serving: one binary, one boot recipe, greedy, the qualified spec-on serving env, real prompt `What is 17*23? Reply with the number only.` at 25 prompt tokens, first generated token `Ass` (derails, never answers) with the door ON vs `Got` (reaches `391`) with it OFF; the door alone reproduced the corruption in isolation over 9 boots while the other 23 serving doors together did not. Bisect receipts: `research/step37-reasoning-effort-20260829`. Removal receipts: `research/step37-bankv2-removal-20260829`. The row's original claim ("outputs BIT-IDENTICAL to v1, gated against the banked tape") was FALSIFIED; the mismatched v1-vs-v2 READER was never localized to a kernel, and with the TP v2 code paths deleted there is no reader left to localize. WHAT WAS DELETED: the env read, the TP-bank permute arm, the `sel_v2`/`sel_v2s` decode kernels, the `sel_v2_gu` fusion family (and its `MEMRA_SEL_GU_RPW`/`MEMRA_SEL_GU_WPR` sub-doors), the `down8`/`down8_rows` kernels (`MEMRA_SEL_DOWN8`), the two-column/t-row verify program (`step35_verify_moe_tn`, `..._device_routed_tn`) and the `moe-sel-census` bin. WHAT SURVIVES: the slot-major permutation itself (`nvfp4_matrix_v2_permute` + its layout unit test), now built ONLY for the always-slot-major `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_EP2` whole-expert banks; their `*_ep` kernels; `qmatvec_nvfp4_fast_v2` as the EP2 host-canonical oracle; the `QT_NVFP4_V2` grouped-GEMM instantiation (EP2 + the `moe-tp2-repro` offline harness via `MEMRA_TP2_QT=107`). PERMANENT SEAM: the server REFUSES to boot any model with `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2=1` or `MEMRA_SEL_DOWN8=1` set (`removed_bank_v2_doors_refusal`, worker.rs); the vars are read nowhere else, so a stale recipe fails loudly instead of silently serving a different program than it was written against. **CORRECTION AND SUCCESSORS, 2026-09-01** (`research/step37-bankv3-20260901`): the layout was INNOCENT and this row's falsification was itself wrong. The corruption was a defaulted `in_f = 0` argument at two `kq_fetch` call sites in the PREFILL grouped-GEMM tail (`moe_kq_sktail_kernel`), which made the `QT_NVFP4_V2` branch read each per-16 UE4M3 scale from inside the packed-codes region while the 4-bit codes stayed correct -- right weights, wrong scale, on every k-block but `kb = 0`. That reproduces the whole fingerprint the bisect could not explain: it is a PREFILL kernel (hence "divergence already in the prime's logits") and a wrong scale on correct codes is a bounded perturbation (hence MARGIN-dependent, not length-dependent -- the corruption was equally present at 613 tokens and merely invisible). It was live on `main` for EP2 prefill until fixed compiler-enforced at `1b18a61e8`; the device-side `nvfp4-bank-oracle` bin now gates the byte-identity claim this row used to make in prose. THE THREE PROGRAMS ARE BACK UNDER THREE SEPARATE DEFAULT-OFF DOORS -- `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM`, `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU`, `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8` (rows below) -- because one door arming three programs is why the bisect could name a door and not a mechanism. This row's names stay REFUSED: no code reads them, and honouring a 140-era recipe as "just the layout" would serve one third of what the recipe asked for. The refusal message names all three successors. |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM` | **on** (flipped 2026-09-01) | **PROGRAM 1 of 3** (`research/step37-bankv3-20260901`, milestone 3). Strict `0`/`1`: build the step TP contiguous NVFP4 expert banks (gate/up/down) in the SLOT-MAJOR row layout — slot g's 16 qs bytes contiguous at `g*16`, one coalesced 512B warp wave, and the two UE4M3 scale bytes packed at `nslots*16 + g*2` — and dispatch the `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2` reader over them. Pure byte permutation of block_nvfp4, same dp4a order, same per-slot scale multiply, same reduce tree, so BIT-IDENTICAL per (expert, row). **DEFAULT ON SINCE 2026-09-01, AS ONE COUPLED DECISION WITH `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8` — AND NOT BECAUSE THIS PROGRAM EARNS ANYTHING.** It does not: on its own it is measurably free (see the pricing sentence below). It flips because PROGRAM 3, the one program that does separate, is gated at its call site on `shard.slot_major`, which is `ep2 \|\| bank_slot_major_on()`. With this door OFF, `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8=1` is a SILENT NO-OP on the TP serving path — `[nvfp4-sweep] down8=false door=true`, no refusal, no warning, and the +5.48% does not happen. That is measured, not reasoned: the `v3-d8only` arm exists to bank it. So the deployable unit is these two defaults together, which makes this ONE decision recorded in two rows rather than two independent ones. The earlier posture — 'the successor to a door whose ON arm shipped fluent wrong text re-enters through gates, not through a default' — was satisfied first: the gates below were all green BEFORE this flip, and the flip carries its own deploy-grade battery on top of them. **BOTH ARMS.** OFF = the block_nvfp4 v1 banks and the `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel` reader, byte-for-byte the current serving path. ON = slot-major banks + `_sel_v2`. **ROLLBACK SEAM**: `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM=0` (no longer `unset` — unset is now the ON path), which by construction also disarms PROGRAM 3, so one variable rolls the whole deployable unit back to the pre-2026-09-01 program. The seam is EXERCISED as an arm of the flip battery (`flip-off`), not merely documented: a seam nobody has measured is not a seam. Strictness matters more now that the default is ON — `door_default_on()` accepts only `0` and `1`, and an unrecognized value KEEPS THE DEFAULT and prints `[nvfp4-door] WARN`, because under a naive `!= "0"` rule a typo like `=false` would leave the program armed while the operator believed it was rolled back. A second, independent seam exists and is receipted — `MEMRA_F16G_TAIL=0` (the 2-stage prefill tail) passes on the *pre-fix* binary where the deep-tail arms fail, so it would have dodged the entire 2026-08-29 incident; nobody knew, because nothing tied the door to the arm. **LAYOUT IS A PROPERTY OF THE BANK, NOT OF THIS FLAG**: the door is read ONCE at bank build and recorded as `slot_major` on `ResidentNvfp4{Column,Row}BankRank`; every reader (the `_sel_v2` dispatch, all four host-canonical oracles, and the grouped-prime `bank_qt`) branches on that stored field. The removed implementation re-read `nvfp4_bank_v2_on()` at each reader site, which is the same class of hole as the defaulted `in_f` that corrupted text. `qmatvec_nvfp4_sel_into` therefore takes `slot_major` as a REQUIRED argument with no default, and the grouped prime refuses outright if gate/up/down banks disagree on the layout. **GATES** (`research/step37-bankv3-20260901/RESULTS.md`): (a) `nvfp4-bank-oracle` device-side bit identity, unpack(v2) vs unpack(v1) through the same `moe_f16_grouped` entry, both step37 layer geometries, all four tile forms — this covers the PREFILL GEMM that no v2 gate ever ran; (b) end-to-end greedy byte identity ON vs OFF on real prompts INCLUDING prefill-heavy shapes; (c) the short-prompt output-content oracle (25-token margin-sensitive shape, first token + content-nonempty) — the instrument that caught the original corruption when 613-token gates could not, because the damage was margin-gated rather than length-gated. **A DECODE-ONLY BYTE GATE PROVES NOTHING HERE AND MUST NOT BE ACCEPTED AS ONE.** **GATES: ALL GREEN** (`research/step37-bankv3-20260901/RESULTS.md`, raw under `raw/`): (a) `nvfp4-bank-oracle` DIFF=0 on both step37 geometries across all four prefill tile forms AND on the decode readers, with a teeth arm (`*1.5f` injected into `_sel_v2`) that FAILS at exactly ×1.5000 and localizes to the P1 cells only; (b)+(c) 18-cell greedy tape byte-identical, `tape_sha256=d6fe97d0a1928e1b6c9d43985557189d41a3f75533e3da91faaff473d7624e68`, ON vs OFF **and vs a pre-restore `d3ac87f80` binary**, so "OFF = byte-for-byte the current serving path" is a receipt and not a claim; the ladder reaches 4,555 prompt tokens and the 25-token `17*23` probe answers `Got … 391` (the correct arm's fingerprint from the 2026-08-29 bisect). **PRICED, x5 interleaved fresh boots, vendor-default sampled, engagement proven from the server log: NO MEASURABLE WIN.** 105.35 vs 106.78 decode tok/s (−1.34%), 100.73 vs 100.80 wall, and the per-boot range `[104.66, 107.95]` OVERLAPS the OFF arm's `[105.11, 107.09]` — the layout is bit-identical and free, not faster. It is a PREREQUISITE for `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8`, which is where the win is, and that is the only reason to arm it -- and since 2026-09-01 the only reason it is a DEFAULT. **THE INERTNESS IS A RECEIPT, not an inference:** arm `D8ONLY` on the pre-flip binary set `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8=1` with this door absent from `/proc/<pid>/environ` and the engine announced `[nvfp4-sweep] down8=false door=true device_routed=true slot_major=false nsb=20 in_class=true` -- the door read, both other eligibility conditions met, and the program not running, with no refusal and no warning. **ITS OWN FLIP IS GATED TOO:** the flipped binary's default-ON program is byte-identical to the pre-flip EXPLICITLY-armed program across binary generations (18-cell tape, `GD82` vs `FON`, same `tape_sha256`), which is the equivalence md5 cannot give on a toolchain where two forced builds of one source differ. Sub-door `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_SM_STREAM` below. |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU` | **off** | **PROGRAM 2 of 3** (same lane). Strict `0`/`1`: run the routed gate and up sweeps as ONE launch (`qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2_gu`) instead of two — the sweeps share `sel`/`aq`/`ad` and have identical geometry, so blocks `[0,out_f)` take the gate bank and `[out_f,2*out_f)` the up bank. Per-row BIT-IDENTICAL to two `qmatvec_nvfp4_sel_into` calls; halves the sweep launch count and doubles grid fill. **STAYS DEFAULT OFF, RE-DECIDED 2026-09-01 WHEN ITS TWO SIBLINGS FLIPPED ON**: it is bit-identical and costs nothing, but it earns nothing measurable (per-boot range overlaps the OFF arm, and it carries the cell's worst within-arm spread), and the better-wins-by-default law rewards *better*, not *free*. An unmeasured win does not become a default just because its neighbours did — and keeping it OFF is also what keeps the flip battery a two-program statement instead of a bundle, which is this lane's entire lesson. It is NOT a prerequisite for anything: PROGRAM 3 needs PROGRAM 1's layout, not this fusion. **AND THIS FLAG'S EXISTENCE IS THE POINT**: in the removed implementation this fusion had NO door of its own and auto-armed on `nvfp4_bank_v2_on()`, so the bank layout and the fusion could never be priced apart. It is one of the three programs inside the bundle's measured −21.5% wall / −23.7% decode (`research/perf-chain-20260831` cell 1: 157.60 vs 120.25 decode tok/s, x5 interleaved), and attributing that number to the layout alone would have been wrong. **SUBORDINATE TO PROGRAM 1 by construction, not by env order**: the kernel reads only slot-major rows, so the launcher refuses banks that do not carry the layout and the call site additionally requires both banks to report `slot_major` plus exact `in_features`/`local_out`/`row_bytes`/`expert_bytes` equality. Arming it without the slot-major layout is inert (the guard falls back to two `_sel_v2`-less sweeps), never wrong — though since 2026-09-01 that layout is the DEFAULT, so arming this door now engages it unless `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM=0` is also set. **BOTH ARMS.** OFF = two separate sweeps, byte-for-byte the prior path. ON = one fused launch. **ROLLBACK SEAM**: unset. **GATES: ALL GREEN** on the cumulative arm `layout+gu` — oracle DIFF=0 for both the gate half and the up half against TWO INDEPENDENT v1 banks, and the 18-cell greedy tape identical. **PRICED, x5 interleaved: NOT SEPARATED.** 107.91 vs 105.35 decode (+2.43% on the median) but the per-boot range `[105.48, 111.44]` OVERLAPS the OFF arm's `[105.11, 107.09]`, and this arm carries the cell's WORST within-arm spread (5.51% decode, 5.15% wall) — a delta smaller than its own noise has measured nothing. Read it as free, not as a win. Its real value is what its EXISTENCE buys: the 2026-08-29 bisect could not name a mechanism because this fusion auto-armed on the bank predicate, and with its own door the layout and the fusion are separately priced for the first time. |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8` | **on** (flipped 2026-09-01) | **PROGRAM 3 of 3** (same lane), and it RIDES LAST — priced only on green gates for the two programs beneath it. Strict `0`/`1`: fuse the routed DOWN sweep with the route-weight combine into one launch (`qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2_down8`, the q8 `down8 w8` occupancy arm — cx-downkernel waves/SM 0.91 → 4.36 — ported to the NVFP4 banks). Block = `(32, n_sel)`: one warp per routed slot instead of one warp per (row, slot), and the `n_sel x out_f` partial-buffer round trip disappears. BIT-IDENTICAL to `qmatvec_nvfp4_sel_into` + `axpy_rows_seq_md_into`: same dot program, same 32-lane reduce tree, and warp 0 lane 0 replays the slot-ordered accumulation of `axpy_rows_seq_md_f32` in its own expression shape so the same compiler contraction applies. **DEFAULT ON SINCE 2026-09-01**, with `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM` as the coupled half of the same decision (see that row: this door alone is inert, and that is receipted, not assumed). It is the only one of the three restored programs with a measured win, it is bit-identical, and per the better-wins-by-default law that is a default-ON with a kill switch. Successor to `MEMRA_SEL_DOWN8`, which is REFUSED at boot: the old name required the old bank door, so one env var moved three programs. **THE DEFAULT IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS, AND THE RECEIPT SAYS SO:** `default ON` and `engaged` are two different facts here, because the call-site eligibility below (device-routed, slot-major, `nsb <= 32`) can disqualify the arm on a route or geometry the operator did not expect. Only the `[nvfp4-sweep] down8=… door=… door_source=…` line settles the second, and `door_source` distinguishes 'armed by the 2026-09-01 default' from 'armed by a recipe' from 'rolled back by the seam' — a default-flip receipt that cannot tell those apart has not measured the default. **ELIGIBILITY, all checked at the call site**: device-routed arm only (the host-routed arm folds the macro into `combine_w` instead of reading `md` on device), the down shard must report `slot_major`, and `nsb = local_in >> 5` must be `<= 32` — the fit-block class the reduce identity is argued at, launcher-enforced together with `n_sel <= 8` so `s_dot[8]` cannot overflow. **BOTH ARMS.** OFF = `_sel_v2` down sweep + `axpy_rows_seq_md_into`, byte-for-byte the prior path. ON = the fused sweep, which produces the accumulator itself (the combine is skipped, not duplicated). **ROLLBACK SEAM**: `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8=0` rolls back this program alone (keeping the bit-identical slot-major layout); `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM=0` rolls back both programs at once. Both seams are exercised as live arms, not just documented: the joint seam is the 6-boot `flip-off` arm of the qualification battery, and the SURGICAL seam (down8 `=0` alone on the flipped default, layout still slot-major) is the `flip-d8off` arm (raw/boot-D8OFF1.receipt: `layout=slot-major source=default-on` + `down8=false door=false door_source=env=0 (rollback seam)` + `slot_major=true`, engagement verified from the server log after a live request; the composition's output program was byte-gated in milestone 4's `v3-sm` arm). **GATES: ALL GREEN** — oracle DIFF=0 against `_sel` + `axpy_rows_seq_md` on the down geometry (the gate/up geometry is out of class at nsb=128 and the instrument says so rather than skipping silently), and the 18-cell greedy tape identical on the cumulative arm. **PRICED, x5 interleaved fresh boots, vendor-default sampled, engagement proven: THIS IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE THREE THAT SEPARATES.** decode **113.83 vs 107.91 (+5.48%)**, wall **107.75 vs 102.53 (+5.09%)**, within-arm spread 1.97%/2.33%, and the per-boot range `[112.59, 114.82]` has **NO OVERLAP** with the OFF arm's `[105.11, 107.09]` — nor with `layout+gu`'s own `[105.48, 111.44]`, so it separates from the arm directly beneath it. NOT a loop or acceptance artifact: **zero** loop exclusions in 20 boots and acceptance FALLS as the doors arm (0.7926 → 0.7747), the opposite of the degenerate-output signature; TTFT flat at 0.243–0.246 s, as a decode-sweep fusion should be. **CONTEXT FOR THE DEFAULT DECISION:** the removed bundle was priced at −21.5% wall / −23.7% decode, and all three programs together measure **+6.60% decode / +6.89% wall** here — ~3.6× smaller. The era measurement's door-ON arm ran the corrupted program and its own receipt shows acceptance 0.970 vs 0.935, the inflation signature; treat the ~24% figure as an artifact of the defect it was measured on, not as headroom still owed. **DEPLOY-GRADE RE-QUALIFICATION FOR THE DEFAULT FLIP (2026-09-01, the 12-boot battery this row previously said it owed):** measured as the DEFAULT on a flipped binary with NO door env set, against the rollback seam (`=0`) as the other arm, 6 interleaved fresh boots each, both request shapes out of the same boots, 12 distinct boot nonces, one binary. **Vendor-default sampled (the serving shape): decode 106.76 -> 112.57 (+5.44%), wall 101.20 -> 107.19 (+5.92%).** Greedy instrument: decode 113.68 -> 121.12 (+6.54%). RULE A fires on every arm and RULE B on both deltas, so the medians decide nothing and the NON-OVERLAP test decides: per-boot ranges `[105.58, 108.64]` vs `[111.16, 113.80]` decode and `[100.80, 103.75]` vs `[106.13, 107.72]` wall -- **SEPARATED on both metrics in both request shapes, 4 of 4**, with the per-boot sign **UNANIMOUS 6/6**. Not an artifact: **zero** loop exclusions in 192 reps, acceptance FALLS (0.785 -> 0.780), TTFT flat, spec engaged in every rep. 8-TURN CACHE TWIN (cache on, spec on, real conversations to **41,474** prompt tokens): the win holds in **16/16 turns (+3.99% to +6.08%)** and does not decay with depth, all 16 turns' output **byte-identical**, acceptance identical to 4 decimals, `cached_tokens` identical between arms, cache engagement proven by a bust probe (170,080 -> 0 cached, TTFT 3.09x slower). **ELIGIBILITY IS NARROWER THAN THE DEFAULT** -- unset the door and the program still needs `device_routed` and `nsb <= 32`; on the prod launcher that means `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1` must be set or the default is INERT, which the `[nvfp4-sweep]` line settles in one grep. NOT RESTORED with it, deliberately: `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2_down8_rows` and its launcher, whose only caller was the two-column/t-row verify program (`MEMRA_TCOL_FFN`'s named feature) — that program is out of this lane's scope and was not in the 140-era serving env, so restoring a kernel with no live launcher would have been dead code. |

Per-row program identical to `_sel_v2` → bit-identical. Geometry-limited and gated in the launcher on `row_bytes % 16 == 0 && in_f <= 4096` (step37 gate/up 2304B qualify, down 360B does not and falls back to single-row). **DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN**: it was never in the 140-era serving env and is not one of this lane's three priced programs, so it carries no pricing row; its predecessor `MEMRA_SEL_V2S` was documented as "geometry-limited and remains opt-in". Restored so a future lane does not repeat the archaeology. **ROLLBACK SEAM**: unset = the single-row `_sel_v2`. Inert without the slot-major layout — which is DEFAULT ON since 2026-09-01, so this sub-door is now one env var away from engaging on the serving path and stays UNPRICED: do not arm it without its own cell. |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU_RPW` / `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU_WPR` | **off** / **off** | Sub-doors of `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU`, both **UNPRICED**, both inert unless `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_GU` is on — and that door stayed DEFAULT OFF in the 2026-09-01 flip, so both remain two env vars away from the serving path. `RPW=2\|4` is the multirow twin (`_gu_r2`/`_gu_r4`): one block covers RPW consecutive output rows of one slot, so each thread's activation group is read ONCE and reused across the RPW gate+up dots (the mmvq multirow recipe) with RPW weight streams in flight. Per-row accumulation order and reduce tree are the base kernel's → BIT-IDENTICAL; falls back to 1 when `out_f % rpw != 0`. `WPR=1` is warp-per-row (`_gu_wpr`): one warp owns a row, 4 slots per lane, warp-only reduce with no shared memory and no barrier, `blockDim.y` packing 4 rows per block. **`WPR` IS A NUMERIC-CLASS DOOR** — the per-row REDUCTION ORDER changes, values move by ULPs, so a bit tape CANNOT apply and acceptance is the run-gen prefill/decode argmax MATCH plus the 12-boot battery (the `MEMRA_STEP_TP_QKV_FUSED` / `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` / `MEMRA_RMS_BLOCK` class). Its motivation is measured: the base `_gu` kernel gives one row to a 128-thread block and `in_f=4096` means each thread owns exactly ONE slot, so the block pays a two-stage reduce to combine 128 partials — nsys 23.6 MB in 29.8us = 792 GB/s, 44% of this pair's GDDR7 peak, while wide-row members of the same family sit at roofline (lm_head 1.62 TB/s). The reduce, not the load, is the cost. **DEFAULTS OFF BY DESIGN**: this lane's three priced arms are all bit-gateable on purpose, and admitting a numeric-class arm in the same battery would mix acceptance classes; `WPR` needs its own argmax cell before it may be armed anywhere. **ROLLBACK SEAM**: unset = the base `_gu` kernel. |

RESIDENCY CONSTRAINT: the mirror is ~0.9 GB per card and does NOT fit beside a 262144-token cache reservation on 96 GB — the first long-context cell died in CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY, and the interleave above therefore ran MEMRA_MAX_CTX=16384 on BOTH arms. A serving flip needs either a smaller ctx reservation or dropping the bf16 slab for decode-only ranks. The door also covers the bf16 GEMVs OUTSIDE the TP banks — the LM head, the shared-expert rows and the dense-FFN layers — through a pointer-keyed mirror cache built on first decode use (`matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror`), which adds roughly 1.7 GB per card on top of the 0.9 GB the attention mirrors cost. **BOTH ARMS (QKV + o_proj), ctx 4081, interleaved x3, tip 98d601256:** off 75.74/75.78/75.93 -> on 80.27/80.78/80.29 tok/s = **+6.1%**, argmax gate MATCH in every cell at logit maxdiff 2.055e0. That is 12.46 ms/token against the 13.19 ms baseline; 90 tok/s needs 11.1 ms, so the measured remainder is a q8 head (-0.16 ms), q8 shexp down (-0.33), q8 dense FFN on layers 0-2 (~-0.22) and then a W4-class attention projection running at the ~1.9 TB/s the q8 mmvq path already demonstrates (~-0.48). |
| `MEMRA_SEL_GU_WPR` | **REMOVED 2026-08-29** | Warp-per-row arm of the v2-bank gate/up fused sweep (numeric-class). REMOVED with `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2` (see that row): the kernel read only the v2 banks and never carried its argmax gate. Removal receipts: `research/step37-bankv2-removal-20260829`. |

Combine = t=1 kernel over t*n_head stacked heads (empty splits are the NEG_INF no-op). Launcher embeds the big-rig split ladder and refuses env split overrides. GATES: run-spec SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS K=1..8 (tiny) + K=1,2 at agentic ctx. Spec ladder at agentic turn-8 ctx: 50.6 -> 54.4 (rows fa) -> 62.4 (head convert killed: the FloatBf16 chunked matmul dequanted the whole 1.24GB LM head per verify round; bf16 rows fast path = t=1 head program per row) -> 66.87 (verify rope pass: same-session rope-rows, 3 launches/rank/layer) = 0.97x eager at accept 89.6%. Requires MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER=1 + MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL=1. |

| `MEMRA_PRIME_TROWS` | off | **SERVING RECEIPTS 2026-08-25 (tip affb4a081, 2-card box, streaming, C=1, temp 0, two interleaved rounds):** TTFT 11.16->2.375 s at 182 prompt tokens, 73.51->10.27 s at 1271, and 4081 tokens went from HTTP 408 (prime could not make first token inside the 90 s `timeout_ms` ceiling — UNSERVABLE) to 32.30 s served. Prime rate 17.2->126 tok/s. IDENTITY: the served token ids match the tokenwise-class run-gen reference exactly `[764,624,15059,2859,223,19,63,342]`; the server's own host-canonical batched prime is the outlier (`20` at slot 6), so this arm moves serving TOWARD the oracle. Still off by default pending the owner's flip. PREFILL-BY-T-ROWS (the #2 serving arc, 2026-08-25): prime the prompt through the same-session t-row walk in 32-row chunks — one rope/append + one fa + one o_proj join + one t-row MoE sweep per rank per layer per chunk, every row the t=1 decode program bit-for-bit. This is the TOKENWISE-prime ORACLE class (strictly more conservative than the batched host-canonical prime it replaces), so the gate is token identity vs MEMRA_PRIME_TOKENWISE=1. Motivation receipt: step37 TP2 host-canonical prime = ~17 tok/s (4.1k prompt = ~240 s TTFT; the memra-server bench 408d on it) vs vLLM prefill 0.6 s. Requires MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER=1 + MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL=1 (+ TCOL_FFN/SPEC_FA2 for speed); text-only fresh primes; suffix/vision/dflash-tap primes fall through. |

| `MEMRA_TEMP` / `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` / `MEMRA_SEED` (safetensors path) | greedy | **FIXED 2026-08-25:** the safetensors TEXT path IGNORED these and still printed `(ST greedy decode)`, so every sampled step37 measurement taken with run-gen was really greedy — the sampler it parsed belonged to the GGUF `generate_with` path. `MEMRA_TEMP>0` now arms the serving device sampler INSIDE the async chain (`filter_stats` -> `gumbel_perturb_filtered_col` -> argmax into the resident `token_d`), so a sampled stream keeps the chain's no-host-sync property and the per-step counter gives each token its own draw. Sampling forces the PLAIN head, because the split-head fast path never materializes a logits row. The bench line now names the shape it measured. Owner rule this serves: "we dont serve greedy, for real benchmarking we use sampling". **MEASURED 2026-08-25 (W8 armed, ctx 4081, chain sampling on device):** greedy 82.21 tok/s, temp-only sampling 82.12, filtered (top_k 20 + top_p 0.95) 78.72. So UNFILTERED sampling is free once the per-token sampler allocations are hoisted, and the entire remaining sampling cost is the top_k/top_p threshold search — `filter_stats_coop_f32` measured ~500 us per token, one instance per token, which is ~4% of a 12.7 ms token and the next thing to attack in that path. Two bugs were fixed on the way here: the chunk-boundary token was drawn by a host argmax (one token in eight was greedy in a run that called itself sampled), and sampling had been giving up HEAD_SPLIT although the split path does materialize the full row. |

Spec is now 0.92x of plain-with-W8 (80.2), up from 0.79x. At the model's NATURAL 262144-token context this OOMed — **until `MEMRA_SWA_RING=1` freed 16.4 GB on card0 (2026-08-26); with the ring the whole door fits with ~15-17 GB spare and PAYS: W8 80.16 and W8+hybrid 81.46 against plain 75.97, i.e. +5.5% and +7.2% at the served context.** The hybrid half, which measured +0.1% at MAX_CTX=16384, earns +1.6% at natural ctx where cache pressure is real — so its default-off is now a memory choice, not a measurement one. **THE DOOR NOW COVERS THE SPEC VERIFY WALK TOO (2026-08-26):** nsys over a K=1 spec window put `matvec_bf16_b4_tcol` at 24.8% of GPU time and `matvec_bf16_qkvg_tcol` at 12.3% — 37% of the verify still streaming bf16 because only the decode twins had been replaced. `qmatvec_q8_0_qkv_rp_t` / `_b4_rp_t` (one warp per (row, column), per-row program identical to the single-row kernels) fix that: spec K=1 at agentic ctx goes **58.44/58.14 -> 66.01/66.08 tok/s (+13%)** with verify-wait 4310 -> 3484 ms (-19%), and because the PRIME rides the same walk its wall drops **33.74 -> 28.51 s (-15.5%)** on a 4092-token prompt. run-spec SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS both arms (output identical to the plain target), acceptance unchanged (230 vs 231 drafted). | `MEMRA_W8_VIEW` | off | Strict `1`: extends the W8 hybrid half (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` + `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`) to the bf16 GEMVs whose weight is a ROW-RANGE VIEW of a resident slab, i.e. the lo halves that `MEMRA_HEAD_SPLIT` and `MEMRA_SHEXP_OVERLAP` keep on rank 0 (LM head lo 4096x64448, shexp down lo). Those halves run CONCURRENTLY with their q8-mirrored hi twins on the other card, so the slow bf16 half was the critical path: the byte ledger prices them at 0.16 + 0.17 ms/token (`research/step37-decode-headroom-20260828/census-ledger-at-78.6.txt`). Mechanism: a view-typed twin of the mirror path (`matvec_bf16_view_via_q8_mirror` + `encode_q8_0_from_bf16_view`, identical kernel and per-row program), engaged from `matvec_bf16_into`'s view branch only when W8 + hybrid are already on. Landing it forced a mirror-cache correctness fix that applies to ALL W8 mirrors: the cache key is now `(ptr, in_f, out_f)`, not the pointer alone, because a view starting at row 0 carries the PARENT slab's base pointer and a pointer-only key would hand the lo half the FULL head's mirror and read 2x past its rows. Engagement is announced unconditionally (`[w8-view] mirror built ...`), one line per distinct shape, so a sweep can prove the door in BOTH directions. NOT armed by `arm_step37_serving_defaults`; unset or `=0` is the rollback seam. NUMERIC CLASS: same class and argmax acceptance as the rest of `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` (weight-precision q8_0 mirror of bf16 rows); run-gen decode argmax MATCH on real prompts at logit maxdiff 9.546e-2 (curve-0400, equal to the w8view=0 baseline's own 9.546e-2) and 1.034e-1 (curve-1000), the `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` ~9e-2 pass class, with the door ENGAGED (view_mirrors=1). **MEASURED INERT ON THE SERVED ROUTE (2026-08-28, interleaved x5 vendor-default sampled, tip 553a072471+patch):** plainv 78.46 vs plain 78.41 (+0.1%), and the mirror announce fired in ZERO of 20 server boots including every `=1` arm, because the served step37 decode (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2` t-walk / spec verify walk) never calls `matvec_bf16_view_into`; only host `decode_step_h` and `decode_step_chain` (run-gen's paths) do. The specv arm's 94.61 vs spec 92.13 is spec noise (spec's own spread 89.66..97.69), not this door. **Default stays OFF**: it buys nothing in serving until the walk's head/shexp lo GEMVs are routed through the view mirror, and the byte ledger caps that prize at ~0.33 ms/token. The `(ptr, in_f, out_f)` mirror-key fix that landed with it applies to ALL W8 mirrors and stands regardless. Receipts: `research/step37-decode-headroom-20260828/` (`RESULTS.md`, `s37h-spec-results.txt`). |

It therefore made the binary an f32-only instrument: the long-context ladder, the spec-at-depth cells and the TP2 gates are not reference-parity runs, yet every one of them silently measured the f32 cache while being written up as running the ship defaults. Cost: a full 100k ladder rung (~9 min of prefill) before a state-alloc probe against `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvq` read 91,475 MiB against the default arm's 96,243 MiB — the 4,768 MiB the default was supposed to be delivering. The pin is now scoped to runs that actually make a golden comparison (`--goldens` or `--prompts`), and **every receipt header carries a `# cache kv_quant=... idxq=... golden_pin=... seams_env=...` line**, because the root cause was not the pin but a receipt that could not state which arm it ran. Golden-comparison receipts are BYTE-IDENTICAL across the change (hidden, verify-bit, tp2-gate, tp2-prefill-gate). |

| `set_router_dev` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=routerdev=0` disables) | **ON** (`ROUTER_DEV_DEFAULT` true, flipped 2026-08-31 on receipts) | qwen4_exp DEVICE MoE router (devtwin lane): `qwen4exp_route_topk_f32` runs the full `host_route_softmax_topk` program on device — softmax over 512 logits (order-sensitive reductions sequential on one thread, host op order verbatim; exp through double, the one op not bit-pinned to host libm), top-10 under the pinned tie rule (weight desc, index asc), renorm with the 6.1035156e-5 floor — and writes sel/w(/tok-map) straight into the slots/arrays the grouped NVFP4 dispatch and the captured MoE-tail graphs consume. Engages on grouped NVFP4 paths only (t==1 decode, graph-driver route slots, merged verify columns); the per-expert prefill executor, the DeviceBf16 draft bank, and the TP2 route keep the host twin (they consume host expert ids by construction). **Default ON by design, with the reasons and receipts stated:** exactness contract = selection ids+order EXACT vs the host twin + weights ULP-bounded, gated by tiny arm 0f `gate_route_kernel` (real 512/10 geometry + boundary-straddling tie batteries; rig AND box receipts: worst weight ULP 0) and the live cross-surface audit `MEMRA_Q4E_ROUTER_AUDIT=1` (every device route in a real run recomputed on host and hard-compared, sigrouter precedent; **250k+ rows, ZERO selection mismatches, worst weight ULP 4 on box / 0 on rig** — the bound is per-host because the kernel's exp is double-rounded rather than glibc's, which is why the audit rides every gate battery). Perf (interleaved x5, decision medians, box): **spec at ship admission thinkon 1.168x (75.0 -> 87.6 tok/s), thinkoff 1.174x (-> 120.4), efflow 1.160x (-> 90.2), raw 1.194x (-> 136.2), long-724 1.116x (-> 68.3), all with BYTE-IDENTICAL 256-token chains**; K ladder 1.14-1.18x over K=1..8; plain decode 1.099x with decode graphs ON, 1.112x with them OFF. Rule gates with the seam armed: verify-bit 24/24, spec-gate byte identity raw/thinkon/long, seam-gate 24/24 argmax (worst KL 0.00000), **tp2-gate 24/24** (worst rel 3.0e-5), tiny 26/26. **PAIR IT WITH `idxcache`** (both defaults flip together — the stack is the unit): this seam ALONE with decode graphs ON measured **0.906x**, a real hazard for anyone enabling half the stack (PROFILE-9 §3a; mechanism named unexplained with an nsys residual). A/B twins `--ab-seam routerdev` and `--ab-seam devtwin` (the stack). Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=routerdev=0` / `set_router_dev(false)`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/devtwin/, perf/PROFILE-9.md. |

Read once per process; changes no arithmetic (a sync cannot move bits). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/devtwin/, perf/PROFILE-9.md §3. |
| `set_idx_cache` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxcache=0` disables) | **ON** (`IDX_CACHE_DEFAULT` true, flipped 2026-08-31 on receipts) | qwen4_exp DEVICE-resident indexer raw-key cache (devtwin stage 3): below the QSA selection horizon ((base_pos+t)/block <= budget — every row structurally full, real geometry position < 2051) the per-layer idx_proj dtoh exists ONLY to feed the host raw-key cache for a possible future scored row; this seam appends the k-part rows d2d (`copy_rows_col_f32`, exact byte moves — no arithmetic) and materializes the host cache LAZILY at the first scored chunk (same bytes dtoh'd later, so the scored path is bit-identical by construction; mid-run flips pay their debt in both directions — h2d backfill on OFF->ON, delta dtoh on ON->OFF). Kills the census's **12 idx_proj blocking dtoh per forward (+1 per draft chain step)** on every sub-horizon shape and the [t x 640] f32 dtoh per chunked-prefill chunk. Rewind clamps the device row count alongside the host truncation (verify_rewind + mtp_rewind). Selection sets are STRUCTURALLY identical below the horizon (the same fast path, decided from positions alone) and bit-identical above it (same bytes); gates: the tiny chunked arms (mtp-spec-ring chunk 8 / prefill-extend chunk 5) cross the tiny horizon (position 11) with the seam armed, exercising device-append, the lazy catch-up boundary, and the scored path inside existing byte-identity/tolerance arms. The TP2 indexer keeps its host twin and PAYS THE HOST-CACHE DEBT on entry (a single-card prefill under this seam can leave the host cache lagging, and the TP2 migration is one-way — a silent lag would build pooled keys from a short cache and select wrong blocks, with the length assert compiled out in release). **Default ON by design, decided WITH `routerdev` as one stack** (see that row): isolated plain-decode A/B 1.024x, and it is the half that makes the router's sign positive with decode graphs ON; stack rows and gates are in the `routerdev` row (incl. tp2-gate 24/24 with both armed). A/B twins: `--ab-seam idxcache` (decode) and `--router-ab` with `--ab-seam idxcache`/`devtwin` (spec loop). Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxcache=0` / `set_idx_cache(false)`. | `set_longatt` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=longatt` forces ON, `longatt=0` forbids) | **AUTO** (`LONGATT_MODE` init 0) | qwen4_exp long-context QSA attention form (yarn affordability lane): attention runs `sdpa_blocklist_f32` over each row's own bounded position list (<= 2052 entries on real geometry) instead of a dense [t, t_kv] mask. **AUTO engages when the block-list form reads strictly fewer KV rows** — as soon as the indexer drops blocks (any non-full row; real geometry: position >= 2051) — **and always past t_kv 12288**, the masked kernel's smem bound (where the dense path REFUSED before this seam existed). **Default AUTO by design, with the reason stated:** this is where QSA's bounded-attention claim becomes real — the dense mask still READS every t_kv row (it only zeroes scores), so masked decode is O(context) bytes, while the block-list form reads <= 2052 rows at any depth (motivating measurement: smoke ladder at a 4k fill spent 97% of the decode token in `qsa.sdpa`, 673 ms/token). Below the drop point every row IS the full causal prefix, so both forms read the same rows and AUTO keeps the untouched masked path (every historical receipt byte-stable). The ON arm is BIT-IDENTICAL to the masked kernel on the same selection (a masked entry contributes exact-0.0 softmax/V terms in the same ascending order). Gates: tiny arm `fixture-longatt` (whole tiny program forced through the block-list path, bit-identical to the masked run) + kernel oracle arm 0f (bit identity vs masked at t_kv 4096; host-twin tolerance past the bound) + `prefill-extend` (chunked driver, modelplan tolerance class — chunk-shaped GEMMs) + the real-checkpoint yarn/ladder receipts. | `set_kv_quant` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvq` arms, `kvq=0` disables) | **ON** (`KV_QUANT_DEFAULT` true since this lane's receipts landed — owner decision K=q8, V=q5 executed per the flags law: within-config exactness green (spec byte-identity 6/6, verify-bit 24/24 ×3, envelope 24/24 @ 3.0e-5), cross-config drift = the near-tie quant class stated in KVQ-CELL.md (worst rows flip between the eos pair), greedy forks on the valid raw instrument in the f32 class, and the quantized cache measures FASTER: 13.36-13.39 vs 13.53-13.57 ms/token interleaved **- that perf half is DEPTH-SCOPED to SHORT CONTEXT and no longer supports the flip on its own: measured 2026-08-31 at a 100,000-token fill the sign REVERSES to -7.4% decode (33.62 vs 36.30 tok/s) and -7.3% prefill wall (561.4 vs 523.3 s), because the block-list path reads up to ~2,052 rows/token at depth while the shallow row reads almost none. **MECHANISM CORRECTED 2026-08-31 (memory lane, PROFILE-C0.md §2): it is NOT dequant ALU cost, it is the LOAD INSTRUCTION COUNT.** `q4e_deq_q8` recomputes the block pointer from the element index, so the score loop re-loads the SAME fp16 block scale once per element — sm_120 SASS, score-loop inner iteration per 8 K elements: f32 twin 37 instrs / 8 KV loads, `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` **120 instrs / 8 KV loads / 8 redundant scale loads**. | `set_idx_sel` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxsel=0` disables) | **ON** (`IDX_SEL_DEFAULT` true, **flipped 2026-09-01 on receipts**; introduced OFF 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp DEVICE QSA indexer top-k SELECTION (`qsa_index_topk_u32`, 262k perf lane). `set_idx_dev` moved the block SCORING to the GPU and then dtoh'd the whole score slab so the HOST could run `top_blocks_ascending` per row; at the product window that host half WAS the wall. Measured before: at a 131,072 fill `qsa.idx_host` was **51,235 ms — 83.0% of a prefill chunk** with every GPU section flat within 4%, growing 16x for a 9% depth increase between 120,000 and 131,072, which priced the whole 262,144-token window down from ~32 to ~15-18 tok/s. (The sub-batch/transient-allocation hypothesis for the same cliff was TESTED AND KILLED first — `MEMRA_Q4E_IDX_SCORE_CAP_MF=128` removed the sub-batch transition and bought 4.4%.) This seam runs the selection on device and reads back `rows x budget` u32 (4 MB at the target geometry) instead of the `rows x blocks` f32 slab (up to 128 MB per sub-batch). After: `qsa.idx_host` is **1,066 ms / 9.6%** of the same chunk (48.0x less). **Default ON by design, and every arm is measured** (new-flags law: default, both arms, rollback seam, receipts, in the change that flips it). **Perf, both arms:** interleaved SAME-FILL A/B at 131,072 (`--ladder-ab-seam idxsel`, one prefill, both arms, exclusive measurement lock, sole tenant) off 56.64 ms / 17.66 tok/s vs on 32.25 ms / **31.01 tok/s = 1.7562x**, 7 reps per arm (escalated from 5), 224 warm samples per arm, spreads 2.40% / 2.12%; reproduces an independent two-process pair (1.76x) on both arms. Target window **262,144: 15.21 -> 23.44 tok/s (1.54x)** with prefill **4,779.1 -> 1,439.2 s (3.32x)**. The DISCONTINUITY is gone: 100,000 -> 131,072 was 1.9x slower for 1.31x depth and is now -7.6%, with prefill flat across a continuous 262k fill (82.8 -> 96.4 s per 16k against the OFF arm's 105 -> 475 step). Variance improves as well — both deep rungs auto-escalated to x5 OFF (2.74% / 1.62%) and sit at 0.36% / 0.02% ON, because the 48-thread host top-k pool was also the jitter source. **Exactness is by CONSTRUCTION, not tolerance:** the key is `(~f32_total_asc_u32(score) << 32) \| block_index`, whose ascending order IS the host `sel_cmp` (score desc under `total_cmp`, block index asc) over the WHOLE f32 domain, and keys are distinct so the k-th smallest is a single element. Gates: tiny arm 0i (`gate_qsa_index_topk`) compares ids AND emitted order against `top_blocks_ascending` at REAL budget 512 up to **65,536 blocks = the 262,144-token window's fill/4**, on ragged sub-batch slabs, plus the tie classes — all-zero (STRUCTURAL here: a relu-sum score means a deep row carries long runs of exact +0.0 and a tie-blind kernel is green everywhere else and silently wrong there), boundary-straddling duplicates, and the `total_cmp` domain (signed zeros / subnormals / negatives / NaN) — green on BOTH card classes. Live at-depth audit **rows=1,549,452 mismatched=0 deepest_blocks=32,793**; decode-row-volume audit **120,000 decode-row selections, 0 mismatches**; greedy chain BYTE-IDENTICAL across the seam at a 100,000-token fill; all four rule gates green and identical to the prior battery to the digit. **No pairing requirement** (unlike the devtwin pair, which measured 0.906x when half-enabled): this seam wins alone on every measured surface, on top of the shipped `routerdev` + `idxcache` + `kvq` stack. | `set_kv_hoist` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvhoist` arms, `kvhoist=0` disables) | **OFF** (`KV_HOIST_DEFAULT` false at introduction 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp HOISTED K BLOCK SCALE in the quantized block-list attention (memory lever lane, 262k). Selects `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5_hoist` over `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5`. It is the fix for the kvq depth penalty recorded in the `set_kv_quant` row above, and that penalty turns out to be a READ-PATTERN ARTIFACT rather than a tax: `q4e_deq_q8` recomputes the block pointer from the element index, so the score loop re-loads the SAME fp16 block scale ONCE PER ELEMENT, 32 times per 32-element block. sm_120 SASS, score-phase inner loop per 8 K elements — `sdpa_blocklist_f32` **37** instrs / 8 KV loads / no scale loads; `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` **120** / 8 / **8 redundant scale loads**; `..._hoist` **52** / 8 / **0** (one per 32-element block). Phase 1 is thread-per-position, so a warp's lanes sit on 32 different selected tokens `k_tok_bytes = 544 B` apart and every load instruction replays 32 ways into 32 distinct sectors — the transaction count follows the load INSTRUCTION count, not the byte count, which is why the quantized cache issued 2x the f32 twin's KV transactions while reading 3.76x fewer bytes. The hoist brings KV-cache loads to PARITY with f32 while keeping the 3.76x byte saving. **BIT-IDENTICAL, and the bar is bit-identity rather than a band** — same product, same `acc +=` order, phases 2 and 3 verbatim. **Default OFF by design at introduction** (new-flags law: unmeasured behaviour does not default ON); the flip rides its own interleaved A/B, which is valid SHALLOW because `qsa.sdpa` is depth-INVARIANT (the selection budget saturates at 512 blocks x 4 = ~2,052 rows from ~8k of fill, measured flat at 10.3 ms across 100k/131k/150k) — one confirmation rung at 262,144 is owed before any number is quoted at the target window. Gate: the `gate_kvq_kernels` arm compares the two kernels BIT-IDENTICAL over 18,432 values at REAL geometry (hd=256 = EIGHT 32-element blocks per head slice, nh=24, nkv=2 so the second KV head starts at element 256, max_count=2052) — a tiny head_dim would run the block loop ONCE and never take the per-block scale advance, i.e. the only thing the seam changes, which is the tiny-green/real-broken shape this lane has been bitten by twice. RECEIPTED DEAD END inside the same seam: the identical hoist in phase 3 (weighted V) was tried and REJECTED by the SASS — the base already lets the compiler merge `d` and `m` into one 32-bit load, and phase 3 is already coalesced (adjacent lanes take adjacent `e`, so a warp covers one 24-byte q5_1 block in ONE sector, where f32 needs 128 B for the same dims), so there was no transaction win to buy; `kvhoist` is therefore a ONE-VARIABLE seam and its phase-3 SASS loops are identical to the base kernel's. Rollback: the default, or `kvhoist=0`. Mid-run flippable (no layout latch). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-C0.md. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_PEER_KV_MAX_CAP` | **8192 rows** (`peer_kv_max_cap`, introduced 2026-09-02) | Row ceiling for a PEER-RESIDENT QSA KV state — `alloc_state_reserve` with a `kv_engine` on a different card, i.e. `qwen4exp_real_gate --ladder-kv-dev1`. Past the ceiling BOTH the engine (at state allocation) and the ladder CLI (at arg parse, so the operator does not pay a ~100 s checkpoint load to learn it) refuse by name with the mechanism and the route that works. **Why a ceiling and not a knob to tune (memra#53):** the block-list form is the ONLY read path for a quantized cache and it is a SCATTER reader — `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` phase 1 is thread-per-position, so a warp's 32 lanes sit on 32 different selected rows 544 B apart and every load instruction replays 32 ways into 32 distinct sectors (the `set_kv_hoist` row above measures that replay in SASS). On the local card the reading SM's L2 absorbs it: the selected rows are shared by all 24 query heads and by neighbouring query rows in the chunk, and `qsa.sdpa` is depth-INVARIANT. Peer memory is NOT cached in the reading card's L2, so each selected position becomes a PCIe round trip and the redundancy L2 used to hide becomes wire traffic. **Measured, same rung, same binary, one card apart** (`research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/yarn/box-ladder-kvdev1-partial.tsv` vs `box-ladder-smoke2-local.tsv`, rung 4,096, chunk 8,192): first-chunk `qsa.sdpa` **501,436.6 ms = 97.2%** of the prefill vs **1,255.4 ms = 8.2%** local (**399x**); prefill **519.1 s vs 18.7 s** (27.8x); decode **462.6 vs 25.7 ms/token** (18x), decode `qsa.sdpa` 430.5 ms = 95.1% vs 7.8 ms = 26.3%; effective P2P throughput ~220 MB/s. The 32,768 rung of that same run never completed, which is why the file is named `partial`. Scaling the 4,096 point by selected-row count puts a 262,144 prefill at **~9 hours** on this route. **This is what memra#53 was:** the 262k spec cell was scheduled on `--ladder-kv-dev1` anyway and ran twice without ever banking a rung row (45 min, then 113 min, killed by an operator both times), trunk card at **sm 100% / mem 0%** with the KV card's memory flat — warps resident and stalled on peer loads while the local framebuffer sat idle. It never deadlocked and no timeout wrapper would have found it; `YARN-CELL.md` had already ruled the route out on these receipts, but the ruling lived in a lane doc instead of the code, so this row and the two refusals are that verdict made enforceable. **The placement was also backwards on its own terms:** the QSA KV is the SMALL allocation at 10,368 B/row across the 12 QSA layers (q8_0 K 544 B + q5_1 V 320 B at kv_width 512), i.e. **2.7 GiB at a 262,144 capacity**; what does not fit beside ~90 GiB of trunk weights is the **~17.6 GiB MTP draft state**, and `--mtp-dev1` / `load_from_dir_dev1` already place that on card 1. **Default 8,192 by design:** it keeps the shallow smoke arm that motivated the flag legal (the 4,096 rung above completes, slowly) and refuses the depths where the route cannot arrive; raise it only to deliberately re-measure the cliff. Changes no arithmetic — a refusal cannot move bits, and the rig tiny fixture gate is byte-identical across the change (sha line only). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/kvdev1-hang/HANG.md. |
| `set_pool_t` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=poolT` arms, `poolT=0` disables) | **OFF** (`POOL_T_DEFAULT` false at introduction 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp DIM-MAJOR POOLED-KEY device plane (memory lever lane, 262k). Selects `qsa_index_score_f32_t` over `qsa_index_score_f32` and reads a transposed mirror of the pooled cache. It targets the SECOND depth-scaling term of the deep decode profile: with `idxsel` armed, `qsa.idx_host` measures 2.5 ms at 100,000 / 3.0 at 131,072 / 3.2 at 150,000 — linear in context, extrapolating to ~5.7 ms at the 262,144 target, behind only `ple.host_ngram_gather` among the terms that grow. The score kernel is thread-per-block, so lane L reads `pooled[(block0+L)*head_dim + d]`: lanes are `head_dim*4 = 512 B` apart, one warp's `k[d]` load touches 32 DISTINCT sectors and moves 1,024 B to use 128 B — **8x sector amplification on the one array whose size IS the context** (33.5 MB per scored row per layer at the target geometry). Dim-major makes the same 32 lanes read 32 consecutive floats: 128 B, 4 sectors, zero waste, for about one extra address op per element (46 vs 37 instrs per 8 elements). **BIT-IDENTICAL** — identical loop order and the same explicit `__fmul_rn`/`__fadd_rn`/`__fdiv_rn` the kernel's existing 1-ULP comment requires (default `-fmad=true` contraction here once flipped near-tie top-k picks), only the address changes. BOTH LAYOUTS ARE MAINTAINED UNCONDITIONALLY on append and the seam selects only the kernel, for two reasons and the second is the load-bearing one: (a) the append cost is then identical in both A/B arms, so the measurement isolates exactly the read pattern; (b) a seam that is flippable between timed rounds plus a layout maintained only while armed means an arm that sat OFF leaves the plane missing every row appended meanwhile, and a stale pooled plane scores stale keys — which reads as plausible output rather than as a failure. `pooled_dev_rows` is therefore the single truth for BOTH layouts, so a flip needs no rebuild and can leave nothing behind. Instrument cost, stated: one extra pooled plane (33.5 MB at the target geometry, 1.6% of the ~2 GB free there) plus a transpose over the delta (512 rows per 2,048-token prefill chunk, 0-1 rows per decode step); the losing layout goes away in the commit that records the verdict — carrying both is an A/B instrument, not a shipping design. Mirror growth no longer copies the old prefix forward: a capacity change changes the plane's PITCH, so every dim would land elsewhere and the plane would be half-addressed; it re-mirrors from the host cache instead, one H2D per capacity DOUBLING. **Default OFF by design at introduction** (new-flags law). Gate: the `gate_qsa_index_score` arm runs transpose + transposed kernel against THE SAME HOST TWIN (not device-to-device, which would let a shared mistake pass twice) and includes the pitch trap on purpose — the plane's pitch is the mirror's block CAPACITY (a power of two) while `n_blocks` is whatever the fill is, so `cap_rows == n_blocks` is the ABNORMAL state and a kernel handed `n_blocks` as its pitch reads dim d of block b as dim d of a DIFFERENT block for every d > 0 (silent wrong values, green in any gate where the two numbers coincide); the arm uses cap_rows=4096 with n_blocks=1031, plus an aligned case. Rollback: the default, or `poolT=0`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-C0.md. |
